# Comrade — Repository Audit & Improvement Plan

_Audit date: 2026-07-10._
_Method: full manual read of every Rust crate, the CLI, the Tauri shell + desktop JS, and the Android app, cross-checked by an 8-dimension parallel agent sweep. Architecture findings were additionally adversarially verified (all CONFIRMED); every other finding below was verified by direct file reads unless explicitly flagged otherwise._

---

## 1. Executive Summary

**Overall health: C+ (prototype grade: promising core, credibility gaps at the edges).**
The Rust workspace shows genuinely strong engineering discipline for a prototype — clean crate layering, typed errors everywhere, ~90 behavior-asserting unit tests, an encrypted-at-rest store with adversarial "scan the disk for plaintext" tests, and CI that gates fmt/clippy/test. The problem is the distance between what the README promises and what is actually wired: three of the seven advertised engines (Saathi mesh, NIP-65 gossip routing, encrypted media) are never invoked by any frontend; the Android app cannot unlock the vault at all, so its voice "post"/"timeline" commands can never succeed; and DMs use the deprecated, unauthenticated NIP-04 scheme in a product whose entire identity is "privacy-first."

**Top 3 risks:** (1) NIP-04 (AES-CBC, no authentication, metadata-leaking) for E2E DMs while NIP-44 is available in the already-pinned nostr-sdk; (2) Off-Grid mode is a *false privacy assurance* — the UI tells the user "public relays paused, Saathi mesh active" while relay websockets stay connected and no engine is ever disconnected; (3) `change_pin` re-encrypts the store non-atomically — a crash mid-way permanently corrupts user data.

**Top 3 opportunities:** (1) close the honesty gap — wire engine lifecycle to the state machine (or mark features experimental) and make the README match reality; (2) close the CI and release blind spots — Android tests and the Tauri crate are never built in CI, and official release APKs ship debug-signed with ephemeral keys and without the voice model; (3) scope the Sabha feed subscription (currently the global Kind-1 firehose) to a follow set, which fixes performance, UX, and privacy in one change.

---

## Decision log

- **2026-08-31 (a playlist you can arrange, and rename, from the Together library):** Owner ask: bring the Together music section up to an Echo/BlackHole-class player. This is the first slice — the one `docs/TOGETHER.md` §20 "What is still missing" named outright ("reordering within a playlist did not [ship]") plus the rename that pairs with it. **The reorder rule lives in core and is mirrored, not re-invented:** `comrade_ui::reorder_tracks` is a pure `Vec` move — the row at `from` lands *at* `to`, both indices **clamp** into range rather than erroring (a drag past the last row is a drag to the end, not a bug), and `from == to` / one-item / empty are no-ops — and `TogetherDecisions.reorderedOrder` is its byte-for-byte Kotlin twin returning the index permutation, so the row a finger moves and the row the vault writes cannot disagree. `ComradeRuntime::playlist_reorder`/`playlist_rename` and their `Comrade` uniffi wrappers apply it; both leave `created_at_ms` untouched, so arranging or renaming a list never jumps it up the shelf — the same principle `set_journal_entry_title` already holds to. `playlist_rename` refuses a blank name exactly as `playlist_create` does. **No `BridgeEvent` variant, no new `UiError` case**, so neither exhaustive match moved and no frb regen is owed; `app/` still has no Together surface, so the frb mirror stays as it was. **Android UI:** the open-playlist view in `TogetherScreen.PlaylistsScreen` gains a rename field (placeholder carries the current name, so an empty field reads as "unchanged") and per-row Move up / Move down buttons. Up/down rather than a drag handle on purpose — a drag reorder in a plain scrolling `Column` is fiddly and unverifiable without a device, and the arithmetic is the shared part anyway; a drag affordance is a follow-up. No early-return was added to that composable (it already carries pre-existing `return@Column`s, tracked in the 2026-08-15 entry and left for their own fix). **Verified:** `cargo fmt --check`; `clippy --workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings`; `cargo test -p comrade_ui -p comrade_jni --lib` green — 267 ui (2 new: the `reorder_tracks` table and a reorder+rename round-trip asserting the shelf order holds; the locked-vault test gained both new methods), 25 jni; 131 `TogetherDecisionsTest` JVM tests under `kotlinc` (1 new, `aDragReorderMovesOneRowAndClampsTheRest`, pinned to the same vectors as the Rust table); the generated uniffi bindings carry `playlistReorder(id, UInt, UInt)` / `playlistRename(id, String)` exactly as `ComradeCore.kt` calls them; and the Compose typecheck lane resolves all 161 sources clean against real Compose 1.6.1 / Material3 1.2.0 (`TogetherScreen.kt` and the four new `strings.xml` ids included, `R` generated from the real resource files) — using the two local shims the 2026-08-24 entry already documents (`.so`→`.dylib` symlink; the `java.lang.ref.Cleaner` fallback in the generated file routed to its JNA twin, runtime-safe behind the existing `Class.forName` guard). **Not verified, CI-first:** `./gradlew test`, the Compose compiler plugin's own rules, and anything about how the screen looks or behaves on a device.

- **2026-08-24 (the Together tab streams from a server you own, and the player finally keeps what you play):** Owner request: "a full-fledged music player in together mode which should support online streaming like blackhole, echo and antra, with ability to maintain sync." §23 of `docs/TOGETHER.md` is the design record; the short form is that **the sync already existed and was not touched** — this session filled the two gaps that doc itself named: an online-streaming source behind §20's pluggable tier, and the player library ("playlists, favourites, queue and history — nothing at all"). **The streaming source is Subsonic/Navidrome**, the one shape of online source this app can carry without touching `catalogue.rs`'s DRM line: the user's own server, searched via `search3`, streamed as plain progressive HTTPS. New `comrade_core::subsonic` builds every candidate URL through `together::valid_stream_url` *inside core*, so a row on screen is by construction a row a Together session accepts — and because such sessions ride `TogetherContent::Stream`, they inherit the fine sync ladder (accurate positions, rate-trimmable), i.e. they sync as tightly as a local file, which beats anything a vendor SDK could offer and needs no new protocol. Auth is Subsonic's salted token (`md5(password+salt)`), MD5 implemented in ~90 inline lines pinned to the RFC 1321 suite plus boundary cross-checks rather than added as a dependency — the same call `urlencode` made; token (never password) matters because **the URL travels to the peer**, and the source card says so. **Jamendo is the second catalogue**, giving `choose_audio_plan`'s OpenLicence tier something true to check: CC-licensed tracks with direct files, working download buttons through §21's existing gate with zero new policy, and `audiodownload_allowed=false` rows carrying a stream URL while failing `is_openly_fetchable`. That forced §20's predicted move: `CatalogueMatch` now carries `source`, TogetherScreen's `MUSICBRAINZ` constant is deleted, and every row names its catalogue. **The player library is four vault trees and fifteen uniffi methods**: favourites (toggle answers what it now is), history (one entry per track, newest-wins, capped at 100 — recently played, not a diary of plays), playlists (removing by key takes out every copy, because the key is the identity), queue snapshots saved on pause/leave (`saveQueueSnapshot`). All vault-backed and therefore all answering `VaultLocked` while locked, defended once more than usual: listening data is diary data, and an empty library would have lied about why it was empty. Android gained: a fifth and sixth source cards ("Your server"; "Public collections" - Internet Archive search/item-listing plus any podcast feed URL, both keyless, both guarded in core via `public_sources.rs`, fixture-tested), a Settings -> Music sources card ("Your server") with its own search screen whose outcome type is deliberately flat (`StreamSearchOutcome`, not a new `UiError` variant — that enum is frb-mirrored and adding a case breaks four Flutter lanes for no informational gain over a typed value), a Settings → Music sources card (server/user/password/Jamendo key, stored as one JSON blob in SharedPreferences so a config can never half-exist), history recording + queue snapshots wired into `TogetherManager` (fire-and-forget; persistence must never cost playback), and `Chosen.ServerTrack` built typed rather than through the paste parser — a token-auth `/rest/stream` URL names no media extension, which is a check for pasted text, not for a value core already guarded. **What was deliberately not done:** no DRM-circumvention adapter (§20's decision stands); no restore path for the saved queue yet (it saves; nothing offers "continue" — rebuilding session UI from a snapshot wants its own pass); no favourites/playlists/history screens yet (storage+runtime+FFI shipped and tested, surfaces are follow-ups); no desktop or Flutter reach (Android-first standing directive; Flutter also needs frb codegen, which cannot run where Dart cannot build). No `BridgeEvent` variants were added, so neither exhaustive match moved and no bridge regen was owed. **Verified:** `cargo fmt --check`; `clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`; `cargo test` green in both feature configurations — 735 comrade_core (13 new subsonic, incl. the RFC vectors and a test pinning each refused server shape against the peer guard itself), 266 comrade_ui lib (7 new: favourite toggle, history dedup/cap/order/clear, playlist round-trip, queue save/load/clear, vault-locked refusals), comrade_jni 25; the 119 `TogetherDecisionsTest` JVM tests (2 updated for the fifth source card and its ordering, with the catalogue-before-server property pinned); **both** Android typecheck lanes green locally on macOS (plain lane 111 sources; Compose lane 157 sources, zero resolution errors) using local script variants for two portability gaps now known: the scripts hardcode `libcomrade_jni.so` (dylib here) and uniffi's generated Kotlin references `java.lang.ref.Cleaner`, which android.jar lacks — resolved in the local variant only by rewriting that generated fallback onto its JNA twin (runtime-safe: it sits behind a `Class.forName` guard), never by weakening the app code. **Not verified, and recorded rather than implied:** everything at runtime — no device has drawn the server screen, hit a real Navidrome, or played a stream; the search3/stream fixtures are hand-built to the documented API shape; and the frb mirror is untouched but unregenerated, **Second batch, same day (the BlackHole-shaped control set):** shuffle
(Fisher-Yates keeping the current track first), repeat OFF/ALL/ONE with the
subtle rule pinned by test — repeat-one governs what happens when a track ends,
but never traps a manual skip — solo-only auto-advance and solo-only playback
speed (both because a together session's playhead belongs to the correction
ladder, not to one device's whim; `speedAllowed`/`manualNextIndex` are pure and
tested), a sleep timer that pauses via `setState` so both devices stop, an
equalizer attached per audio session and only where our own decoder mixes
(never embeds or followed apps; millibels stored, no conversions), synced
lyrics from LRCLIB parsed twice (Rust `parse_lrc_document` + Kotlin
`TogetherDecisions.parseLrc`) with shared fixtures, a resume card over the
saved queue (solo-only, resolvable-rows-only), favourites/recent/playlists
surfaces over the vault lists, and two more keyless sources: Internet Archive
(advancedsearch + metadata APIs, derivative-MP3-first) and podcast feeds
(tolerant RSS reading of three facts per episode). **CI needed no new lanes**:
the existing `catalogue-http` job is exactly where the new feature-gated tests
(subsonic lookup path, Jamendo parse, public sources) run, workspace covers the
pure half, gradle covers the JVM tests. **Verified:** 130/130 TogetherDecisions
JVM tests (11 new), 736 core + 265 ui + 25 jni cargo tests green, fmt/clippy
clean, plain typecheck lane 113 files clean, Compose lane zero resolution
errors. **Not verified:** runtime behaviour again (EQ on real vendor effects,
LRCLIB live answers, Archive API shapes beyond fixtures).

so the first `flutter_rust_bridge_codegen generate` must be run before any Dart consumer expects these functions.

- **2026-08-21 (TRAVEL-1, closed — the emulator finally opens the tab):** `MainActivityUiTest` now grants `ACCESS_COARSE_LOCATION` up front and adds a Travel leg: open the tab, wait for a real terminal state (`Ready`'s `travel_guide` tag, or `NoFix`/`Failed`'s "Try again"), then take one more action — the same "screen survived the recomposition" proof the Tasks and Ride regression legs already give, and the exact gap TRAVEL-1 named: `TravelScreen`'s `LaunchedEffect` flips `loading` then `guide`/`error` a few seconds after the first frame, which is the shape that killed `TaskListScreen` and `RideScreen` on lanes that only see the first frame. **Not verified**: no Android SDK in this sandbox, and `src/androidTest` isn't covered by `.claude/scripts/android-typecheck*.sh` either (main-source-only) — CI's emulator lane is the first place this actually runs.

- **2026-08-16 (a tab for where you are standing, and the honest half of it is the half that needs no key):** Owner asked for a Travel tab listing the legendary local restaurants — the ones reviewed by thousands on Google Maps — plus things to do and facts about the place, from the user's location. Built as `comrade_core::travel` (a new engine, 40 tests), `comrade_ui`'s `travel_guide`, a uniffi surface, and an Android tab. **Four decisions carry it, and each rejected an obvious alternative.** (1) **"Legendary" is a formula, not a star average.** Sorting on `rating` puts a 5.0-from-four-friends above a 4.6-from-nine-thousand, which is the opposite of what was asked for; `legend_score` is a Bayesian rating (250 imaginary reviews at 3.9) times a logarithmic crowd weight that reaches 1.0 at 1 000 reviews and is **capped at 1.4** — the cap is what stops an airport food court with 400 000 reviews outranking a genuinely loved place with 8 000, and it has its own test. The *badge* (`is_legendary`) is a separate, stricter question, because a list still has to be ordered when nothing in it is a legend. All of it is in core, so three frontends cannot disagree about what a legend is. (2) **The coordinate that leaves the device is not the coordinate of the phone.** Every outbound query is built from the centre of the ~150 m geohash cell the fix falls in (`geo::Precision::Block`), and distances on screen are measured against the real fix locally — so the screen stays accurate while a third party learns "somewhere in this block". `Neighborhood` (~1.2 km) was the first instinct and is wrong: at that size the blur is comparable to the search radius and the answer stops being about where the user is. Android therefore asks for `ACCESS_COARSE_LOCATION` only — fine location would be asking for precision the app immediately discards — and uses the platform `LocationManager` rather than Play Services, which also sidesteps the `app/`-compiles-`android/`'s-Kotlin dependency trap. The guide cache is keyed by cell and is **in memory only**, cleared on `lock_vault`: the places are public, the cells are a record of where somebody has been. (3) **There is no API key in this repository and there will not be one.** Google Places is the only public source with review counts in the thousands and it bills per request; a key in an app binary is a key that gets scraped and billed to whoever owns it. So the key is the user's, lives in the encrypted vault (not a preference file, because it is billable), is write-only from the UI, and `ApiKey` has a hand-written redacting `Debug` so it cannot reach a crash report through a `{:?}`. (4) **A missing key is a notice, not a failure** — the one place "fail fast on missing config" is applied as *loud* rather than *fatal*. Failing the whole guide because the paid half is unconfigured would throw away the working two thirds (OpenStreetMap places, Wikipedia facts, both keyless); instead the guide returns with `ratings_from: None` and a sentence. What must never happen is those rendering identically, so "no ratings configured" and "nothing legendary nearby" are kept apart by `ratings_from` + `notice` and by exactly one function on each side (`TravelDecisions.ratingsExplainer`). **Two smaller things worth recording.** `travel_guide` is a **free function**, not a method: the caller clones a cache handle and reads the key under a short read lock and then calls it with **no lock held**, because up to five HTTP round trips inside a held `RwLock` is the shape of the two deadlocks this repo has already fixed. And unlike `catalogue.rs`, the **response parsers compile unconditionally** — only the socket is behind `travel-http` — because the response format is what breaks when an upstream changes, and a fixture test that only runs under a feature nobody enables is a test that does not run. **Verified:** 40 core tests in both feature configurations, 7 runtime tests, 23 `TravelDecisions` tests on a plain JVM `kotlinc`, `cargo fmt --check`, `clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings` on a freshly-updated stable (1.97.1), the full `cargo test --workspace`, the new `travel-http` clippy+test lanes, **both** Android typecheck lanes (145 sources against real Compose 1.6.1/Material3 1.2.0, zero resolution errors), 459 desktop node tests, and the whole Flutter lane — `analyze --fatal-infos`, 453 tests, `dart format`, the frb bridge regenerated twice with the pinned 2.12.0 codegen (byte-identical the second time) and the round-trip test run against a real `libcomrade_jni` rather than skipped. The bridge regeneration was **owed** here and the non-Compose typecheck is what caught the half of it that is easy to miss: two new `UiError` variants mean the frb mirror in `api.rs`, the Kotlin `UiException` `when` in `ComradeCore.kt`, and the Dart `describeUiError` switch all fail to compile until they are updated — three lanes, none of them in `ci.yml`. **Also worth stating because it is the closest thing to a live test this feature has:** running the `travel-http` lane in this sandbox made a real Wikipedia request and returned six correctly-ranked, distance-ordered facts about Colaba (Gateway of India at 5 m, Bademiya at 15 m), which is the query builder, the parser, `first_sentences`, `rank_facts` and the DTO all working end to end against the real API. The two tests that discovered this by *failing* are now `#[cfg(not(feature = "travel-http"))]` — a CI lane must not hammer two free public services on every push. **Not verified, and recorded rather than implied:** how the screen looks or whether it survives a recomposition (AUDIT TRAVEL-1 — the emulator is the only lane that answers that, and this change does not add a case), and every real Overpass and Google response, since every parser test is a fixture. **Android only, deliberately (`CLAUDE.md` §11):** `desktop/` cannot reach a third-party host at all under the Tauri shell's CSP without a `src-tauri` change this sandbox cannot compile, and `app/` has no Travel screen — the FFI surface is uniffi-only. Both are parity debt, named in `docs/TRAVEL.md` §9, not a closed decision.

- **2026-08-16 (a ride signal takes both roads, and the faster one alone would have been the wrong answer):** Owner asked for the mesh follow-up `docs/RIDE.md` §9 named as the most valuable one, and it is: two people on one motorcycle are a metre apart, and the mobile data they are riding through is the worst link either of them has — the strongest case for the local radios anywhere in this app. **The receive half needed nothing at all**, which is worth recording because it is the payoff of an earlier decision: a sealed frame off the radios is opened by `SealedIngress::accept` and fed into *the same* `dispatch_incoming_dm` a relay DM goes through, so the ride arm's acceptance gate, age gate and dedup already applied to mesh-delivered signals from the day they were written. Only sending changed. **The interesting decision is that it publishes to the relay too, every time.** `send_together` returns as soon as a local radio accepts a frame; copying that here would have been wrong, for a reason `LocalRadios::send` states in its own doc — the boolean means a radio *took* it, never that it arrived. A together session repairs a lost signal on its next heartbeat; a ride signal has no heartbeat, no Lamport counter to notice a gap, and deliberately no outbox, so a frame a radio swallowed is a "pull over" that silently never happened. The cost is one gift wrap for a feature that emits a handful of signals across a whole ride. **That buys two hazards, and the second one is the one worth reading.** (1) The two copies carry different wrapper event ids — they are different deliveries — so `handle_ride`'s event-id set cannot pair them, and without help one tap buzzes a driver twice; the ride arm now runs the dispatcher's `is_cross_transport_duplicate`, which keys on content. (2) But content dedup runs for **120 seconds**, and the catalog is fixed — so `pull_over` tapped again produces byte-identical JSON, and on a motorcycle a phrase repeated a few seconds later because the first one did not take is the *likely* case, not a double-send. Naive content dedup would have eaten exactly the message a rider most needs to repeat. `RideEnvelope::at_ms` is what separates them: one send is byte-identical wherever it goes, two sends never are. It is an identity, not a deadline — freshness still comes from the carrying DM's `created_at`, as a nudge's does — and it rides inside the E2E envelope, so no relay learns it. Both halves are asserted by one test (`one_ride_signal_on_two_transports_raises_one_card_but_a_repeat_still_raises_its_own`), because either alone passes against the build that gets the other wrong. **Verified:** `cargo fmt --check`, `clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`, the full `cargo test --workspace` (20 binaries), and a frb codegen run confirming the bridge is untouched — `RideEnvelope` is internal to Rust and never crosses the FFI, so no frontend changed and no regeneration was owed. **Still not built:** starting the *WiFi* mesh for a ride, which remains engine-lifecycle work (AUDIT A1 / ADR-4). Outside the off-grid workspace the local rung is Bluetooth — which, for two seats of one motorcycle, is the right radio anyway.

- **2026-08-15 (the last early return in the Android source, and a safety net that turned out not to be broken):** Follow-up to the Ride crash below. Two things were flagged while fixing it; **one was real and one was my misreading**, and both are recorded because the misreading is the more useful of the two. **The real one:** `LibraryBrowser` in `ui/TogetherScreen.kt` still carried an early `return@Column`, on `libraryGranted` — which flips the moment someone grants the media permission, so the Column emitted a different number of composable groups either side of a *live* state change. That is the same shape that killed `TaskListScreen` (2026-08-04) and `RideScreen` (2026-08-15), and it was the last one left in the Android source; it is an `if/else` now, with a comment naming the failure mode and pointing at `.claude/rules/android.md`. Nobody has reported this screen crashing, which is consistent rather than reassuring: reaching that branch needs the media permission *refused*, so most installs never compose it, and the two that did crash were both on paths people use daily. **The misreading, stated plainly because acting on it would have removed a guard:** the compiler warns `RelayConnectionService.kt:629` that a `when` is exhaustive so its `else` is redundant, and I first read that as the `BridgeEvent` match having an `else` — which would have meant a new bridge variant no longer failing the Android build, defeating what the `TaskCompleted` hook assumes. It is not. The `BridgeEvent` `when` in `route()` has **no** `else` and is genuinely exhaustive; the warning is about `contentKind = when (content)` over `TogetherContent`, where the `else` is deliberate, documented in the comment directly above it, and load-bearing: a variant this build has not learned reaches `PlaybackModeDecision.ownershipFor` as an unrecognised kind and is refused (it returns null), rather than failing to compile a lane `ci.yml` does not run. **Removing that `else` to silence the warning would convert a graceful degradation into a build break**, so it stays and the warning is accepted noise. **Verified:** `android-typecheck-compose.sh` — all 138 sources including `androidTest` against real Compose 1.6.1 / Material3 1.2.0, zero resolution errors. **Not verified, and worth saying:** the restructured branch has no device-test cover, because reaching it needs a *denied* media permission and the emulator lane grants what it needs; the fix is structural and typechecked, not exercised.

- **2026-08-15 (Ride crashed on open, and the defect was one this ledger already named):** Reported from a handset — *"the app is closing when I click on riding together"*. `RideScreen` had three early returns (`RideScreen`'s own `return@Column`, and one each in `GlanceCard` and `MusicControls`), so the Column emitted a **different number of composable groups before and after its state flipped** — which throws on the *recomposition*, not the first frame. That is why the screen drew and then took the process a moment later: the comrade list loads asynchronously and the glance card's clock ticks once a second, so the fatal recomposition was never more than a second away. **This is verbatim the 2026-08-04 `TaskListScreen` defect**, whose entry notes its two early returns were *"the only two early `return@Column`s in the Android source"* and replaced them with a single `when`. This screen added three more, four months of institutional memory later, which is worth recording as a process fact and not just a bug: the rule lived in a decision-log entry and in one screen's comment, where nothing made it reachable from the screen being written. All three are `if/else` now, each carrying a comment naming the failure mode. **`ui/TogetherScreen.kt:1043` still has one** — out of scope here, recorded rather than fixed silently. **Why every lane was green while the app died on open:** the JVM lane cannot see a composition, and the APK lane proves only that it compiles; `RideDecisions` had 14 passing tests the whole time, because the decisions were never wrong — the composition around them was. The regression cover is therefore on the emulator, where the Tasks one already is: `MainActivityUiTest` taps `drawer-ride`, waits *past a clock tick*, picks a seat, and waits past another, because the assertion that matters is not "the screen drew" but "it survived the recomposition" — a bare existence check passes against the broken build. **Also, and separately, the mode was renamed**: the drawer said *Riding together*, a sentence among nouns; it is **Ride**, with `MotorcycleIcon` drawn to a Himalayan 411's silhouette (two equal wheels, high flat bar, the tall screen over the front wheel) rather than Material's scooter glyph, inlined in `AppIcons.kt` like every other icon here. The screen also stopped repeating the app bar's title under it. **Verified this round, and the boundary moved:** `dl.google.com` is reachable from the sandbox again, so **both Android typecheck lanes ran for the first time on this feature** — `android-typecheck-compose.sh` compiles all 138 sources, now including `androidTest`, against real Compose 1.6.1 / Material3 1.2.0 with **zero resolution errors**. Plus the 14 `RideDecisions` JVM tests. What it still cannot answer is whether the fix *works* — that is the emulator lane, and it is the one this entry is really about.

- **2026-08-14 (a mode for two people on one motorcycle, built as a vocabulary rather than a session):** Owner request: the right mode for a driver and a pillion — music, a way to talk over engine noise, and route suggestions. Built as `comrade_core::ride`, an eighth DM control envelope carrying either a catalog phrase (`slow_down`, `pull_over`, `fuel_soon`, `break_please`, `all_good`) or a route suggestion (maneuver + optional distance + optional 80-char landmark note). **Three decisions are the substance, and each rejected an obvious alternative.** (1) **One-shot, not a session.** Together is a session because it defends shared state — one playhead — against feedback; a ride signal defends nothing, is acted on once, and is false a minute later. `comrade_core::nudge` is the shape that fits, and copying `together`'s session machinery would have bought Lamport arbitration nobody needs at the cost of a state machine to establish and tear down. (2) **No coordinates, ever.** The obvious build of "navigation" shares a position; this shares *what the pillion chose to tap*. A position is the most sensitive thing either phone holds, nobody decided to send it, and it is not needed to say "left at the next junction" — `docs/TOGETHER.md` §2's filename argument in another shape. There is no dependency on `comrade_core::geo` and no permission to ask for, and the exact JSON key set is asserted by a test so adding a field fails the build. (3) **Urgency is derived in core, not per frontend**, so two phones cannot disagree about whether "pull over" is worth a buzz — ADR-2's rule applied to haptics. Derived on arrival rather than carried, so a sender cannot declare its own message an emergency. **The music half needed no wire protocol at all**: a Together session already holds two playheads in step, and what ride mode adds is the decision that a driver gets play/pause and next while a scrubber and previous are refused — a drag target under a thumb at speed is a swerve, and a mis-tap on previous restarts the track on *both* phones. Replay safety is the nudge's story, not the session's: an acceptance gate, a 60 s age gate from send time with the peer TTL clamped, and event-id dedup; nothing is persisted, and the screen enforces the same 60 s a second time by removing a stale card rather than dimming it (stale here is not old news, it is false). **One `BridgeEvent` variant for the whole vocabulary**, so adding a phrase touches core and two decision tables and no bridge. **Verified**: 9 core tests, the runtime's stranger-gate and stale-signal cases, a two-peer exchange over a real in-process relay, both FFI bridges with frb regenerated, the full Flutter lane green locally (analyze/test/format), and 14 JVM tests for `RideDecisions`. **Not verified, and recorded rather than implied**: every Android file that touches the framework — `RideScreen.kt`, `RideSignals.kt`, the `RelayConnectionService` arm, the `ComradeCore` wrappers, the `MainActivity` drawer entry. `.claude/scripts/android-typecheck.sh` fetches androidx from `dl.google.com`, which this container's network policy refused with a 403 at the proxy, and Maven Central does not mirror androidx — so **both Android typecheck lanes were unavailable this session** and CI is the first compiler those files meet. New gap worth naming rather than fixing silently: **speech and haptics stop when the ride screen closes**, because `RideSignals` arms them on `setActive` — a ride with the phone in a pocket delivers nothing audible, and closing that is a foreground service, not a flag (`docs/RIDE.md` §9).

- **2026-08-14 (a conversation had no way to keep a subject together, and the obvious fix would have split every topic in half):** `comrade_core::topic` — threads and topics inside a two-person chat, Telegram's forum topics and Slack's threads, with a thread sheet on all three frontends and `/assign #topic` in the composer. **The load-bearing decision is that the slug *is* the id.** The first design gave each topic a random 128-bit id like `karya::Task`, and it is wrong here for a reason that only shows up in the field: both people can type `/assign #deposit` in the same conversation before either envelope lands, and two ids for one word means two topics with the same name, filing into different rows, with *neither side able to see the split*. Keying on the slugified name makes creation idempotent by construction — no merge, no tie-break, no Lamport clock (`crates/comrade_core/src/topic.rs`, `EncryptedStore::save_topic` deliberately has no timestamp guard because the caller has already merged). What that costs is stated in the module header rather than left to be discovered: **a topic cannot be renamed into a different word**; `#Deposit`/`#deposit` are one topic that remembers the first spelling (`Topic::merge_name`), but `#deposit` → `#flat` is a new topic. Same trade `karya` makes by refusing to reopen a task — a terminal rule is what lets two devices not need to arbitrate. **Filing emits no chat bubble**, which is the one place this departs from `karya`, and the reason is a product one: a task is something a person is being *asked*, while a topic is where a message already in front of them is *filed*, so a conversation that grew a "moved to #deposit" line every time somebody tidied would punish tidying. `BridgeEvent::TopicsChanged` carries the conversation and nothing else and the sheets re-read; the counts are derived on read (`ThreadIndex`) rather than stored, because a stored count drifts the first time a backfill inserts an old message mid-thread. **Two limitations are recorded rather than hidden**, TOPIC-1 (slugs are Latin ASCII, in an app that ships Hindi strings) and TOPIC-2 (an attachment can start a thread but not join one), both with exit conditions. The shared decisions — ordering, which rows are hidden, the preview branch, and `/assign`'s three-way answer — live in three mirrored copies (`TopicDecisions.kt`, `topics.mjs`, `topic_view.dart`) pinned by 11 identical vectors each, and **none of them re-derives a slug or walks a reply chain**: those are core's, which is the `/pay`-drifted-across-four-frontends lesson applied before the fact rather than after. The preview *wording* is deliberately not in core — an uncaptioned attachment and a root that is not on this device both arrive as an empty string plus a boolean saying which, so the sentence stays where a translator can reach it. Verified: 18 core, 6 storage, 14 runtime, 11 Kotlin (on a plain JVM), 14 desktop node, 20 Dart; `flutter analyze --fatal-infos`, `dart format`, `cargo clippy --workspace -D warnings`, and the frb bridge regenerated with the pinned 2.12.0 codegen and the round-trip test run against a real `libcomrade_jni` rather than skipped. **Not verified here:** anything Compose, and any Kotlin importing Android — `.claude/scripts/android-typecheck.sh` cannot run in this container because the environment's network policy blocks `dl.google.com`, so the androidx jars it needs cannot be fetched. That is a container limitation, not a script one, and CI is the first build for those files.

- **2026-08-14 (the desktop thread had the reply-jump feature and none of it ran, and reading history was interrupted by events that added nothing):** Two defects in the same screen, both invisible to every lane that exists. **(1) The quote jump was dead on arrival.** `goToQuoted` opened with `$("#dm-log")` and the page declares no such id — the thread is `#chat-log`, and the `dm-` prefix belongs to the twelve composer controls *below* it (`index.html:288-325`), which is what makes the slip so easy to read past. `$` returned null, the `if (!log) return` under it swallowed that, and so the quote button rendered, carried a title and an `aria-label`, stopped propagation on click, and did nothing — the failure mode of a wrong selector is a feature that looks finished. The shared rule it was built on (`chat_thread.mjs:quoteScrollTargetId`) was correct, tested, and mirrored in all three frontends the whole time; only the one line that reaches the DOM was wrong, which is precisely the half `node --test` cannot see. **(2) `renderConversation` ended in an unconditional `log.scrollTop = log.scrollHeight`.** Android and Flutter have carried `isNearBottom` since they were written *because* a reader scrolled up in history must not be yanked to the newest line; desktop never got it. The consequence was worse than missing the new-mail case, because that function is not only called for new mail — a delivery tick (`main.js:2009`), a peer rename (`main.js:2018`) and a presence change all rebuild the thread, so an event that added nothing to read still threw you to the bottom. It also destroyed the quote jump's own landing the moment any of those arrived, so the two bugs would have masked each other had the first one worked. **The fix keeps the decision where the other two frontends keep it:** `chat_thread.mjs` gains `isNearBottom({scrollTop, scrollHeight, clientHeight})`, the DOM spelling of Flutter's `isNearBottomByOffset` (travel is `scrollHeight - clientHeight`, same 220px slack, same "nothing to scroll counts as bottom"), and `renderConversation` measures before it wipes and restores after. Switching peers always lands at the bottom — `#chat-log` is one element reused by every conversation, so its offset outlives the thread that produced it and a naive restore would open the new peer at the old peer's scroll position. **The regression test is the part worth keeping.** A dead `$("#id")` is not a visible failure and `main.js` is not unit-testable, so `dom_bindings.test.mjs` checks the binding statically instead: every id `main.js` looks up must be declared in `index.html` or created at runtime by `main.js` itself, with the runtime allowance (`media-lightbox`) re-verified against the code that creates it so it cannot rot into cover for a real one. It fails on the pre-fix tree and catches the whole class, not the one instance. **Still open, and deliberately not fixed here:** desktop remains the only frontend with no day separators and no unread divider — `startsNewDay`, `firstUnreadIndex` and `startsUnread` exist in `ChatThread.kt` and `chat_thread.dart` with tests, and have no desktop counterpart. That is a parity gap in a shipping frontend, not a bug in what shipped, and it wants its own change.

- **2026-08-14 (the journal got a door, and it opens one note at a time):** Task:
  share and render journal notes in chats. The journal is the most private thing
  in the product — sealed at rest, never synced, unreachable from a linked
  browser at any scope (`link.rs:340`) — and the missing half of why people write
  things down is being able to say *this is what I meant* to one person. Today
  that meant retyping the entry into the composer or screenshotting a private
  screen, which is the disclosure happening anyway, without the app's help and
  without its encryption. **The shape is a copy, chosen one entry at a time:**
  `RuntimeHandles::share_journal_entry` (`comrade_ui/src/runtime.rs:6458`) reads
  the entry, formats `comrade_core::note`'s wire form and sends it as an ordinary
  Vault DM — no new transport, no bulk form, no date range, and the entry itself
  is not touched, flagged or moved (pinned by
  `sharing_a_note_leaves_the_journal_exactly_as_it_was`). **The marker stays on
  the wire**, copying `TARA_CHAT_PREFIX`'s reasoning exactly: a NIP-17 DM opened
  in another Nostr client should still say where the words came from, which a
  JSON payload would not. `MessageDto::shared_note` **and**
  `DirectMessageDto::shared_note` carry the parsed note, so the live event and
  the reloaded thread agree and no frontend re-reads the grammar — the second of
  those was added after finding that desktop and `app/` append live arrivals
  straight to the thread, which would have shown a note as marker text until
  restart (the bug `chat_commands.mjs`'s `splitAuthor` mirror exists to paper
  over for Tara, and which core answering both paths avoids repeating). **Three refusals
  are the design.** (1) The picker offers saved contacts only — every other send
  path can reach whoever's thread is open, and a note going to the wrong name in
  a list of strangers is the accident worth designing out. (2) Sharing does
  **not** screen the words for distress the way `tara_in_chat` does: there the
  user was addressing the companion and a mistyped sigil must not publish a
  crisis; here they picked an entry and then picked a person, and refusing at
  that moment would be the app deciding a bad week is too shameful to say aloud.
  (3) `share_journal_*` was added to `is_never_grantable` — it reads the journal,
  so a linked browser must not reach it however much the name looks like a
  messaging call. **Two honest gaps.** The chat-list preview shows the raw wire
  form (`conversations()` reads stored rows, as it already does for "Tara: …"),
  and desktop can *render* a received note but cannot send one, because that
  window still has no journal screen at all — OQ14, unchanged.

- **2026-08-08 (a question about the noise suppressor found the injection point was wrong):** Owner asked for two things on top of the entry below — *"a dedicated mic icon … where users can talk"*, and then *"isn't it better to have the noise cancellation and echo suppression … when the mic is turned on during together music/video play. make sure the actual media audio is untouched."* **The second is not a feature request; it is a bug report against the entry below, and it is right.** That entry records `JavaAudioDeviceModule.Builder.setAudioBufferCallback` as the seam that makes streaming feasible. It does make it feasible, and it is in the wrong place: the callback sits in `WebRtcAudioRecord`'s read loop, **before** the audio processing module, so a film injected there is fed to machinery built for speech — the noise suppressor gates a sustained note as noise, the automatic gain control lifts quiet scenes and flattens loud ones. Nobody would report that as "the injection point is wrong"; they would report it as *"the stream sounds bad"*, which is the kind of defect that survives a release. The seam that satisfies both halves of the ask at once is `ExternalAudioProcessingFactory.setCapturePostProcessing` (also fork-only): the microphone goes through AEC, NS and AGC exactly as a call's does, and the media audio joins **after** them and is never touched by any of it. `together/AudioInjection.kt` is now an `ExternalAudioProcessingFactory.AudioProcessing`, installed at `call/CallManager.kt:2074`. **The correction paid for itself twice over.** The `JavaAudioDeviceModule` became vestigial once injection moved, and removing it fixed a device-test failure it had caused: building that module probes the hardware AEC and NS, which cost ~900 ms inside `ensureFactory` — run before a call is placed — and `CallManagerLifecycleTest` asserts a return to `Idle` inside a 1600 ms `Ended` linger. A latency regression that reported itself as a state-machine failure, and would have been chased there. **The float buffer is the one assumption worth naming.** libwebrtc's post-processing stage hands out float samples on the *int16* scale (±32768), not normalised to ±1; that lives as `PcmMix.FLOAT_FULL_SCALE`, a single constant, so if a build ever disagrees there is one edit and the symptom is unmistakable — film audio either inaudible or clipped flat. `AudioInjection.process` also **fails to silence, never to noise**: a frame whose geometry is not 4 bytes per sample at 10 ms is left alone rather than written into on a guess, because a wrong guess about a buffer's layout is full-scale garbage on somebody's ears. **The mic defaults off** and `TogetherManager.toggleMic` (`:276`) is the only thing that turns it on — sound joins the stream either way, since the film is what is being shared; the toggle decides whether the sender's room joins it, which is `PcmMix.mixInto` against `replaceInto`. **The video path** is `together/PlayerVideoCapturer.kt`: the `MediaPlayer` is given the capturer's `SurfaceTexture` as its output surface, so the frames the decoder already produces are the frames that go out — no second decode. `isScreencast()` returns **false** deliberately, because a true there tells the encoder to favour detail over motion, which is the correct trade for a slide deck and the wrong one for a film. **The transport needed no wire change**, which is the part worth keeping: `together/StreamTransfer.kt` is a second `PeerConnection` negotiated over the *existing* `ShareSignal::Transport`, and the receiver tells a stream from a handover by the fact that **a `Transport` offer arriving with no armed transfer cannot be a file** — the handover's exchange is `Ask` → `Offer` → `Accept` → `Transport…`, so nothing asked for one and nothing accepted one. The SDP's own m-lines say the rest (a transfer opens a data channel; this adds tracks). No new signal, no `frb` regeneration, no version skew — an older build drops an offer it has no session for, which is what it already does. It gets its own connection rather than sharing the transfer's for the same reason the transfer does not share the call's: a continuous encode with a deadline and a bulk push under one congestion controller means the bulk push wins and the film stutters. **The gesture and the consent flow** are in `ui/TogetherScreen.kt:114-129`: `RECORD_AUDIO` first — worth knowing *why* a feature that is not about the microphone asks for it, which is that the media audio joins on the capture path so a capture has to be running at all — then `MediaProjection`. Both are refusable independently and neither is fatal: refusing the microphone streams the picture with no sound, and the launchers are hoisted out of the `when` because one created inside a branch is destroyed as the session changes state. Screen capture proceeds on the owner's reasoning that `FLAG_SECURE` is the platform's own protection and the app already sets it. **A trap found and now in `CLAUDE.md`:** `app/android/app/build.gradle.kts`'s `stagePreservedServices` copies every non-Compose `.kt` out of `android/app/src/main/java` and builds it into the Flutter app, so a new Android dependency has to be declared in *two* Gradle files or four Flutter jobs go red on a change that never touched Dart. **Verification:** `.claude/scripts/android-typecheck.sh` clean across 96 files (the script is the durable half of this work — it type-checks every non-Compose Android source here, against the real AAR and real uniffi bindings, and CI is no longer the first build for anything but Compose); decision tests green under `kotlinc` + JUnit; `node --test desktop/ui/*.test.mjs`, `cargo fmt`, `clippy -D warnings` and `cargo test --workspace` green. **Not verified, and it is the part that matters:** no capture has been started, no buffer injected, no frame sent, on any device. The one red CI job on this branch, `MainActivityUiTest > onboardingLeadsToChatsShell`, fails identically on `main` (run 31269792691) and is not from this work.

- **2026-08-08 (§1 is reversed for content we decode ourselves, and the one piece that was actually in doubt is possible):** Owner ask: *"ideally the app should just stream whatever is playing on one device — video/audio — with the best sync."* The instinct is right and worth stating as the finding rather than the concession: **every hard thing in §3–§11b exists because two independent players must be kept in step**, and a stream does not have that problem — one playhead, A/V sync handled by the transport, exact by construction rather than tuned. Design recorded as `docs/TOGETHER.md` §15. **What §1 actually forbids is narrower than it reads.** It rules out re-streaming or proxying *licensed* content — copyright, bandwidth, and a technical wall on DRM'd platforms — and every word still holds. It never reached a person sending **their own file to one person they invited**, which this app already does as the §9a handover; decoding that same file and sending pictures instead of bytes is the same act with a different codec. So streaming is allowed for a file the leader holds, and refused for the embed (both sides get the real player free, and re-streaming breaks YouTube's terms) and for service tracks (licensed, and capture is blocked anyway). **The feasibility risk was audio, not video.** Video was already most of the way there — `CallManager.startScreenShare` pushes a `ScreenCapturerAndroid` into a live `VideoSource` today. Audio looked closed: libwebrtc's Android path captures from the *microphone* and offers no supported way to hand it a buffer, so a shared film would arrive with the noise of the sender's room over it. Checking the AAR rather than assuming found `JavaAudioDeviceModule.Builder.setAudioBufferCallback` — present in `io.github.webrtc-sdk:android`, absent from upstream — whose `onBuffer(ByteBuffer, …)` is handed the record buffer *before* the encoder, so writing into it replaces the mic. **Superseded the same day — see the entry above: that seam is *before* the audio processing module too, which puts the film through the noise suppressor and the gain control. The injection moved to `ExternalAudioProcessingFactory` and the `JavaAudioDeviceModule` was removed.** **That makes the choice of WebRTC fork load-bearing rather than incidental**, which is now written down where a future dependency swap will hit it. The PCM comes from `AudioPlaybackCapture` with `addMatchingUid(Process.myUid())`: an app may always capture *itself* whatever anyone else's capture policy says, so `TogetherPlayer`'s `MediaPlayer` needs no replacing — at the cost of a `MediaProjection` consent dialog even to record ourselves. **Built:** `together/PlaybackCapture.kt` (the capture and the injection, type-checked against the real AAR) and `together/PcmRing.kt` with 9 tests. The ring is split out for a specific reason rather than tidiness: its read cursor is `writeAt - stored`, which goes negative every time the newest write has wrapped, and Kotlin's `%` keeps the sign of its left operand — a bug that throws on the audio thread, minutes into a session, and never in a short manual try. Its two policies are the audible ones and are tested as such: a full ring drops the **oldest** (the newest sample is the one closest to the picture, so preferring stale audio makes a momentary lag permanent), and an underrun yields **silence** rather than a repeat (a gap is a click; repeated audio is a stutter that sounds like a damaged file). **Deliberately not wired:** the `JavaAudioDeviceModule` has to be installed on `PeerConnectionFactory` — which is **shared with calls**, so replacing the default audio device module changes how every call captures (echo canceller, noise suppressor, source, sample rate). That is the area `.claude/rules/android.md` names as the most bug-prone in the repo, and it is not being done as a side effect at the end of a long session; the component is complete and inert until something installs it. **Q19 — screen capture of other apps** is scoped in §15 and not built, with its limits written down before anyone depends on them: `FLAG_SECURE` surfaces (Netflix, Prime, Disney+) come through black and cannot be fixed; apps setting `ALLOW_CAPTURE_BY_NONE` come through silent with no error (confidence is in the mechanism, not in which apps — that needs a device); and it may ship only as *"show them your screen"*, never named or marketed as a route to somebody else's subscription, the same line §13 draws for patched clients. **What streaming does not replace:** the handover is still better wherever it runs — full quality, survives going offline, no continuous bandwidth — and it got better the same day (§12, plays while it arrives). Streaming is the *third* answer to §9a, not a replacement for the first two. **Verification:** Android type-check clean across 90 files; 103 decision tests green (was 94). Nothing here has run: no capture has been started, no buffer injected, no frame sent.
- **2026-08-08 (read polius/FileSync against our own file transfer, then fixed three of the four gaps it named):** FileSync is a browser-only, self-hosted, one-to-many WebRTC file sender. Most of it is answering questions this repo answered differently on purpose — it runs a signaling server where we sign into a Nostr envelope, and half its cleverness is getting bytes past the browser's own download machinery, which Android and Tauri do not need. What it has is the same transfer run in front of real users on three engines, with the scars left in the code as comments. Full ledger in `docs/FILESYNC_REVIEW.md`, including what was **rejected** and why, so it is not revisited: the three-tier sink (desktop's 256 MiB ceiling is `crypto.subtle.digest` having no streaming form plus S-4, not the download mechanism), the signaling server, the per-recipient rewriting of private relay-candidate IPs (their coturn runs in a Docker bridge; ours is `network_mode: host`), and the outbound concurrency cap. Three things we already do better are recorded too, because they read as features in its source and as absences against ours — it has **no integrity check on the wire at all**, its sender `await`s a drain between chunks (which is why `_awaitDrain` had to grow close/error listeners), and it relays bulk through the operator's TURN without comment. **Q18 (H), P8 and Q19 are fixed and struck**; T5 is half closed. The work is worth reading for how it went rather than what it changed. The first attempt at Q19's stall watchdog *created* the bug it was added to prevent — one budget armed at data-channel open, no notion of a transfer not yet allowed to start, so it ended healthy transfers waiting on relay consent and dismissed the consent dialog while the person was reading it. A review caught that, plus a check-then-act race in arming that leaked a file handle only because P8 had just started holding one, and a late chunk that overwrote a peer's refusal with "Couldn't write the file". Then rebasing onto the streaming work surfaced a seam neither branch could see: §15 routes a transport signal by whether the file engine is armed, and Q19's watchdog enlarged the window where it is not, so a file offer arriving after this side gave up would have started an unexpected audio and video call. Fixed as a refinement of §15's rule, not a contradiction of it. Q20-Q23 record what was found and deliberately left: a `RefusalReason` enum that cannot say "I could not read the file" (a cross-layer `uniffi` change), `onTransport`'s missing redelivery guard, the mirror of the §15 seam after a *successful* transfer, and a seen-set inconsistency in `panic_wipe`. **Verification honesty:** every Rust change is covered by fmt / clippy `-D warnings` / `cargo test --workspace`, and the core regression test was confirmed failing with its guard neutralised. Kotlin is type-checked against real `android.jar` and real uniffi bindings (96 files) and the pure decision layer runs here (44 JUnit tests, with this round's red-before-green reproduced against the previous rule) — but **nothing in this repo executes `armSend`, `armReceive`, `claim`, `tearDown`, `endIfCurrent`, `startStallWatch`, `pump` or `ShareTransfer.onSignal`**, which is where every one of these defects lived. `./gradlew test` cannot run in that sandbox, no byte has moved over a real data channel, and the lock-ordering argument that justifies not using `@Synchronized` around a `PeerConnection` close is reasoning from the code, not an observation. That is the claim most worth checking on a device.

- **2026-08-08 (a user reported off-grid delivery still broken, and the tests said it worked):** The report was two phones on a hotspot with no upstream, and two phones over Bluetooth — neither delivered. Both traced to one line in `LocalRadios::send` (`crates/comrade_ui/src/runtime.rs`), which tried the WiFi mesh and **returned early when the publish succeeded**, so Bluetooth was never used whenever a mesh was up. The bug is that `MeshLink::publish` returning `true` does not mean the message arrived — it means gossipsub accepted the frame, which requires only that *somebody* subscribes to the sealed topic, not the recipient. A hotspot with client isolation is the exact shape that breaks: mDNS crosses the AP, peers are discovered, a publish is accepted, and nothing is carried; any unrelated third device on the network does it too. **This is the same error as the original `peer_count` indicator** — treating an intermediate success as delivery — which is worth stating plainly because it was reintroduced one layer down within a day of being fixed one layer up. Both radios now carry every frame, which the single-seal design already made safe: one ciphertext means a device hearing it twice dedups on envelope id. **The deeper finding is about the tests, not the code.** `saathi.rs` proved the protocol and `dak::ble` proved the wire format, so the two ends were green while everything between them — the routing decision, the ingress, the receipt, the outbox retry — had never been exercised together. `crates/comrade_ui/tests/offline_delivery.rs` closes that: two whole runtimes, no relay configured at all, and an `Air` harness that hands BLE packets between them in-process. It reproduced the user's failure on the first run. It is a required CI gate ("Offline delivery — no relay, no internet") rather than a line in the general `cargo test` job, because a shipped promise that has already broken in the field should name itself when it breaks again. The mDNS half is `#[ignore]`d into the existing environment-dependent lane.

- **2026-08-08 (owner asked for BLE, "like bitchat" — route 2b lands):** Follow-on from the two-phone report above. Route 2 needs a shared IP network, so two phones in a field, at a protest or in a blackout have nothing to talk over; BLE is bitchat's actual transport (whitepaper §4) and is now Comrade's route 2b. **Nothing in `dak` changed to add it** — `seal_dm`/`open_dm`, `Envelope` and the outbox never knew what carried a frame, which is exactly why the sealed-frame abstraction was kept transport-agnostic while only one radio used it (`dak::mesh`'s module doc had said "or, later, in Bluetooth range" since it was written). **The split is the design decision.** `comrade_core::dak::ble` owns the wire format, fragmentation and reassembly; `comrade_ui`'s `BleRouter` owns dedup, TTL, relaying and the two queues the radio drains; `android/…/ble/BleMeshService.kt` owns GATT roles, advertising, scanning and MTU negotiation and *nothing else*. The seam is four FFI calls (`ble_set_active`, `ble_set_mtu`, `ble_deliver`, `ble_drain_outbound`), and it is drawn where it is because everything that decides what goes on the wire, what is forwarded, and what a stranger in radio range can make the process allocate then runs under `cargo test` — while the half no CI can ever reach stays as thin as it can be. **Fragmentation**: a negotiated ATT MTU gives ~180–500 usable bytes against a 16 KiB envelope cap, so packets carry a 14-byte header (`version ‖ packet_id ‖ ttl ‖ index ‖ count`) and are rebuilt on arrival; the header carries no identity, so a passive listener learns that *a* device sent *some* frame in *n* pieces. **Flooding** is bounded three ways, all bitchat's: a 7-hop TTL decremented on relay, dedup on `packet_id` so a cycle in the peer graph cannot echo, and duty-cycled low-power scanning rather than continuous (a mesh that flattens the battery is a mesh nobody leaves on). Note the device relays frames it can never open — that forwarding *is* the mesh. **Reassembly is the attack surface** and is treated as such: anyone in range can open one by sending a first fragment claiming a large `count` and never sending the rest, so `Reassembler` caps concurrent partials *and* fragments per packet, evicts by age, and validates `count`/`index`/version before allocating anything proportional to what the sender claimed. A rebuilt envelope is still unauthenticated there; `open_dm`'s inner MAC is what decides it is genuine, exactly as on WiFi. **Two refactors fell out and are the better part of the diff.** `SealedIngress` extracts the per-frame handling both radios now share, so a Bluetooth frame is opened, authenticated and dispatched by the identical code a WiFi frame is — one door, as the original design intended, rather than a third copy of the gating/receipt/dedup rules to keep in sync. `LocalRadios` replaces the bare `MeshLink` at every offline-fallback site (send, delivered receipt, together signal) and seals **once** for both radios: a handle per radio would have meant finding every such site and remembering the second one, and the site that gets missed is always the receipt — whose absence makes the sender retry to its attempt cap. Sealing per-radio would also put two different ciphertexts for one message on the air and defeat the receiver's cross-transport dedup. **Precedence treats BLE as a *local* route** alongside WiFi rather than a third tier: the user's app-bar choice is "nearby before the internet", and that stays true whether nearby means this WiFi or Bluetooth range; `LocalRadios::send` tries WiFi first (higher bandwidth, reaches the whole network rather than the room) and Bluetooth second. **Permissions**: `BLUETOOTH_ADVERTISE` + `BLUETOOTH_SCAN` with `neverForLocation`, which is load-bearing rather than boilerplate — without it Android treats a BLE scan as a location request and demands `ACCESS_FINE_LOCATION`, i.e. a privacy-first messenger asking for the user's physical location in order to message somebody in the same room. Asked once on the way in rather than at send time, because a granted permission is what makes the radio discoverable *in advance*; denied is a first-class answer that simply leaves Bluetooth off the route list. **Verification, and the honest half.** Rust: `cargo fmt --check`, `clippy --workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace --locked` all green — 605 `comrade_core` and 216 `comrade_ui` tests, of which 12 + 6 are new here (wire format, fragmentation at every MTU from the floor up, out-of-order and duplicate arrival, TTL exhaustion, the hostile-packet table, bounded reassembly, an end-to-end seal→fragment→reassemble→`open_dm` round trip, relay-once semantics, and BLE-only delivery). Kotlin: **not compiled** — no Android SDK here — and, unlike every other Android change in this ledger, **CI cannot validate it either**: no runner has a Bluetooth adapter, so `BleMeshService.kt` is unproven until it runs on two real phones. That asymmetry is why the file is deliberately thin and why every decision worth testing was pushed across the FFI. **Not done**: iOS, and background execution beyond an unlocked foreground app (Android throttles background BLE scans hard; going further needs a foreground service with its own notification, deliberately not added here).

- **2026-08-08 (field report: two phones, one WiFi, airplane mode — presence showed, nothing sent):** Owner installed on two devices, put both in airplane mode with "This network first", saw the comrade dot and could not send a message. Four independent causes, each sufficient on its own, and **not one of them was visible to the in-process mesh test** — which is the finding worth keeping: a green `two_devices_on_one_network_exchange_a_sealed_dm` is evidence about the protocol, not about the platform. (1) **No WiFi multicast lock on Android.** `AndroidManifest.xml` had declared `CHANGE_WIFI_MULTICAST_STATE` since the mesh landed and nothing ever took the lock it grants; Android's WiFi driver silently drops multicast frames not addressed to the device, so mDNS announced outwards and never heard a reply. A Linux CI host has no such filter, which is exactly why this survived every green build. New `MeshRadio` holds it while the mesh runs and only while it runs (a held lock wakes the WiFi chip for every multicast frame on the network), driven from the one choke point that already sees every status change, `MeshStatusMonitor.update`. (2) **The indicator counted sightings, not reachability.** `SaathiEngine::peer_count` was the mDNS-discovered set while sending depended on peers *subscribed to the sealed-mail topic*; between those — a dial, a Noise handshake, a subscription exchange — `gossipsub::publish` fails `InsufficientPeers`. So the badge could truthfully say "1 device nearby" and every send still fail, which is precisely the report. New `MeshReach { discovered, deliverable }` keeps them apart, `peer_count` is now the second, and the driver tracks it off gossipsub's own `Subscribed`/`Unsubscribed` plus `ConnectionClosed` rather than off mDNS. mDNS `Expired` deliberately no longer drops a peer from deliverable: a lapsed record with a live TCP connection is still somewhere to send to. (3) **mDNS re-queried every 5 minutes** (libp2p's default), so a phone that joined a moment later — or whose one startup announcement was dropped, ordinary for multicast — was invisible for up to five minutes; now 20s. (4) **Gossipsub's heartbeat was 10s**, the floor on "the other phone appeared" → "a message will send"; now 1s. Discovered peers are also now dialled explicitly with the address mDNS handed us instead of leaning on gossipsub's implicit peer-id dial. Effect on the existing hardware-independent test: it went from needing a 30-second publish-retry loop to passing in 0.25s. **Three further gaps found while in there, all fixed.** *Routing ignored availability*: precedence was purely the user's app-bar setting, so with no relay connected a relay still went first and burned `CONNECT_WAIT` (5s, in `send_dm_reply`) before the local network was tried — `SendPlan::for_attempt` now takes a `TransportReach` and lets availability outrank the setting, which is the "switch between offline and online depending on what's there" the owner asked for; the setting is the tie-break for when both work, and `force_both` now arms off the route that actually led rather than off the setting (else a relay-preferring user with dead relays would sit on the mesh forever). *An attempt was a minute, not a failure*: `MAX_ATTEMPTS` is 8 and the flush cadence 60s, so mail queued with no network at all was marked **failed after about eight minutes**, silently overriding the 24-hour `TTL_SECS` that is supposed to govern off-grid waiting — a flush that finds no route now returns without spending an attempt (`prune`, and therefore real TTL expiry, still runs first). *Recovery waited for the next tick*: the outbox is now woken the instant the local network becomes deliverable, so a peer joining the WiFi a second after you hit send no longer leaves the message under a clock icon for the rest of the minute. Presence beacons a relay will not take are now sealed onto the mesh too — the receive side needed nothing, since an opened mesh frame already runs the same ingress — so the dot means "reachable now" rather than "was reachable within the last `PRESENCE_TTL_SECS`", which is what made the network look alive while nothing could be sent. **No FFI shape changed**, deliberately: `MeshStatusDto` keeps its two fields so the committed flutter_rust_bridge bindings stay valid, and the fix rides entirely on `peer_count` meaning something truer. **Verification**: `cargo fmt --check`, `clippy --workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings` and `cargo test --workspace --locked` all green (209 `comrade_ui` tests, 593 `comrade_core`), plus both `#[ignore]`d real-mDNS mesh tests run locally. New tests: `saathi::tests::being_seen_on_the_network_is_not_the_same_as_being_reachable`, `runtime::tests::a_route_that_is_down_never_goes_first_whatever_the_user_picked`, `…::the_app_bar_setting_decides_only_when_both_routes_work`, `…::with_nothing_reachable_the_order_is_whatever_was_asked_for`, `…::patience_runs_out_on_whichever_route_led`, `…::mail_with_nowhere_to_go_waits_for_the_ttl_instead_of_burning_the_attempt_cap`, and Android `MeshRadioTest`. **The Kotlin is not compiled here** (no Android SDK) — CI is its first build. **Still unverified, and it is the thing that matters**: the two-phone re-test. Everything above is reasoned from the reported symptom plus the code; only hardware can confirm the multicast lock was the missing piece. Also still true and now stated in the README: airplane mode with WiFi *off* is not a network, so route 2 needs WiFi or a hotspot re-enabled, and guest-WiFi client isolation defeats it regardless.

- **2026-08-08 (the three players behind the seam, and three confident plans that were wrong):** `docs/TOGETHER_PLAYERS_HANDOFF.md` Tasks B, C and D, built against the `SessionPlayer` seam Task A landed. Every decision layer in PR #101 was unreachable from any UI; a user's experience was local files and files handed over a peer connection. All three routes are now wired. **Task B — the YouTube embed** (`together/YoutubeSessionPlayer.kt`, `TogetherScreen.EmbedSurface`, `ChatsScreen`'s `PLAY_EMBED` arm): the one route to "play something neither of us has" that needs no account on either side. **The plan's dependency would have turned the branch red**, and the check that missed it is the reusable part — `androidyoutubeplayer:core:13.0.0` was verified *present on Maven Central*, which it is, while its POM pulls `lifecycle-runtime-ktx:2.9.4` (compiled against API 35; AGP fails a `compileSdk = 34` build outright) and `kotlin-stdlib:2.1.0` (metadata the pinned 1.9.22 compiler refuses). 12.1.2 carries `lifecycle-runtime-ktx:2.6.0` and `kotlin-stdlib-jdk8:1.8.0`; its API surface for everything used here was checked **class by class with `javap` against both AARs**, which is a thing this sandbox can do for a dependency it cannot compile against, and which should be the habit before writing an adapter only CI will link. **Ad breaks are held and deliberately not named.** §9a's problem is real — a break is per-viewer, so one side gets a pre-roll and the session comes apart by exactly that much through no fault of the clock — and `TogetherDecisions.StallWatch` (10 tests) observes the one fact available: the player claims to be playing and the position it reports is standing still. It does **not** claim to know why, because an ad and an unlabelled stall are the same observation from inside the embed. That costs nothing, since both want the same answer, and the poll expresses it as `together_report_position(pos, false, …)` — a heartbeat the peer answers by holding — never `together_set_state`, which would pause them for the length of our ad. The half of the ask that could not be built honestly is the *sentence*: the heartbeat carries no reason, so a held peer is indistinguishable from a paused one, and adding a reason is a protocol change left open rather than invented. `STALL_AFTER_MS` is one tick wider than `COARSE_EXTRAPOLATE_MAX_MS` so the estimate has been *seen* to go flat first, with a test asserting the inequality rather than letting it survive as a coincidence. Background playback stays off (a Premium feature of their client, not ours to grant), and the "this keeps playing when you leave the app" note is swapped for one that says YouTube pauses — a note claiming otherwise would be the comment-shaped lie this repo calls a bug, printed at the user. **Task C — following another app's session** (`MediaSessionListenerService`, `together/MediaSessionAccess.kt`, `together/ExternalSessionPlayer.kt`): one implementation reaching Spotify, YouTube Music, podcast apps and VLC alike, with no OAuth, no client id and no vendored `.aar`. The listener service overrides nothing — `getActiveSessions` demands an *enabled* notification listener and that API is the whole reason it exists, which an empty body states better than a comment. The `PlaybackState.STATE_*` ints are mapped in exactly one function, so `MediaSessionDecisions` still imports nothing and still runs under `kotlinc`; `trackKey` joined it, because which fields identify a track is a decision rather than a read (blank title → blank key, refused; missing **artist** → weaker key, *not* silence, since podcast apps routinely publish none). **The plan listed the pieces and not how a session starts, and the honest answer is narrower than it implied: Comrade follows, it does not start** — there is no "play track X" on another app's transport controls — so the entry point is the invited side, gated by `PlaybackModeDecision.ownershipFor`, asked by both the button and the action so they cannot disagree. Starting *from* what the phone is playing needs a `TogetherContent` variant that does not exist, and is logged as an open question rather than added, since a wire variant ripples into the Kotlin and Dart exhaustive matches. Two traps found in the building: `state.actions and ACTION_SEEK_TO != 0L` parses as `actions and (ACTION_SEEK_TO != 0L)` because Kotlin's infix `and` binds looser than `!=` (it fails to typecheck here, which is the lucky outcome; the same shape on an `Int` mask compiles and is always wrong), and `MediaController.registerCallback(cb)` posts to the *calling* thread's `Looper` and throws when there is none. **Task D — playing a handed-over file while it arrives** (`transfer/PartialFileDataSource.kt`, `transfer/ShareReadPolicy.kt`, `TogetherPlayer.open(MediaDataSource)`): core's `playable_at`/`runway_ms` had been dead code since the transfer landed and are not any more. **Writing it uncovered a real ordering bug in code that had been correct until now** — `FileTransfer.onChunk` recorded the chunk in the tracker *before* writing its bytes, which was invisible for exactly as long as nothing read the partial file; the moment a decoder does, that window has the bitmap claiming bytes the file still holds as zeroes, and a decoder reading zeroes does not stall, it produces a corrupt frame or abandons the file. Write first, record second, which also gives the `synchronized` block in `readAt` a happens-before edge that publishes the bytes wherever the flag is visible. Second regression of the same species: reopening the finished file reset the playhead to zero, so the transfer *completing* threw the listener back to the start of a track they were halfway through — the position is carried across now, with the seek arming the echo suppressor, because an unexplained `onSeekComplete` is re-broadcast as the user having seeked. **"Android has no tracker yet" was also wrong**: `ShareDecisions.Tracker` shipped with the transfer and had `playableAt`; what it lacked was `tailCompleteAt` and the verdict, and `playableAt` is now built *on* the former rather than beside it. The thresholds live in `ShareReadPolicy`, a file with **no imports at all** — its neighbour `ShareDecisions` cannot run under `kotlinc` because it needs the generated uniffi types, which sharpens `CLAUDE.md`'s rule: the bar is not "no Android imports", it is **no imports**. **Q18 — the frb mirror for `ReadVerdict` is left undone on purpose.** `Comrade::share_read_verdict` is exposed over uniffi, synchronous and lock-free because the only place Android wants it is inside `readAt`, where a lock on the runtime would deadlock against the transfer writing the chunk being waited for. `#[frb(mirror(ReadVerdict))]` would mean regenerating the whole bridge for a type no Dart code references — `app/` has no together or share surface at all — and the regeneration cannot be verified here without a ~10-minute Flutter install. Exit condition: the first Dart consumer. **Verification**, by exit code: `cargo fmt --check`, `clippy --workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings` (after `rustup update stable`, per the trap that has already turned a branch red once), `cargo test --workspace --locked`, `node --test desktop/ui/*.test.mjs` (417), and the Android decision layers under `kotlinc` + JUnit (**94**, up from 74). **Not verified at first push, and the caveat turned out to be load-bearing within the hour:** CI failed on one line — `refreshLive(durationMs = …)` against a function with no such parameter, in a file the JUnit lane cannot see because `TogetherManager` imports Android. **The fix took a minute and the round trip took a build**, which is the cost this repo has been paying per Android typo since it started. So the boundary was pushed instead of the caveat repeated: `.claude/scripts/android-typecheck.sh` compiles **84 of the 87 sources under `mullu/comrade/`** with no Android SDK — against a real `android.jar` (Robolectric's `android-all` for API 34), the **real** generated uniffi bindings (`cargo` produces those; only `sdkmanager` was ever missing), and the real AARs for WebRTC, Vosk and the YouTube player, with `R`/`BuildConfig` generated from the actual resource files. Only the files importing `androidx.compose` are left out, the Compose compiler plugin being the part not worth reproducing. It was checked by **putting the bug back and watching it go red** — reproducing CI's exact message at CI's exact line — because a check that cannot fail is not a check. `CLAUDE.md`'s "What this sandbox cannot run" is updated accordingly; this is the second time that section has been too pessimistic, the first being the discovery that `kotlinc` runs the framework-free files at all. **What genuinely remains CI-first:** Compose (`TogetherScreen`), the manifest entry, Gradle's dependency resolution, and `./gradlew test`. And none of that is "the feature works" — nothing here has run a `MediaPlayer`, followed a real `MediaSession` or drawn a frame.

- **2026-08-05 (two owner decisions taken: Comrade asks for the music library, and Tara gets her own bubble):** Both open questions from the `@tara` work, answered and built. **Q15 — the permission.** The owner chose to ask, so `AndroidManifest.xml` declares `READ_MEDIA_AUDIO` (and `READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` capped at API 32), and `together/MediaLibraryAccess.kt` decides when the question is put. Three things about that decision are the substance rather than the manifest line. It asks **at the moment somebody names a song**, never at startup, because a permission prompt on a cold app is a prompt with no reason attached. It asks **at most once**: Android stops showing the dialog after a refusal and a later request returns denied with nothing on screen, so a second ask would be a button that silently does nothing — `next(granted, askedBefore)` returns `Picker` from then on and the file-picker route, which needs no permission at all, carries the feature. And it asks **only when a copy would change the answer**, which `/play` establishes by calling core twice — `play_route(plan, false)` against `play_route(plan, true)` — rather than reimplementing the route table in Kotlin: a Spotify link resolves to "open it there" either way, and raising a dialog to reach the same sentence is how people learn to refuse. The invitation path gets its own affordance instead, a "Look in my library" button on the card, because that is the moment the permission is obviously worth something; `TogetherManager.lookAgain` re-runs the lookup and joins if the copy is there, and returns whether it was so the card can say "no copy of that on this phone" rather than leaving a tap that appears to do nothing. That auto-open (`TogetherManager.kt:125`) had never worked once since it was written. **Q16 — the author field.** The exit condition was a container with `flutter_rust_bridge_codegen`, which earlier sessions did not have; this one installed the pinned 2.12.0 and Flutter 3.44.8 and **regenerated** `frb_generated.rs`/`.dart` rather than hand-editing them, per `.claude/rules/flutter.md`. `MessageDto` now carries `author: MessageAuthor`, and all three frontends draw her line as hers. Two departures from the exit condition as written, both on purpose.
