The prefix **stays** on the wire and on disk rather than going away: a NIP-17 DM read in some other Nostr client has no field to read, and "Tara: …" is a truer fallback there than her words in the sender's mouth — `split_author` is the one place that turns the wire form into `(author, content)`, called from both `MessageDto` construction sites, and `her_line_is_still_marked_as_hers_after_a_reload` is the test that a restart cannot silently turn her answer into one of yours. And her bubble is **left-aligned on both devices with no ticks**: her answer is carried by whichever phone asked, so aligning by `outgoing` would put one line on opposite sides of the two screens and stop it reading as a third participant at all; the tick goes with it, because the question directly above carries the same receipt and a tick on a third party's line reads as a claim about her. What the field does not buy is authentication, and that got *sharper* rather than softer — a forged "Tara: " used to be plain text and is now a named, coloured bubble. Logged as **Q17** and accepted, with the honest tests kept (`the_marker_is_a_label_and_the_test_says_so`, and the desktop mirror's twin) and the rule that nothing may gate on the field. **Verification**, by exit code: `cargo fmt --check`, `clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace`, `node --test desktop/ui/*.test.mjs`, and — for the first time in this repo's Claude sessions — `flutter analyze --fatal-infos`, `flutter test` and `dart format --set-exit-if-changed` actually run rather than deferred to CI. **Not verified:** Android (no SDK here), so the manifest, the two permission call sites and the Compose bubble are CI's first build; and `desktop/src-tauri`, which still cannot link without GTK headers.

- **2026-08-04 (a browser can be a screen, not a peer — and two things got measured):** Owner ask: finish the Flutter migration and add a web frontend you sign in to by scanning a QR. The analysis is `docs/FLUTTER_WEB_MIGRATION.md`; two measurements in it decide the whole shape and both were compiled here rather than reasoned about. **(1) `comrade_core` does not build for `wasm32-unknown-unknown`** — `cargo check -p comrade_core --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` fails with 48 errors in `mio`, reached through tokio's net stack. Combined with `comrade_storage` opening the vault via `redb::Database::create` (`crates/comrade_storage/src/lib.rs:116`), which wants a real file and an exclusive lock, the "run the core in the browser" branch is closed, not expensive. **(2) The subset a linked browser actually needs *does* build** — `crypto.rs` + `pad.rs` + `link.rs` + `error.rs` compile for that target with `link.rs` unchanged and `crypto.rs` needing one import line (`nostr_sdk` → `nostr`) — with one caveat the first version of this entry omitted: the probe also stubbed `call::EMOJI_ALPHABET`, `link.rs`'s only cross-module reference outside its tests, so the wasm-reachable subset is `link.rs` + `error.rs` + that `const`, and the Phase W1 extraction has to carry the alphabet with it or leave a dangling edge into `call.rs`. So the web client is a **linked device**: it mints a throwaway keypair, shows it as a QR, and a device with an unlocked vault adopts it; the identity nsec never enters the browser and what crosses is a scoped, expiring, revocable capability. `crates/comrade_core/src/link.rs` is that protocol (61 tests), written against `nostr` from the start specifically so finding (2) stays true. Two boundaries are in the type system rather than in a host's approval sheet, so no future UI can offer them and no user can be talked into them: **the journal and Tara are unreachable at every scope** — there is no `LinkScope` that turns them on, and `LinkScopes::permits` refuses every `journal_*`/`tara_*` method regardless of grant — and `unlock_vault`/`lock_vault`/`generate_identity`/`generate_keypair`/`npub_from_nsec`/`set_turn_server`/`panic_wipe` stay with the key-holder. An unknown method fails closed, so an export added to `api.rs` without a matching `scope_for` arm cannot silently widen the perimeter. **No signature scheme was added**: frames ride inside `dak::courier::seal`, whose inner MAC is keyed by the static-static shared secret, so `courier::open` already returns an *authenticated* sender — which is exactly what lets the browser learn which npub adopted it (`the_seal_authenticates_which_host_answered_an_offer`). The pairing fingerprint shares the call SAS's emoji alphabet (`call.rs`'s `EMOJI_ALPHABET`, now `pub(crate)`) so the user learns one comparison ritual, but is deliberately **not** symmetric — the roles are fixed here, and it pins which way round the pairing went. 20 bits is thin for an offline digest and adequate here: the evil twin must collide with the fingerprint of *your* browser's fresh random session key, which they do not know when they show you their code, and prompts are rate-limited with the limit inside the snapshot so a force-quit does not reset it. **New gaps recorded rather than fixed**, all in `SCREEN_INVENTORY.md` §5 items 10–12. Five Compose screens (2,024 LOC: Breathing, Focus, Comrades, Reader, Together, plus the voice-model dialog) are absent from `app/` *and* were absent from the inventory that exists to stop exactly that, and its header LOC figures are stale by more than 2×. Sharper than "unported views", though: **the whole Attention feature is unreachable from Flutter at the ABI.** `active_focus_session`, `start_focus_session`, `finish_focus_session`, `focus_presets`, `focus_prompt`, `focus_reflection`, `focus_sessions`, `suggested_focus_minutes`, `attention_days`, `attention_summary` and `record_attention_day` — 11 functions — are exported by **both** shipping bridges (uniffi and Tauri) and by **no** line of `crates/comrade_jni/src/api.rs`. Same class as the Sakha hole `FRONTEND_STRATEGY.md` §10 found, four times the size, and it cannot start without a codegen run. Together is bridged to FRB (6 exports) but declared by nothing in Dart. This is `FRONTEND_STRATEGY.md` §2's *parity debt* — the thing it measured as the real problem, as against UI duplication — reproduced on the replacement frontend before the originals have been retired, which is the argument for gating retirement on it. One more: the feed's gentle-stop threshold lives only in `android/.../attention/ScrollSitting.kt` (`THRESHOLD_MS`, ten minutes), tested only in Kotlin and absent from `comrade_core`, `desktop/ui/` and `feed_screen.dart`; a Dart port must move it into `comrade_core::attention` rather than become its third copy. **Caught by review, on this branch, and worth recording as method rather than as trivia:** (a) **The "verified clippy" claim in the first two commits was false and the branch was red.** `LinkScopes::from_iter` tripped `clippy::should_implement_trait`; it is now a real `impl FromIterator`. Clippy *had* been run — but its output was filtered through `grep -E "^(error|warning)"`, and clippy colourises, so `error` never starts a line and nothing matched, while the **exit code was never examined**. Grepping a compiler's stdout is not a substitute for its status. (b) **The pairing fingerprint prevents less than the module claimed.** A `LinkOffer` carries nothing about the host, so the browser cannot compute a fingerprint until it has *already* been granted a session — meaning an evil twin's tab shows a fingerprint matching the phone's exactly, and what the emoji actually provide is after-the-fact detection ("that row is not a tab I have open — revoke it"). Prevention rests on the approval sheet, `MAX_PAIRING_ATTEMPTS`, and revocability. Closing it needs the offer to commit to a host key, which is a protocol change and a `WIRE_VERSION` bump; it is written down in the plan as open rather than papered over. (c) **`Reassembler` had no session identity** while its cap was documented "per peer": one instance shared across linked sessions would let session B complete, contradict or starve session A's half-built frames, since the frame id is an attacker-chosen `u64`. There is now no `Default` and no `new()` — `for_session` takes the session key, so the sharing bug is unwritable. (d) **`grant()` could not enforce the pairing rate limit it depended on.** `note_pairing_attempt` was a separate, unenforced obligation, and it is the only bound on the fingerprint grind; it now returns a `PairingAttempt` receipt that `grant` consumes and checks against the offer, so the ordering is a compile error rather than a doc comment. (e) **`toggle_workspace` was reachable under "Send messages as you"** — and `comrade_ui` notes that entering `OffGridTravel` "really starts mDNS discovery, it doesn't just flip a label", so a browser tab could have decided what the host phone radiates to the room. Now never-grantable, along with `back`. (f) **Relay hints were not sanitised the way labels are**: `validate_relay` rejected only `char::is_whitespace`, and neither NUL nor U+202E RIGHT-TO-LEFT OVERRIDE is `White_Space`, so both reached the linked-devices list where U+202E reverses everything after it. Both paths now refuse the `Cf` bidi controls too, which `char::is_control` does not cover. (g) **`LinkGrant.version` was written and never read**, so `WIRE_VERSION`'s refuse-don't-guess promise covered only the offer URI; `LinkGrant::accept` is now the reader, and also checks the grant's session and that its claimed host is the key that actually sealed it. (h) **`flutter build web` bakes a gstatic CanvasKit URL** that `index.html`'s own CSP blocks, so the page would have booted to a permanent placeholder; the lane now passes `--no-web-resources-cdn` and greps the artifact for off-origin hosts. (i) Stale citations fixed: `comrade_storage/src/lib.rs:115`→`116`, and `test_turn_connectivity` was wrongly grouped with the four Sakha methods — it exists on no runtime and no bridge at all, so no codegen run can export it.

**Not done, stated plainly:** `crates/comrade_jni/src/api.rs` is untouched, so nothing in `link.rs` is reachable from Dart — adding to it needs `flutter_rust_bridge_codegen`, whose last step is `dart run build_runner`, and this container has no Dart while CI checks the generated code is not stale (`flutter.yml:137-141`). `app/web/` exists and has a `flutter build web --release` lane, but the web target reaches only `FakeComradeRepository`; no QR has been rendered, no camera has scanned one, no grant has crossed a relay, and the host-side method table and transport (Phase W3) are unbuilt.

- **2026-08-04 (Tara joins the conversation, and three device-reported bugs):** The owner asked for `@tara` to work like `@Meta AI` in WhatsApp — a third participant both people can see — with `/tara` staying the private session. **The sigil is now the audience**, and that is the whole design: `ChatCommand::TaraHere` for `@tara` and `ChatCommand::AskTara` for `/tara`, two variants rather than one with a `shared: bool`, so every match site has to name which audience it is handling and the one that must never reach a relay keeps the name it always had. Both composers label the box from the moment the sigil is typed, because one character decides whether a private thought gets published or a question the other person was waiting for was never sent at all. **The one exception is core's, not a frontend's:** a question that trips `detect_distress` is answered and **nothing is sent** (`TaraChatDto::kept_private`) — a crisis hand-off is not something to publish into somebody else's chat on the asker's behalf, and the grammar cannot tell that case from any other until the reply exists, so the check sits in `RuntimeHandles::tara_in_chat` between the reply and the send. `distress_in_a_shared_ask_is_answered_but_never_sent` asserts the thread *and* the outbox stay empty. The third-party reframe was already there and matters more here, since a characterisation of `@ana` would now be delivered to the person you are talking to; `naming_a_third_party_still_reframes_when_the_room_can_read_it` pins it. The shared exchange deliberately does **not** touch the private `tara_companion` thread — a question asked in front of somebody is not a turn in a journal-adjacent space the peer has no part in — so the prompt-rotation seed counts Tara lines in *that* thread instead. **What is honest rather than ideal:** her answer travels as an ordinary DM carrying `tara::TARA_CHAT_PREFIX`, because `MessageDto` has no author field and adding one means regenerating `crates/comrade_jni/src/frb_generated.rs`, which no sandbox here can do. The prefix is therefore **rendering, not authentication** — `the_marker_is_a_label_and_the_test_says_so` asserts that a hand-typed line matches, so no frontend can be built on the belief that a match proves she spoke. The answer also replies to the question by event id: two DMs sent in the same second share a `created_at` and the receiver's thread sorts on that, so without the `e` tag her answer could land above what it answered — and the tag is skipped when the question only reached the outbox, since a local id names no event any relay has seen. **Then the three bugs, all reported from a device and none catchable by an existing test.** (1) The `/` picker could not be scrolled: a bare `/` matches the entire catalogue at two lines a row, inside an uncapped `Column`, so the rows below the fold were unreachable and the thread was pushed off screen. `pickerRows` was correct and its tests still pass — the defect was in *how many rows fit*, which nothing tested. Desktop was never affected (`.command-picker` has always had `max-height` + `overflow-y: auto`). (2) An ambiguous `@handle` was a dead end: *"pick which one"* with nothing to pick, so the command could not be completed at all. The ambiguity was right; the chooser was missing. `ComposerPlan.Choose` / `CHOOSE` now carries the candidates and picking one re-runs the same draft through `withChoices`, which honours a pin **only** while it still names one of that handle's candidates — a stale choice silently retargeting a message is the same failure by another door. Every row carries the short key, because the usual reason a handle is ambiguous is that both contacts also chose the same name, and a chooser offering two identical rows is no chooser. (3) Opening Tasks from the drawer killed the process: `TaskListScreen` used the only two early `return@Column`s in the Android source, so the Column emitted a different number of composable groups before and after `loaded` flipped — the shape that throws on the recomposition rather than the first frame. It is a single `when` now, like every other screen here. **That third diagnosis is from reading the code, not from a stack trace** — there is no Android SDK here — so the regression cover is on the emulator instead: `MainActivityUiTest` taps `drawer-tasks` and waits for the list to finish loading, which is exactly the recomposition that failed. Two things were fixed in passing and are worth naming because both were silent: the composer never showed the helplines beside a distress reply, so `AUDIT.md` §8's honesty gate held on the Tara tab and not in the composer reaching the same engine (`ChatCommands.crisisLines` is the plain-text twin of `CrisisCard`, since a composer note is one `Text` with no card slot) — and `desktop/ui/styles.css`'s `.input.composer-aside` rule was **missing its closing brace**, which swallowed the following `.share-consent` block entirely, so the relay-consent panel had been rendering unstyled. **Verification**, by exit code: `cargo fmt --all -- --check` 0, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings` 0, `cargo test --workspace --locked` 0, `node --test desktop/ui/*.test.mjs` 0 (258 cases). The uniffi Kotlin was **generated** and read rather than guessed at, confirming `suspend fun taraInChat(peer, text): TaraChatDto`, the `asked`/`answered`/`reply`/`keptPrivate`/`crisis` property spellings, and `ChatCommand.TaraHere(text)`. **Not verified:** no Android SDK, so no Kotlin compiled here and nobody has typed `@tara` into a running app; `desktop/src-tauri` cannot build in this container (no `gdk-3.0.pc`), so the new `tara_in_chat` Tauri command is registered and unexercised until CI; `app/` (Flutter) gets none of this, as with the rest of the in-chat actions.

- **2026-08-04 (`/play` finally opens a session, and found the reason it cannot yet):** `docs/CHAT_ACTIONS.md` §7's next gap after the task list: `/play kun faya kun` parsed, `play_query` resolved it, Android's `TogetherManager` and `LibraryResolver` both existed, and nothing joined them — the composer said *“Open Together from the menu”* about a session it could have started itself. The chain runs end to end now. **The decision went into Rust rather than into the composer**, which is the choice worth recording: `comrade_ui::play_route(plan, found_local_copy)` returns one of five routes, and only `StartTogether` opens a player. It is pure, so it is tested where the tests actually run in this container — three cases, one of which exists because the tempting shape was wrong. `found_local_copy` is consulted **only** for `FindLocally`: someone with a similarly titled mp3 on their phone has not acquired the right to decode a Spotify stream, so a `/play <spotify url>` must not quietly start a session on a different file while the UI claims the two of them are listening to the same thing. A frontend deciding that for itself is how the two would have drifted. Two refusals are deliberate and are **not** “not supported yet”, because neither is coming: Spotify and Apple Music serve audio no third-party client may decode, and Android cannot *start* a YouTube session either — core can carry that invitation, but this app has no player for one and `TogetherManager` cannot join its own, so sending it would invite somebody to something we cannot attend. **Then the real find, logged as Q15.** The library lookup that the whole feature turns on **cannot succeed at all**: `LibraryResolver.query` reads `MediaStore.Audio.Media.EXTERNAL_CONTENT_URI` and the manifest declares neither `READ_MEDIA_AUDIO` nor `READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE`, so the cursor is empty on every supported release. It is not this feature's bug — `TogetherManager.kt:125` auto-opens the listener's own copy through the same call when an invitation arrives, so **that has never worked either**, and its silence is indistinguishable from “you don't have this track”. Antra's framing — *the answer to how do we both have this file is you already did* — rests entirely on this lookup. What shipped is the honest version rather than a quiet manifest edit: `LibraryResolver.mayRead` tells “not allowed to look” apart from “looked, not there”, and `ChatCommands.LIBRARY_UNSEEN` says the first instead of the second, because sending somebody hunting for a file that is already on their phone is worse than admitting the app cannot see it. The file-picker route needs no permission (SAF grants per file), so `/play` is useful today. Whether Comrade should ask to read someone's music library is a user-visible privacy decision and the owner's to make; Q15 states both exits. One more thing was caught in review rather than by a test: `ComradeCore.playRoute` first defaulted to `Nothing` on FFI failure, and every route carries a *sentence* — so a broken bridge would have told the user “name a song, or paste a link” when they had named one. It returns `null` now and the caller says what actually happened, which is the same rule the `/play` refusals are built on. **Verification**, by exit code: `cargo fmt --check` 0, `clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings` 0, `cargo test --workspace` 0, `node --test desktop/ui/*.test.mjs` 0. The uniffi Kotlin was **generated** with `comrade_uniffi_bindgen` and read rather than guessed at, which is how `PlayRoute.NOTHING`'s trailing `;`, the `START_TOGETHER`/`ASK_FOR_FILE`/`OPEN_ELSEWHERE`/`PLAY_EMBED` spellings, `MusicService.APPLE_MUSIC`, `playRoute(plan, foundLocalCopy)`'s signature, and the fact that `comrade_ui`'s `MusicService`/`Recording` are *imports of* the `comrade_core` types (so the two crates' names are one type, not two) were all confirmed. **Not verified:** no Android SDK, so none of the Kotlin compiled here and nobody has typed `/play` into a running app; `desktop/src-tauri` cannot build in this container either (no `gdk-3.0.pc`), so the new `play_route` Tauri command is registered and unexercised until CI. Desktop is deliberately unchanged otherwise: `DESKTOP_CAN_PLAY` still refuses `/play` because there is still no player there, and the command is exposed so the decision is not reimplemented in JS the day one lands.

- **2026-08-04 (the task list, and a CI failure worth recording as a method problem):** Three things, and the first is not about the feature. **CI was red on both in-chat-actions commits while the branch had been reported green.** Two mechanical faults — `clippy::assertions_on_constants` on a constant-folded `assert!` in `catalogue.rs`, and `ChatCommandsTest.kt` importing `kotlin.test` when this project's test classpath carries `org.junit.Assert` (which also takes its message argument *first*, so two call sites were wrong even after the import was fixed). The Kotlin one blocked `compileDebugUnitTestKotlin` **and** `compileReleaseUnitTestKotlin`, so the whole Android lane failed and none of the ~290 existing JVM tests ran. **The method problem is the part worth keeping**: each lane had been checked with `cargo … 2>&1 | grep -E "^(error|warning)"`, and cargo's error lines begin with an ANSI escape rather than the letter `e`, so the filter could not match the failure it was aimed at. Lanes are checked by **exit code** now, which is also what the repo's own `/verify` skill does. A second thing that had been left on the table: this workspace ships `comrade_uniffi_bindgen`, so the Kotlin bindings *can* be generated here — doing so confirmed every signature the branch's Kotlin depends on (`Mention`'s `UInt` spans, `CommandSpec.takesMention`, all nine `ChatCommand` variants and their arities, `TaskDto`/`OfferOutcomeDto` fields, and that `assignTask`/`setTaskState`/`offerAction` are `suspend`). It does not replace a compile, but "unverified" was doing more work than it had to. **Then `origin/main` was merged** — 35 commits of reactions, attachment handoff and share settings, 15 hunks across 9 files. Eight were unions with nothing to decide; the one that mattered was `dispatch_incoming_dm`, where main and this branch had both claimed the slot after step 6 and both renumbered the tail. The chain now reads `6 together · 6a handoff · 7 reaction · 8 task · 9 offer · 10 media · 11 plain chat`, main's first because reactions and handoff are the higher-traffic paths and every arm returns early. Two things had to survive that and did: both karya and offer arms still call `is_cross_transport_duplicate` on the envelope bytes before applying — the only thing between one offer and two bubbles — and the DTO region kept both sides. `desktop/ui/main.js` merged clean textually despite +933 from main, which is exactly where a silent break hides, so it was read rather than trusted (one `input` listener, `handleDmSend` still calling `handleChatCommand` before anything can reach `send_dm`, the picker and the handoff card as separate elements, every mock case from both sides still answered). **And the task list finally exists**, closing what `docs/CHAT_ACTIONS.md` §7 called its largest gap: `/task water the plants` used to be stored, reachable over FFI, and rendered by nothing, while the composer said *"Added to your list."* about a list nobody could open. Android reaches it from the drawer (`ChatNav.Tasks`), desktop gained a fourth tab. The decisions are pure and mirrored — `TaskList.kt` and `task_list.mjs`, 11 JVM cases against 15 `node --test` — and the rule they exist to hold is that **the buttons a row offers mirror `karya::may_transition`**: an assigner never sees Done, an assignee never sees Withdraw, a finished row shows nothing, and a note to self gets all three. A UI offering a button core answers with "that is not yours to change" is a UI that lies about what it can do. Three bugs were caught writing it, all by checking rather than assuming — and the third is the one that would have shipped: **(i)** the screen reached for a `displayName(alias, name, npub)` and a `listContacts()` that do not exist (the real helpers are `peerTitle` and `contacts()`, and `peerTitle` already *is* the alias → published-handle → short-key order, so it was reused rather than reimplemented); **(ii)** `task_list.mjs` read `assignedByMe`/`mineToDo` when **Tauri sends serde snake_case**, which would have shown every row with no buttons at all and failed silently. uniffi does generate camelCase, so the two frontends legitimately differ in spelling while mirroring in behaviour — now stated in the module header, because that is precisely the kind of divergence a reader assumes is a typo. And **(iii)** every desktop button sent the state as `"Done"`/`"Declined"`/`"Withdrawn"` while `karya::TaskState` is `#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]`, so serde would have rejected all three and every control on the new panel would have returned an error. What made it *invisible* is the part worth recording: the mock backend written alongside it did `String(args.taskState).toLowerCase()`, so the panel worked perfectly in the only environment anyone could run it in. A mock more forgiving than the thing it stands in for does not reduce risk, it relocates it past the last place it could be caught — that mock now rejects anything outside `task_list.mjs`'s own `STATES` list, and the action → wire-state mapping moved out of the onclick into a tested `wireState()`, per `.claude/rules/desktop.md`'s rule that a decision in `main.js` belongs in a pure module. Android was never exposed: `setTaskStateTyped` takes the uniffi enum, so there is no string to get wrong. The two subtitle doc-comments were also corrected while here — both claimed the resolver they are handed follows alias → handle → short key, which is true of Android's `peerTitle` but not of desktop's `displayName`, which has no alias to consult; they now state the actual contract, which is "whatever precedence that frontend already uses everywhere else", since a task list naming people differently from the chat list beside it would read as a second address book. **Verification**, all by exit code: `cargo fmt --check` 0, `clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings` 0, `clippy -p comrade_core --features catalogue-http` 0, `cargo test --workspace` 0, `cargo test --features catalogue-http` 0, `node --test desktop/ui/*.test.mjs` **252**, and 0 from the `bridge-event-gate.sh` hook inherited from main (this branch adds no `BridgeEvent` variant, so its count matches). CI confirmed all three workflows green on the fix commit (`aef6bca`) before the merge was started, and green again on the merge itself (`779212d`), per the one-push-one-green discipline this entry exists to justify. **Not verified, and one correction to the entry below.** No `kotlinc` and no Android SDK, so `TaskListScreen.kt`, `TaskList.kt` and `TaskListTest.kt` are signature-checked against generated bindings, not compiled — and nobody has tapped Done on a real device. **And `desktop/src-tauri` clippy has never run in this container at all**: it exits 101 before compiling anything, because the crate needs GTK/webview development headers and `gdk-3.0.pc` is not installed. The entry below says "`desktop/src-tauri` clippy clean" and that claim was never true of any local run — the lane is real and it does pass, but it passes *in CI* (the `Desktop — Tauri shell clippy` job), which is the only place it has ever been executed for this branch. Recorded here rather than quietly deleted, because the failure is the same one this entry opens with: a lane reported as checked when the check could not have produced that answer. The container's capability gap is now in `CLAUDE.md`'s sandbox list, so the next session does not rediscover it by making the same claim.

- **2026-08-04 (owner, four in-chat actions, and two of them needed a boundary drawn):** *"in chat actions (play something together e.g /spotify Kun Faya kun), invoke tara (@tara what does she @xyz thinking of herself), assign tasks e.g (/task get some work done @xyz) and assign in app actions (/assign @breath). feel free to re label any you think for"* — the composer could only send text, while everything the app can *do* sat behind a screen you had to leave the conversation to find. Four commands landed, plus the grammar behind them (`comrade_core::command`, 43 tests) and one new engine (`comrade_core::karya`, 18). `docs/CHAT_ACTIONS.md` is the design record. Six things are worth recording here rather than leaving in the diff. **(1) The grammar went into Rust, not into three frontends.** The app already had one command parser — `VoiceCommand.kt` — and `/pay`'s regex already existed *four* times (`vault.rs`, the desktop JS, the Dart fake, the CLI) and had drifted. So the composer grammar is one module with one answer, and both shipping frontends call it. What did **not** happen is the tempting corollary: `VoiceCommand.kt` still has its own grammar, because folding it in is a refactor of a tested parser this container cannot run tests for, and doing it blind would have been trading a real risk for a tidiness win. Named as follow-up, not done. **(2) The hard part of a slash grammar is what is *not* a command.** A message swallowed as an unknown command is worse than one that arrives confusing, because the sender believes they sent it — so `20/80 split`, `see /Users/me/notes`, a pasted `/usr/local/bin` and `/Notes` all stay text, by four separate rules with four tests, and a command-shaped-but-unknown token is *reported* rather than sent. **(3) `/assign` was relabelled, and the verb was dropped entirely.** You cannot assign somebody a breath: the breathing screen's own copy says *"you don't have to feel better yet"* and its `strings.xml` carries a comment forbidding claims the app cannot support, so an imperative was the wrong register. `/breathe` opens it here; `/comrade-breathe @ana` offers it to her — the action *is* the command, `comrade-` means "for my comrade", and there is one fewer concept than any verb would have added. The owner specified the received copy and it is used verbatim: *"Your comrade asked you to take a deep breath — they are here for you"*. Two rules now hold every offer line and a test enforces both: it says **what the sender did, never what the reader is** (the app has no idea whether anybody is anxious), and the tail is **the sender's own claim**, which is the one reassuring thing this app is actually in a position to pass on. Gated comrades-only and behind the **shared** `nudge::nudged_recently` cooldown — the same floor-on-notifications argument the entry above records for the breathing screen's trigger — and `offer_action` returns an **`OfferOutcomeDto`** rather than a count, because a count could not tell "the cooldown is running" from "that person is not your comrade" and both frontends said the first for both — naming a cause that was not real and never suggesting the fix. It deliberately does not reuse the `comrade_nudge` envelope, whose guarantee is that it carries no reason at all. **(4) The owner's own Tara example is the case the guard exists for, and this is the one place the request was not implemented as literally worded.** *"@tara what does she @xyz thinking of herself"* asks the companion to characterise a named third party. `tara::mentions_third_party` now sits in front of the engine where `detect_distress` sits, and an aside naming a person gets the askable question back rather than an answer — *"I can't tell you what someone else thinks or feels — I'd only be making it up, and it wouldn't be fair to them. What I can stay with is your side of it: what's coming up for you about @xyz?"*. Two reasons, both from `docs/TARA.md`'s non-negotiable gates: inferring what a real person thinks of themselves is a psychological assessment of somebody who has not consented, is not in the room and cannot correct it (gate 1); and `ReflectiveCompanion` is a **cue-word template matcher**, so asked about a third party it would emit a fluent, confident sentence containing no information, about a human being the reader knows. The owner was asked and chose "guard, plus a reframe hint", which is what shipped. The rule is deliberately blunt — *any* aside naming a person turns around, with no cue list deciding which questions count as assessments, because a fuzzy matcher guarding a hard boundary fails on exactly the cases that matter. Bluntness costs almost nothing: the redirect is also the right reflective move, so the safety property and the product behaviour are the same sentence. `detect_distress` still runs first, and a test pins that ordering. The aside itself is seeded with **only what the user typed** — never the chat history or the peer's messages, which would make the other person an unconsenting participant. **(5) Audio acquisition: the owner asked for the Antra chain including acquisition, and one mechanism was declined.** `comrade_core::catalogue` builds the identity half (a `CatalogueResolver`, defaulting to MusicBrainz — chosen because it needs no API key, so there is no credential to ship in a binary or have revoked out from under every install) and a real four-tier acquisition ladder (`choose_audio_plan`): **this device → the person you are talking to (`share`) → an archive whose licence permits it → embed-only**. What is absent, and absent by design rather than unbuilt, is Antra's last two tiers: DRM-protected service streams (defeating a technological protection measure is a liability *distinct* from infringement — DMCA §1201, EU InfoSoc Art. 6, India's Copyright Act §65A) and its mirrors/Soulseek (unlicensed redistribution, and a Comrade running a mirror would be the backend `share` exists to avoid). `docs/TOGETHER.md` §9 had already ruled both out; this records that the code matches, with the exit condition stated — a licensing arrangement or a source serving unencrypted audio under terms permitting redistribution, **not** a better downloader. The concrete outcome for the owner's own example is worth stating: `/spotify Kun Faya Kun` resolves identity through the catalogue, then finds it in your library, receives it from the peer, or plays the YouTube embed. It works; it just does not take the DRM path. The licence check runs **before** the fetch (a tier that downloaded first and inspected afterwards would already have made the copy it was deciding about) and lives in `choose_audio_plan` rather than in the adapter, so a sloppy or hostile resolver returning an all-rights-reserved URL changes nothing — pinned by `a_catalogue_that_offers_licensed_audio_cannot_smuggle_it_past_the_gate`. **(6) Zero new `BridgeEvent` variants, and that constraint shaped the whole design.** A variant would have meant an exhaustive Kotlin `when`, *two* exhaustive Dart switches, and regenerating `frb_generated.rs` — which `flutter_rust_bridge_codegen` was not available to do here, so half of it would have been hand-edited, against this repo's own rule that the file is generated and never edited. So a task and an offer ride marker-keyed JSON envelopes like every other control payload, **one message each**, sent through a new `send_control_envelope` rather than `send_dm` — the latter is the *chat* path, and putting an envelope through it put raw JSON in the **sender's own** thread and chat-list preview, the same defect the 2026-07-29 media entry records. The receiver renders its own chat bubble from the envelope through the existing `IncomingDirectMessage`, and both dispatcher arms run `is_cross_transport_duplicate` on the envelope bytes first: reusing the real event id only catches a *same-transport* redelivery, and the mesh and relay copies of one message carry different ids, so without that check every offer taking both routes raised two bubbles. The bubble is stable English that `parse_task_line`/`parse_offer_line` read back, and each frontend localises from the parsed action — if the wire format were the human sentence, translating it would stop two phones in different languages reading each other's threads. Tasks additionally learned a product rule: **a task is a request, not a write to somebody else's list.** An assignment arrives `Open` and can be **declined** as a first-class outcome; an assigner may withdraw but never complete; nothing reopens (a returning task is a new task, because "done, no wait, not done" is an argument two devices would have to arbitrate and neither has standing to win); and an assignment is accepted only from an already-accepted conversation, because a stranger who can push rows onto your list has been handed a harassment channel. Task ids are minted by the assigner rather than derived from the DM's event id — the first idea, and wrong, because the assigner does not know that id until the relay accepts the send and the two sides see different ids anyway. **Verification**, on a container where `rustup update stable` was run first (1.94.1 → **1.97.1**, which is exactly the trap `CLAUDE.md` records — a clean `-D warnings` on the image's older clippy is not proof of a clean one in CI, so every lane below was re-run on the newer compiler): `cargo fmt --all --check` clean, `clippy --workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings` clean, `cargo test --workspace --locked` green — **comrade_core 456** (88 new: `command` 44, `karya` 21, `catalogue` 15, `tara` 8), **comrade_storage 51** (3 new: the round trip, ciphertext-at-rest, and that panic-wipe reaches the new tree), **comrade_ui 158 lib** (14 new) **+ 16 two-peer** (4 new, and they are the ones that matter — they drive a real assignment over an in-process relay to a second real identity, prove both devices key the same id, prove "done" comes back to the assigner, prove a stranger cannot write to the list, prove an offer arrives as a readable line with no JSON in it, and prove one offer raises exactly one bubble). `cargo test -p comrade_core --features catalogue-http` **462**, with a **new CI lane** for it plus a clippy lane, because a guard behind a feature with no lane never runs — and the three guards that lane's comment names (HTTPS-only, the read cap, no redirects) each have a test now rather than only the first. `cargo test -p comrade_core -p comrade_ui --features comrade_ui/media-http` still green. `desktop/src-tauri` clippy clean. `node --test desktop/ui/*.test.mjs` **195** (28 new). `Cargo.lock` is unchanged: the first cut of the catalogue fetch reached for reqwest's `stream` feature and `futures-util`, and switching to the inherent `resp.chunk()` that `media::fetch_and_decrypt_media` already uses removed both. **A review pass caught six real defects after the first green run, and they are worth listing because three were comments asserting guarantees the code did not provide** — the failure mode `CLAUDE.md` names explicitly. **(i)** Both envelopes were sent through `send_dm`, the *chat* path, so a `/task` put raw JSON in the **sender's own** thread and chat-list preview and queued it in the outbox; every other control envelope in that file already used `vault.send_dm` and this now does too, through a `send_control_envelope` whose doc states the cost (no outbox retry). **(ii)** An offer arriving over both transports raised **two** bubbles: `deliver_synthetic_line` deduped on the event id, and the mesh and relay copies carry different ones — the comment claimed that check was sufficient, which it was not. Both arms now run `is_cross_transport_duplicate` on the envelope bytes first, with a two-peer test. **(iii)** `AppAction::key()` returned `"breath"` while serde's `rename_all` produced `"breathe"`, giving one action two strings; the desktop label map keyed on the first and the bridge sent the second, so `/breathe` said *"breathe is on the phone app"*. The two are now identical and a test pins them together, plus one that catches a label falling through to its raw key — the original assertion was `/breath/i`, which passed for both spellings. **(iv)** `offer_action`'s bare count could not distinguish the cooldown from "not a comrade", and both frontends said the former for both. **(v)** A note to self could not be **withdrawn** — `party` reports `Assignee` for a solitary task and `may_transition` reserves withdraw for an assigner — while `karya`'s own table said it could. Deleting your own private note now works. **(vi)** Both frontends fell **open** when the grammar was unreachable: a thrown FFI call on Android, and on desktop a `chatCommands` left null by a failed dynamic import, handed `@tara i can't stand my brother` to `send_dm`. Both now fail closed, which is what the comments above them already claimed. Two smaller ones: the Android `/task` confirmation was erased by the line after it was set, and `ChatCommands.kt`'s KDoc claimed `VoiceCommand` already delegates to core when it does not.

**Not verified, and stated rather than implied:** no Android SDK and no Flutter in this container, so `ChatCommands.kt` (and its 22 JVM cases, which mirror the desktop vectors) and every `ChatsScreen.kt`/`ComradeCore.kt` edit are **reasoned from the code and never compiled** — CI is their first build, and nobody has typed a command into a running app on any platform. And the honest feature gaps, all listed in `docs/CHAT_ACTIONS.md` §7: **there is no task list screen on either frontend**, so a `/task` with no `@who` is stored, reachable over FFI, and rendered by nothing — the largest gap and the next thing to build; `/play` parses and resolves but is not joined to Android's existing `TogetherManager`; `/breathe` names its tab instead of navigating, because `ConversationScreen` owns no nav state; a received offer renders its line but has no button; and the catalogue adapter has never made a live request.

- **2026-07-31 (media sharing was down, and the reason was a single dead host):** A field report — "still not able to share any media" — with a screenshot of the shipped Compose app reading `Send media failed: v1=http error: error sending request for url (https://cdn.hackers.town/upload)`. Three separate defects, one of them total. **(1) The upload host was one hard-coded string** (`DEFAULT_BLOSSOM_SERVER = "https://cdn.hackers.town"`) and it had stopped completing TCP connections. `curl -m 12` to it returns nothing at all (exit 28, no HTTP status), which is exactly the transport error the screenshot shows — so *every* attachment, on *every* frontend, had been failing for as long as that host had been down, with no way for a user to route around it. It is now a list — `DEFAULT_BLOSSOM_SERVERS` — tried in order, and the ordering is measured rather than guessed: `examples/blossom_probe.rs` performs a real signed BUD-01 upload to each host and fetches the blob back, and reported `nostr.download` **accepting anonymous uploads with a byte-identical round trip**, `blossom.band` rejecting us (415 for opaque bytes, 400 for a declared type) and `cdn.satellite.earth` answering 401. The two that reject us stay in the list as fallbacks — they cost nothing while the first host works, and being down to one host is precisely the condition that caused this. **(2) There were no timeouts anywhere in the media stack.** `reqwest`'s default is to wait forever, so the failure mode above could equally have been a spinner that never resolved; upload and download now use a 10-second connect budget (a dead host must cost seconds, not minutes, before the next is tried) and a 120-second transfer budget (a 10 MB attachment on a phone uplink is slow, but not unbounded). **(3) The upload contradicted its own privacy comment.** The line above it read "upload ciphertext only — the host sees opaque bytes", and then it sent the *plaintext's* MIME type as the request's `Content-Type` — telling the media host whether the user had just sent a photo, a voice note or a PDF, about a body that is AES-GCM ciphertext and could not be any of them. Uploads now declare `application/octet-stream`; the real type never leaves the E2E envelope (the NIP-94 event that carries it is built for its id and never published). Separately, the `v1=` in that error message is uniffi's rendering of a variant's *field names*, which had been reaching users verbatim for every error in the Compose app — `ComradeCore.rethrowing` now goes through a `UiException.humanMessage()` that mirrors the Flutter app's `describeUiError`, so both frontends word the same failure the same way. **Verification:** `cargo fmt`/`clippy --workspace --all-targets`/`cargo test --workspace` green, including three new hermetic tests (the scalar default cannot drift from the list; a misconfigured uploader falls back to the defaults instead of becoming unable to send; a failover names *every* host it tried, exercised against refused loopback ports so it needs no network). The end-to-end proof is the probe example, which is deliberately **not** a test: it needs the internet, and a CI job that fails when someone else's server is down is a job people learn to ignore. **Not fixed here, and named as the follow-up:** the host list is still not user-configurable. Today's measurement is one day's measurement, and the durable answer is a settings field like the TURN one — the difference between "broken until the next release" and "change the host". It needs a new bridge call on both ABIs and UI in three frontends, which is a feature rather than an outage fix.

- **2026-07-29 (media sharing + screenshots):** Two owner asks, one pass. **(A) Media sharing had a real data-loss bug**, of the same shape as the `send_dm` one the outbox fixed: `upload_and_send_media` encrypted the file, uploaded the blob, persisted the local `MediaRef`, and *then* published the reference DM — and if a relay refused that DM it returned `Err` with the ref already stored. The sender saw a failure **and** saw the attachment in their own thread; the recipient never learned the blob existed; nothing was left to retry from, and the upload was paid for. The reference is ordinary DM content, so it now queues in the same `dak::outbox` as text, keyed by its NIP-94 event id, and the flush loop delivers it when a relay comes back. Two details are load-bearing: the flush loop must **not** re-key a media envelope into a stored message (`rekey_stored_message` would create a text row whose body is the envelope JSON — a chat bubble full of machine-readable noise, and the chat list's preview), and the reaping pass captures each queued item's peer *before* pruning, because a media reference has no stored row to look its conversation up from. Second bug, also visible: `conversations()` and `message_requests()` derived a thread's newest item from stored *messages* only, so a conversation whose only content was an attachment **did not appear in the chat list at all**, and a thread whose newest item was a photo showed a stale text line. Both now fold the media tree in with a `📎 …` preview. Third, hardening: the caller's MIME type is validated and lower-cased (a peer sending `IMAGE/JPEG` used to miss every frontend's `startsWith("image/")` and render as an unopenable file), captions are stripped of control characters and bounded, and an empty payload is refused *before* the upload rather than after it. **(B) Screenshots are now allowed across the app by default**, reversing M1-6's window-wide `FLAG_SECURE`. The justification in the code was "screens can display key material"; checked against both frontends, **no screen renders a secret key** — what is shown is an npub, which is public by construction and printed next to "names repeat, keys don't". So the flag blocked ordinary uses (screenshot a plan, keep a journal entry, attach a picture to a bug report) to protect something that was not there, while stopping no realistic threat: anyone holding the phone can photograph the screen. Blocking is now a user setting, off by default, stored once in `mullu.comrade.ScreenSecurity` and read by *both* frontends so they cannot disagree — deliberately outside the encrypted store, because the flag must be right for the first frame, before any unlock. The scoped mechanism (`setSecureWhileVisible`, reference counted; Dart's `SecureScreen`) ships in exactly one place: the vault passphrase field **while the user has revealed it**, which is the one genuinely secret thing on screen anywhere in the app. **(C) The Flutter app's media seams are no longer stubs.** New `mullu.comrade/media` channel: `ACTION_OPEN_DOCUMENT` picker, camera photo/video, audio playback via a `MediaDataSource` over the decrypted bytes, and "open in another app" via a new `InMemoryMediaProvider` that serves a *seekable* descriptor from memory (`StorageManager.openProxyFileDescriptor`, API 26+ — a pipe would fail the PDF readers and players this exists for). The Compose app had to write decrypted media to `cacheDir/media` for `MediaPlayer`/`VideoView`/`ACTION_VIEW` and pay for it with a purge that a missed call site turns into plaintext on disk (S-4); the unified app now has **no disk plaintext path at all** — the one unavoidable file, a camera capture, is read once and deleted in a `finally`. Received video, which reported "not wired up" forever, is handed to the system player from memory; a real in-app video player is still unbuilt and is named as such. Voice notes come back (D13 reversed in substance, not in gesture: tap-to-record, not press-and-hold, because the gesture was what had no meaning with a mouse). **(D) The composer is Telegram's**, per the owner: emoji inside the field on the left, paper clip inside it on the right, and one round button outside that is Send whenever there is text and otherwise the swappable mic/camera. Every control is capability-gated from a single startup probe, so a device with no camera app never shows a camera button. The emoji picker is hand-rolled (~220 lines, no new dependency — every pub alternative ships a font or drags in `shared_preferences`) and inserts at the caret, not at the end. **(E) Desktop leak**: the SPA minted an object URL per viewed attachment and never revoked one, so every decrypted attachment stayed in the webview heap for the process's life; now a bounded, revocable LRU, cleared on unlock. **Verification honesty:** `cargo fmt --check` and `clippy --all-targets` clean; `cargo test --workspace --locked` green (393 passed, 1 ignored), of which `comrade_ui`'s lib is **97** (the new cases cover the queue-on-refusal path, the envelope that must never become a chat bubble, an expired reference reported against its conversation, the chat-list/request previews, MIME normalisation and caption sanitising). On the Flutter side a stable SDK was fetched for this pass, so for the first time these claims are *run*, not reasoned: `dart format` byte-identical to CI's, `flutter analyze --fatal-infos` **no issues**, `flutter test` **169 passed / 4 skipped** (was 104). `node --test desktop/ui/*.test.mjs` is **60** (was 54) — the six new ones pin the object-URL cache's eviction and revocation, which is the leak fix. **The Kotlin half is compiled** — an SDK, NDK 28.2.13676358, cargo-ndk v4 and both Android Rust targets were installed for this pass, and `flutter build apk --debug` completes: a 189 MB debug APK carrying `libcomrade_jni.so` for arm64-v8a (13.5 MB) and x86_64 (15.2 MB). It took two fixes to get there, both in the new provider and both worth recording because a review had missed them: `uri.lastPathSegment` (a `String?`) was being handed to `ConcurrentHashMap.remove`, whose key is non-null; and a `MatrixCursor` row built with `map { … }.toTypedArray()` inferred its element type to the intersection `Comparable<*> & Serializable`, which Kotlin 2.x will not reify. After those, one `e:` and zero `w:` across the whole module. **What is still unverified is everything at runtime**: no device, no emulator, so not one attachment has been picked, played or opened out, and `FLAG_SECURE` has never been applied to a real window. `desktop/src-tauri` remains uncompilable here (no GTK/webkit2gtk), so the SPA change has `node --check` and its own unit tests behind it and no rendering behind it at all.

- **2026-07-10 (owner):** Proceed on the current Rust stack; framework maturity risk (sled 0.34, yrs pre-1.0, libp2p/nostr-sdk churn) is *accepted* on the assumption these mature over time. Consequences: D1's sled migration is **out of scope** (document in SECURITY.md instead); unmaintained transitive-dep advisories from these frameworks are ignored with reasons + exit conditions in `deny.toml`; the hickory-proto DNS advisories wait on a libp2p release against hickory ≥ 0.26.
- **2026-07-10:** M0 executed (wrapper, CI lanes, cargo-deny gate, desktop lockfile, change_pin crash-safety regression test) plus M1 quick wins (M1-3 README truth pass, M1-6 backup/FLAG_SECURE/nsec masking, M1-7 checksum logic — pin pending network access, M1-8 CSP). MSRV measured from the lock: **rustc ≥ 1.88** (supersedes the sweep's 1.83 estimate in N3).
- **2026-07-12:** Field report: two fresh devices could not find each other by @handle. Root cause: the one-shot Kind-0 publish raced the relay dials and was never retried (fixed — bounded connect-wait, retry with backoff, republish on every launch), and search fanned the NIP-50 filter across non-search relays (fixed — dedicated search relays, client-side match filter, direct npub lookup). Chat UI now titles peers alias → published @handle → key, with a per-contact alias editor. Session-android feature parity adopted as the communication roadmap (§7); parity is a direction, not a claim.
- **2026-07-16:** Voice-model availability reworked (closes M1-7/S10, fixes a latent bundled-model bug). (a) The fetch script's sha256 pin is now committed — computed from a mirror download whose sha512 also matches Alpine aports' pin of the same `alphacephei.com` zip (two independent sources, same bytes). (b) Root-caused the field report "Voice unavailable: model-en-us/uuid": vosk-android's `StorageService` requires an `assets/model-en-us/uuid` marker the fetch script never wrote — so even model-baked APKs failed, and CI APKs (which never run the script) shipped no model at all. The script now writes the marker (and heals an existing staging). (c) APKs without a bundled model now offer a Google-style on-device download prompt on the first voice tap (Settings tap-to-talk / "Hey Comrade" enable, journal dictation): one-time ~40 MB fetch with progress/cancel/retry, sha256-verified against the same pin, staged then atomically moved under `filesDir/voice-model`, picked up by every recogniser via the shared `VoskModel` holder (the wake-word service now uses it too instead of a private unpack). The shared model is reference-counted and closes ~30 s after the last recogniser releases it, so turning voice off reclaims its RAM; it stays resident only while the wake word is enabled or a dictation is in flight.
- **2026-07-12 (owner, supersedes the 2026-07-10 entry above):** D1's sled migration is back in scope and has landed — `comrade_storage` now persists to `redb` instead of `sled`, keeping the identical Argon2id + AES-256-GCM envelope. `EncryptedStore::open` transparently migrates a pre-existing sled store in place on first open (decrypt under the caller's PIN, re-ingest into a fresh redb file, archive the old sled files under `sled-archive/` inside the same directory) — no caller (CLI, JNI, Tauri) changes. `sled` stays a dependency *only* for that one-time migration reader (`comrade_storage::migrate`); it is no longer used for the live store, so `deny.toml`'s sled-transitive-dep ignores now carry an updated exit condition (remove once the migration reader itself is retired). A useful side effect: because redb's write transactions are atomic, `change_pin` now runs the whole rekey in a single transaction and is genuinely crash-safe — this closes finding **S2** for real (the regression test that used to be `#[ignore]`d as a known-bug xfail now passes unconditionally); M1-2 is done.
- **2026-07-12 (owner):** M1-1 lands as full NIP-17/NIP-59 gift-wrap, not plain NIP-44-in-Kind-4 — resolving OQ4 in favor of metadata privacy over interop with older plain-NIP-44 clients. `VaultEngine::send_dm_reply` builds every new DM via `EventBuilder::private_msg` (Kind-14 rumor → Kind-13 seal → Kind-1059 gift wrap, signed by a one-time key); the inbox subscription now takes both `Kind::GiftWrap` and legacy `Kind::EncryptedDirectMessage`, decrypting each with the appropriate path, with the `since()` window widened by NIP-59's 2-day timestamp randomization so a fresh gift wrap can never be dropped as "too old." Separately, `comrade_core::call::IceStrategy` adds a STUN-first, TURN-on-failure policy (`ice_servers_for`) — `place_call`'s initial offer now asks for STUN only; a new `ComradeRuntime::call_ice_servers_for("stun_and_turn")` exists for a frontend to call once it detects its `RTCPeerConnection` can't connect (the CGNAT case) and restart ICE with the relay included. The ICE fallback is engine-level and tested but **not yet called by either frontend** — flagged in §8.1, not claimed as end-to-end wired, per Theme 1 discipline.
- **2026-07-15:** Six connectivity/calling tickets (COMMS-01–06, referenced by that tag throughout the touched code) close gaps this document already named — §8.1's "always-on background delivery needs a connection service" note, OQ10's TURN question, §3.3's unbounded-feed-subscription finding, and the calls signaling layer's untested call-setup races. **COMMS-01** (background connectivity): `GlobalScope` is gone from `ComradeCore`'s class initializer; `initializeEventBridge()` is now a `suspend fun` awaited by `ComradeApplication.appScope` at startup and, idempotently, by `unlockVaultTyped`, closing a race where an event published before a listener subscribed was silently dropped (a zero-receiver `tokio::sync::broadcast` send is a no-op). A new opt-in `RelayConnectionService` (foreground, `dataSync` type, default-on toggle in Settings) becomes the sole consumer of the event queue once the vault unlocks, and stops on lock/logout; `ChatEventRouter`, not `MainActivity`, now owns notification routing, so an Activity recreation can never double-register a listener or double-notify. The security boundary is documented, not oversold: this covers backgrounded-but-*unlocked* only — process death still drops the in-memory vault key, and there is still no push-wakeup path for an actually-dead process (a push token is a real metadata tradeoff for a privacy-first app; deliberately out of scope). **COMMS-02** (TURN): `comrade_core::call` gains `validate_turn_url` (rejects anything that isn't a well-formed `turn:`/`turns:` URI before it's persisted) and `mint_turn_rest_credentials` (coturn's `use-auth-secret` REST mode — HMAC-SHA1 username/password, time-limited; cross-checked against an independently computed Python HMAC in tests). `deploy/coturn/` ships a TLS coturn compose file + config + README for an operator who wants to self-host — the "self-hosted coturn" option OQ10 already named, now turnkey rather than theoretical; Comrade still ships no credential-broker service of its own (no account server to host one on), so an operator mints credentials via the tested function or the README's `openssl` one-liner. Settings' TURN card never reads back a saved credential (write-only, matching the existing nsec-masking discipline) and adds an honest "Test relay connectivity" diagnostic (`CallManager.testTurnConnectivity`, a throwaway RELAY-only `PeerConnection`) reporting no-server/relay-available/relay-unavailable instead of hanging. **COMMS-03** (two-peer test harness): a hermetic in-process Nostr relay (`crates/comrade_ui/tests/support`) backs a new `two_peer_integration.rs` suite proving request-gating, DM delivery, and the full offer/answer/ICE/hangup/call-log path between two real `ComradeRuntime`s — no Docker, runs under plain `cargo test`. `deploy/test-relay/` (nostr-rs-relay) plus a new `TwoPeerJniIntegrationTest` cover the same ground across the real Android JNI boundary when a relay URL is supplied via instrumentation args (the test skips, rather than silently falling back to the public relay pool, when it isn't). Scope note: the ticket's "two-installation" device harness is implemented as a lower-risk `deviceHarnessRole` Gradle property (a conditional `applicationIdSuffix`) rather than a full product-flavor split, specifically to avoid renaming every Gradle task `android-apk.yml`/`release.yml` invoke — a genuine reduction from the literal ask, left as follow-up if a fuller two-APK harness is wanted. **COMMS-04** (bounded event queue): `ComradeCore`'s unbounded `ConcurrentLinkedQueue` is replaced by `EventBus`, a three-tier priority queue — critical (calls/DMs/requests: never dropped), coalesced (mesh/ledger/profile/receipt status: latest-per-key only), feed (bounded, oldest-dropped) — with live depth/drop/coalesce/lag counters exposed as a `StateFlow`. `SabhaEngine`'s public-feed subscription moves from an unconditional relay-wide firehose to `FeedFilterSpec` (followed-authors scope once any contact exists, a bounded global bootstrap window otherwise), closing part of the executive summary's "scope the Sabha feed" opportunity. A `#[ignore]`d load test (`feed_flood_load.rs`, ~1000 concurrent public events against a live DM) is wired into CI as its own required `load-test` job. **COMMS-05** (call-setup races): `CallManager.startOutgoingCall` now builds its `Session` synchronously, before `placeCallTyped`'s async round-trip begins — closing a real bug where `hangup()` called during that window found `session == null`, silently no-opped, and let the delayed continuation send an offer after the UI had already gone back to idle; `endWith` now also cancels the in-flight `placingJob`. Reducer-style lifecycle tests live in a new instrumented `CallManagerLifecycleTest` (cancel-before-session, place failure, late-callback-after-cancel, simultaneous-offer-during-provisional-phase), since they need a real `PeerConnectionFactory`/JNI boundary a JVM unit test can't load. **COMMS-06** (Bluetooth routing): `BLUETOOTH_CONNECT` (API 31+) is requested only when the user explicitly selects the Bluetooth route in `CallScreen`'s `AudioRouteButton`; denial calls a new `CallManager.onBluetoothPermissionDenied()`, which drops Bluetooth from `availableRoutes` for the rest of the call and explains the speaker fallback via a toast instead of silently doing nothing. **Verification honesty**: every Rust change is covered by `cargo fmt --check` / `clippy --all-targets -D warnings` / `cargo test` (workspace, plus the `media-http` feature and the load test) — all green. Every Kotlin/Android change (all six tickets touch it) and the `desktop/src-tauri` event-forwarding update are **not compiled in this environment** (no Android SDK; the Tauri crate needs system GTK/webkit2gtk libs this sandbox lacks) — they're reasoned from, and kept structurally close to, already-proven patterns in the same files, but should be treated as unverified until the repo's own CI (`android-apk.yml`'s `connectedDebugAndroidTest`, the `desktop` clippy lane) runs them for the first time. `deploy/coturn/` and `deploy/test-relay/` are likewise unverified — no Docker daemon was available while authoring them.
- **2026-07-15 (cont'd):** Idempotency + lock-discipline pass, closing gaps the COMMS entry above left open. **Call-signal idempotency**: relay at-least-once delivery plus the 2-day gift-wrap backfill window meant a redelivered/backfilled call signal used to replay as if new — `dispatch_incoming_dm` (`comrade_ui::runtime`) now drops any call envelope older than 90s (`CALL_SIGNAL_MAX_AGE_SECS`) and dedupes by wrapper event id via a small hand-rolled bounded LRU (`CallSignalDedup`, cap 512, no new dependency); `CallSignalDto` gained `created_at` (the signal's true send time). Android's `CallManager` mirrors this at the WebRTC layer: a duplicate `Answer` is dropped unless `signalingState == HAVE_LOCAL_OFFER` (`decideAnswer`), a same-call duplicate `Offer` received again pre-accept is a no-op instead of falling into the busy-auto-reject branch (`decideOfferForExistingSession`), and a bounded `endedCallIds` set (cap 32) drops a redelivered terminal `Offer` instead of re-ringing — all three extracted as pure, WebRTC-object-free functions with dedicated unit tests. **DM durability**: `dispatch_incoming_dm` now dedupes plain-text/media DMs against what's already persisted before emitting an event or a delivered receipt (persist-then-emit, so a crash in between self-heals as "already seen" next time), and a new `vault_last_seen_at` watermark (existing app-settings tree) widens `VaultEngine::subscribe_inbox_with_callback`'s `since` floor backwards when the device was offline longer than the gift-wrap skew — a message sent 3+ days into an offline stretch is now delivered on the next launch instead of only ever looking back 2 days. **P2 closed for every method that matters**: `send_dm`, `send_dm_reply`, `broadcast_chitthi`, `send_call_signal`, `hangup_call`, `upload_and_send_media`, `download_and_decrypt_media`, `search_profiles`, `sync_ledger`, `sakha_add_entry` now split into a cheap synchronous `RuntimeHandles` snapshot (Arc/Option clones, generalizing the pattern `profile_refresher` already established) plus a network-touching body borrowing nothing from the runtime; both bridges (`desktop/src-tauri/commands.rs`, `comrade_jni`) take the snapshot under a briefly-held guard and run the network call guard-free — proven by a new regression test (an unreachable-relay `send_dm` no longer stalls a concurrent `toggle_workspace`; the test fails without the fix). `pair_sakha` intentionally keeps `&mut self`: `SakhaEngine::pair_with` has no internal network `.await`, so its write guard is already bounded to local CPU work, not a relay round-trip. Android's tap-to-talk (`SettingsScreen.VoiceSection`) no longer dispatches `CommandDispatcher.handle` from the recognizer's main-looper callback either — the other half of this row's "still open" note. **Unbounded vault inbox removed**: `VaultEngine`'s `inbox: Vec<VaultMessage>` field, its push, and the uncalled `subscribe_inbox`/`inbox_snapshot` accessors are deleted (zero external callers, confirmed by a workspace-wide grep first) — the event callback was always the real delivery path. **JNI event forwarder hardened**: `comrade_jni`'s forwarder no longer invokes `BridgeEventListener.on_event` from a `tokio::spawn`ed task (where a Kotlin listener re-entering a sync `blocking_read`/`blocking_write` method would panic); it now pushes onto a bounded (1024), drop-oldest, hand-rolled `Mutex<VecDeque>` queue drained by one dedicated named `std::thread` — documented as an invariant in the crate's module header, and pinned by a test with a listener that calls a `blocking_read` method from inside `on_event`. **Call robustness pack** (Android `CallManager`): a call that reached `Active` and then loses its media path (ICE `FAILED`, or `DISCONNECTED` past a 15s grace) now arms a 20s recovery countdown instead of the callee waiting forever (previously only the caller retried TURN); `setLocalDescription` failure now ends the call the same way `setRemoteDescription` failure already did; simultaneous mutual calls to the same peer ("glare") resolve deterministically by comparing npubs rather than both sides self-`Busy`ing; `PeerConnectionFactory`/`EglBase` init moves off the caller's Compose-click thread into the existing background coroutine; an `OnAudioFocusChangeListener` mutes/restores the local track around a transient focus loss; the always-listening wake-word recognizer now pauses for the mic's duration during a call or a voice-note recording (`WakeWordService.pause`/`resume`); the incoming-call notification and ringing screen resolve the caller's alias/published name instead of the bare key; the calls notification channel is bumped to `comrade_calls_v2` with `setSound(null, null)` so `Ringer` is the only thing that rings (channel settings are sticky, so silencing the old channel id alone would never take effect for existing installs); and a busy-reject call-history row no longer logs `startedAt = 0` (rendered as the 1970 epoch). **Verification honesty**: every Rust change (`comrade_core`, `comrade_ui`, `comrade_jni`, `desktop/src-tauri`) is green under `cargo fmt --check` / `clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings` (workspace + the separate `desktop` clippy lane) / `cargo test --workspace --locked` (plus the `media-http` feature combination and the `--ignored` load test). The Kotlin/Android changes are, like the COMMS entry above, **not compiled in this environment** (same missing Android SDK — `dl.google.com` is blocked by the network proxy) — reasoned from and kept structurally close to proven patterns in the same files, and independently re-read end-to-end, but unverified until CI's `android`/`android-apk.yml` lanes run them for the first time.
- **2026-07-15 (cont'd 2):** Pre-merge independent review of the idempotency pass above caught three real regressions the pass itself introduced, all fixed before merge. **`ensureFactory` race**: moving `PeerConnectionFactory`/`EglBase` init off the main thread (see "Call robustness pack" above) put it in `io.launch` *before* the coroutine takes the `CallManager` monitor, so two overlapping call-setup attempts (e.g. a glare loser whose non-suspend native init can't actually be interrupted by `.cancel()`, racing a new session's own init) could both see `factory == null` and double-initialize; `ensureFactory` now takes the monitor itself (`synchronized(this)`) rather than relying on its callers to already hold it. **Unsynchronized `connectedAtMs` read**: `peerObserver.onConnectionChange` read `s.connectedAtMs` outside any `synchronized` block, the one deviation from this class's documented "all `Session` access goes through the monitor" discipline; now read inside `synchronized(this@CallManager)` before deciding the connection-state action. **Wake-word pause/resume wasn't actually safe to overlap**: `WakeWordService`'s single non-refcounted `listening` boolean (added above) assumed a call and a voice-note recording would never both hold the mic at once, but the call screen doesn't cancel an in-flight recording — a concretely reachable two-touch sequence (hold the record button, place/answer a call, release the button) let the recording's `resume()` restart the recognizer while the call was still `Active`. Replaced with `MicHolderSet`, a tiny pure set-based holder tracker (`acquire`/`release` return whether this was the first/last holder) — `pause`/`resume` now take a `MicHolder` (`CALL`/`VOICE_NOTE`) and the recogniser only actually restarts once every holder has released it; an unmatched `resume()` (e.g. a call that ends before its own `setupPeer` ever paused — `endWith` calls it unconditionally) stays a harmless no-op, same as the old boolean's idempotency guarantee, just correct across concurrent holders now. Unit-tested (`WakeWordServiceTest`, pure JVM, no Android/Vosk dependency). Found by a full independent line-by-line read-through of every changed Kotlin file (necessary since this sandbox still can't compile Android code) plus a targeted sub-check confirming the overlap was actually reachable through the app's real navigation, not just theoretically possible.
- **2026-07-16:** Comms architecture + delivery roadmap authored — see `docs/COMMS_ARCHITECTURE.md` (the working plan for all comms work; owner ratification of its §4 decisions is pending, so this entry records an engineering proposal, not an owner sign-off). Priorities in the owner's requested order — voice-call reliability, then the state layer, messaging completion, and video polish — sequenced into four waves / 18 work packages, with six ADRs. **ADR-1 (state layer):** redb stays the single source of truth — no materialized Rust store (avoids dual-write divergence and lock-boundary teardown hazards); frontends stop tick-driven full refetch and instead consume per-conversation delta events plus a `{created_at,id}`-cursor `messages_with_page` API, adding the missing `OutgoingDirectMessage` `BridgeEvent` variant; the COMMS-04 two-bus split and the P2 `RuntimeHandles` lock rule are preserved unchanged. **ADR-2/3 (calls):** convergence is decision-table-only and desktop-first — no `CallManager` rewrite; desktop gains a pure `call_decisions.mjs` module under a new `node --test` CI lane now, a shared Rust decision module later, with the test vectors as the cross-implementation conformance contract. A **P0 was confirmed by direct read**: desktop `handleIncomingOffer` answers `busy` to *any* offer while `state.call` exists (`desktop/ui/main.js:996-1014`, routed before the `callId` guard at `:1096`), so an Android caller's same-`call_id` TURN re-offer is rejected — cross-platform TURN fallback is broken today; the fix mirrors Android's `decideOfferForExistingSession` (same-`call_id` ⇒ renegotiation, not busy) and needs no wire change (`CallSignal::Offer` has no ice_restart flag). Also confirmed: the desktop caller uses STUN-only with no ICE-restart while the callee uses STUN+TURN, and `call_ice_servers_for`/`call_sas`/`turn_server_status` exist in the runtime but are unregistered in Tauri's `invoke_handler` (`lib.rs:89-144`). **ADR-4:** `ComradeRuntime` becomes the engine-lifecycle owner (Nostr disconnect on Off-Grid, netstat-visible, tested) — the comms-scope closure of **A1**/M2-4 (OQ2 noted as a dependency, not solved). **ADR-5:** local-first metrics only (atomic counters + bounded histograms behind a `metrics_snapshot()` DTO, cleared on lock); no off-device export without an owner decision. Open owner decisions carried forward: **OQ10** (TURN operator; the CI `TURN_*` secrets are still empty — this gates end-to-end validation, not the code), telemetry-export policy, and the plaintext message-index key (WP14) if pursued. Nothing in this entry is shipped work — it records the plan.
- **2026-07-28 (owner direction: "analyze bitchat and take all the good parts"):** A full read of [permissionlesstech/bitchat](https://github.com/permissionlesstech/bitchat) (whitepaper v2.0, ~75k lines of Swift, its `docs/` set and privacy assessment) produced a take/adapt/reject ledger in **`docs/BITCHAT_ADOPTION.md`** and eight new `comrade_core` modules, all unit-tested. **The headline gap it closed is a real bug, not a feature**: `send_dm` used to turn a relay publish failure into an `Err` and the user's text was *gone* — nothing queued, nothing persisted. `comrade_core::dak::outbox` (bitchat whitepaper §6.1) now retains it (100/peer, 24 h TTL, 8 attempts), persists the queue as one more sealed row in the existing store rather than needing bitchat's separate ChaChaPoly seal + Keychain key, retries on a cadence and at every launch, clears on the peer's receipt (peer-scoped, so one peer's ack cannot clear a colliding id queued for another — a bug bitchat had to fix in its own outbox), and marks the message *failed* visibly when it gives up. **`dak::courier`** ports §6.2 whole — day-rotating `HMAC(recipient key, ctx‖UTC day)` tags with ±1-day candidates, trust-tier quotas (a stranger sub-pool that can never crowd out an accepted contact's mail), spray-and-wait budgets that a replayed deposit cannot replenish, a 10-minute speculative-handover cooldown — with sealing rebuilt from Comrade's own primitives (ephemeral-key ECDH for confidentiality so the wire carries no sender identity, plus an inner static-static HMAC that authenticates the sender deniably); its no-forward-secrecy caveat is documented exactly as bitchat documents it, and prekeys are the named follow-up. **`gcs`** is the Golomb-coded-set filter behind §6.3 gossip sync, the primitive the Saathi mesh needs to heal a partition. **`anon`** closes §8's long-standing "anonymous thoughts are actually pseudonymous" gap with bitchat's device-seed HMAC derivation, extended with a per-post ephemeral key so two anonymous Chitthis are unlinkable *to each other*; `sabha` gains anonymous publishing with an hour-coarsened `created_at`. **`geo`** brings location channels including the privacy rules that are the actual design — heartbeats only at ≤ city precision, and `Presence::Unknown` so a UI cannot render a misleading "0 people" where nobody announces. **`pad`** adopts bucket padding and *improves* on it: bitchat's one-byte pad length means any frame needing >255 bytes of padding ships unpadded (its own §9 lists this as future work); a two-byte trailer closes it. **`seen`** replaces the hand-rolled `CallSignalDedup` with one tested LRU/TTL set plus a content normaliser. **`metrics`** adopts identity-free local counters — a closed enum of static keys, so a caller cannot attach a peer or a timestamp even by accident — which also satisfies the org observability standard without building a record of who talked to whom. **Panic wipe** landed in `comrade_storage` (+ `ComradeRuntime::panic_wipe`, + an Android bridge command): where bitchat's release checklist has to remind maintainers that "new persistent stores must add an explicit wipe hook", this enumerates the database's *actual* tables, so a tree added next year is covered by construction — pinned by a test that writes to an invented tree name. Deliberately **not** taken: the BLE flood-control constants and source routing (Comrade's mesh is libp2p Gossipsub, which already does that layer), bitchat's proprietary non-NIP-44 Nostr envelopes (Comrade chose interop on purpose), and its never-rotating 8-byte peer ID — the exposure bitchat itself calls "the weakest part of this design". **Verification honesty:** `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`, and `cargo test --workspace --locked` are green, with 100+ new tests. The courier store, the GCS filters, the geohash channels, and the metrics snapshot are engine-level with **no frontend driving them yet** — 🧪 in the README, not a checkmark (Theme 1 discipline). No Kotlin/Compose or Tauri UI was written in this pass: the new bridge commands (`panic_wipe`, `flush_outbox`, `outbox_pending`, `metrics_snapshot`, `broadcast_anonymous_chitthi`) exist for a UI to call, and the Android/desktop screens are the follow-up.
- **2026-07-29:** **Comrade presence** shipped (owner request: "choose a contact to be your comrade and get notified when they are online"). Design decision recorded here because the obvious implementation was rejected: **NIP-38 (public, replaceable Kind-30315 user status) is not used** — a public status event publishes a minute-by-minute log of when a person holds their phone, to every relay and anyone watching, which gives away more than the message contents gift-wrapping exists to hide. Instead presence is a `comrade_presence` control envelope inside a NIP-44/NIP-17 gift-wrapped DM, addressed to chosen peers only (new `comrade_core::presence`, joining `dm`/`call` in the same marker-per-envelope pattern, so a beacon can never be mistaken for chat text or vice versa).
