Try again"* button, not a lost install. `UpdateDownloadState.Installing` gained `bytesStaged`/`totalBytes`, with `totalBytes == 0` meaning "committed, the platform owns it" the same way `Downloading` already means "size unknown" — so the retry is offered only in the phase where "nothing happened" could be true, never while a bar is moving. **A collision found while adding an id:** `UpdateDownloadService` posted its "download failed" notice to `PROGRESS_ID + 1`, which is `0xC0DE24`, which is what the 2026-08-03 entry gave `UpdateInstaller.INSTALLED_ID` — the two replaced each other in the shade. The arithmetic is why it was invisible: grepping for the number never finds it. Failure id is now written out; `INSTALLED_ID` moved to `0xC0DE27`. **Verification**: `./gradlew testDebugUnitTest` green — **328 JVM unit tests**, 9 new here (`UpdateCheckTest` for the flex-window gate, the reschedule trap, the privacy switch and the retry rule; `UpdateDownloadsTest` for the two phases of `Installing`); debug + release Kotlin, `compileDebugAndroidTestKotlin`, and the Flutter host's `:app:compileDebugKotlin` all compile, which is what proves the two new classes are picked up by `stagePreservedServices` for both hosts. Both *merged* manifests carry `UpdateCheckJob` (with `BIND_JOB_SERVICE`), `UpdateInstallService` and `RECEIVE_BOOT_COMPLETED`. `flutter analyze --fatal-infos` clean, `dart format` clean, `flutter test` **315 passed**, one new — it pins that the hand-off shows a percentage and does *not* offer the retry. **Not verified**: no device again. Nothing here can confirm that a real `JobScheduler` fires this job on a real phone under Doze and battery optimisation, and an OEM that aggressively restricts background work can defer or drop it — the in-app check on foreground is unchanged and remains the floor. The observable test on the next release is a notification arriving without the app having been opened, and an Install tap that shows a moving progress line instead of several silent seconds.

- **2026-08-04 ("implement the android half of large-attachment sending"):** D34 landed the protocol and left the transfer inert, and this is the half that moves bytes — on Android only. **Nothing about moving bytes was written twice.** `together.ShareTransfer` had the whole engine already (receiver-driven chunked transfer over its own `RTCPeerConnection`, flow control against `bufferedAmount`, `RandomAccessFile` assembly, whole-file hash, ICE trickling, the relay policy) welded to a listening session in four places: it signalled through `together_share`, it read the file the player had open, it staged into `cache/together-share/`, and it handed the finished file to `TogetherManager`. Those four are now `FileTransfer.Wiring` (`transfer/FileTransfer.kt`) and everything else moved across unchanged — same 16 KiB chunks, same 256 KiB low-water mark, same `shareChunksToSend` budget re-read inside each batch, same per-chunk `chunkFrameFits`, same STUN-only list then judge-the-chosen-pair. `ShareTransfer` kept its whole public surface, so `TogetherManager` and `TogetherScreen` are untouched and the commit that moved it can be read on its own. One genuine addition: the sender's bytes are a `Source`, not a path, because an attachment is a `content://` URI and copying 400 MB into the cache to give it a path would be a second plaintext copy for no gain (S-4). **The manager owns the transfer, not a screen** (`handoff/AttachmentHandoffManager.kt`, shaped after `TogetherManager` and `CallManager`): a 400 MB send must not die because a thread was navigated away from, and an `Accept` — the thing that authorises the other device to build a peer connection — is only ever sent from a person pressing Receive. **The gate the runtime enforces says the peer is an accepted conversation; it says nothing about the offer being well-formed**, so every peer-chosen field is checked before it reaches a filesystem or a screen: the hash becomes a path only through `HandoffDecisions.stagedFileName`, the Kotlin mirror of `comrade_core::handoff::staged_file_name` with its `../../etc/passwd`, wrong-length and non-hex cases mirrored too; the chunk size must be the 16 KiB both senders use, because the tracker allocates one boolean per chunk and a 1-byte chunk size on a 4 GB offer is a four-billion-entry array — an out-of-memory kill dressed up as a file transfer; the total is checked against `MAX_HANDOFF_BYTES` (64 GiB, and the exit condition is a bitmap tracker) **and** against the free space actually on the device, because failing at 90% wastes the sender's upload as well; and the filename is display text with separators and control characters stripped, never a path. **`attachmentRejection` was wrong for the new road and was not edited.** Its 10 MB is what the core can encrypt in one buffer and what a Blossom operator will take — neither applies device to device — so a second function `peerToPeerAttachmentRejection` mirrors into all three frontends and all three test files (`util/attachment_caption.dart`, `ui/AttachmentCaption.kt`, `attachment_caption.mjs`), refusing only the empty file, which is not a size problem. **What the person is told**: the preview sheet asks `attachment_route_for_bytes` — never a local constant — and names both costs of the direct road before Send (they must be online *now*, since there is no store-and-forward and should not be; and a direct path must exist, which §8.1 puts at 30-40% failing behind CGNAT), a `TransferVerdict` refusal says which network fact it was and that something under 10 MB would work, an offer this device cannot take shows the reason and no Receive button, and dismissing a finished transfer is labelled "Delete it" because that is what it does to the staged plaintext. **Verified** in this container, which does have an Android SDK contrary to `.claude/rules/android.md`: `./gradlew compileDebugKotlin --offline` and `./gradlew test --offline` green (403 tests, 0 failures, of which 20 are new — 17 `HandoffDecisionsTest`, 2 mirrored rejection cases, and the moved `ShareDecisionsTest` still passing in its new package), `node --test desktop/ui/attachment_caption.test.mjs` 21, `cargo fmt`/`clippy -D warnings`/`cargo test --workspace` green on stable 1.97.1 after `rustup update`. **Three gaps are named rather than papered over**: an incoming offer is visible only in the open thread, because there is no notification for one yet; the protocol carries no completion acknowledgement, so the sender's panel shows the transfer as running until they dismiss it while the receiver's says it arrived; and a large send leaves no chat bubble, because there is no NIP-94 event to build one from. **Not verified, and it is the important part**: no emulator and no second device, so not one byte has crossed a data channel, no Compose layout has been rendered, and the `content://` positional-read path is reasoned from the SAF contract rather than exercised against a real provider. Divergence **D34a** in `app/lib/SCREEN_INVENTORY.md`.
- **2026-08-04 (owner, "what are the options to enable sharing of media gt 10mb?" → "proceed with 4"):** Four options were put up; this is the first increment of the one chosen. **The 10 MB cap is not ours to raise.** `MAX_MEDIA_BYTES` appears in four places and every one is a one-line edit, but three things behind it do not move with the constant: encryption is single-shot whole-buffer (`aes256gcm_seal(key, plaintext)`) and download accumulates into one `Vec` (`media.rs:502-514`), the desktop webview path base64s the payload so a 100 MB file is four live copies in a webview, and — decisively — the Blossom host has its own ceiling. Measured rather than assumed: `blossom.band` publishes **20 MiB free / 100 MiB paid** and already rejects this app anyway (415 on opaque bytes, per the 2026-07-31 entry, because it only accepts image/audio/video and we correctly upload ciphertext as `application/octet-stream`); `nostr.download` advertises no `max_byte_size` in its NIP-96 doc at all, which is worse — the real limit is whatever that operator configured today — and its own `content_types` list is `image/*, video/*, audio/*`, so our opaque uploads work only because that list is not enforced on the Blossom endpoint. **So over the cap the file goes peer to peer**, reusing `comrade_core::share` — already chunked, resumable, receiver-driven, integrity-checked, over a WebRTC data channel with nobody in the middle — rather than growing a second copy of it. New in `comrade_core::handoff`: an envelope of its own, because `together`'s share signals ride inside a session envelope and an attachment has no session; a **three-step** sequence (offer → accept → transport) with no `Ask`, because the sender picked the file and nobody has to discover who wants it; `AttachmentHandoff`, carrying the name, MIME type and caption a recipient needs to answer a 400 MB offer *before* a byte moves; and `route_for_bytes`, which makes the question "which road does this file take" rather than "how large may an attachment be" — the threshold is the hosted ceiling itself, not a number of its own, so there is nothing to tune. `Decline` and `Refuse` are separate variants on purpose: a person saying no and a network saying no have different follow-ups, and collapsing them is how a UI tells someone their friend refused when actually their carrier did. **The gate is the one that matters.** A handoff has no session to hide behind, so on receipt it clears `IncomingGate::Accepted` — the same bar a call signal clears, and for the same reason: both end in a peer connection and both make this device gather ICE candidates for whoever is on the other end. Tested three ways (stranger negotiates nothing; an ungated one is dropped rather than surfacing as a message request full of JSON; a together envelope is not mistaken for a handoff). `staged_file_name` is a function with tests rather than an interpolation because the content hash is **peer-supplied and becomes a path** — the same lesson `ShareDecisions.incomingFileName` learned on the Android side. **What is deliberately not done, and must not be mistaken for done:** the data-channel half. It still has to be lifted out of `together.ShareTransfer`, which is hard-wired to a listening session's file and playhead. Both frontends route `BridgeEvent::AttachmentHandoff` and do nothing with it — inert on purpose, so no UI offers a large send that would never complete. Two costs of this road that no amount of work removes and the UI will have to state plainly: the recipient must be **online now** (there is no store-and-forward, and there should not be — the point is that nothing else holds a copy), and a **direct path must exist**, with `RelayPolicy::DirectOnly` the default and `AUDIT.md` §8.1 putting 30-40% of remote pairs behind CGNAT. Divergence **D34** in `app/lib/SCREEN_INVENTORY.md`.

- **2026-08-04 (owner, "implement telegram like media preview before sending media"):** Attaching was one step in all three frontends — a pick, a capture or a document went straight to encrypt-and-upload — so the first sight of what had been chosen was in one's own thread, already delivered, and a wrong pick was unrecoverable. It is now three: refuse, confirm, upload. **(1) Refuse first.** The 10 MB cap and the empty-file case moved *ahead* of the sheet, from one shared message (`attachmentRejection`), because every frontend used to surface the cap only after the upload had begun — discarding the caption the sender had just written. Android additionally refuses from the content provider's own `OpenableColumns.SIZE` where it reports one, so picking a 1 GB video no longer means reading a 1 GB video into memory to learn that it is one (`ui/ChatsScreen.kt` `pickMedia`). **(2) Confirm.** A sheet holds the file itself, the device-chosen filename and its size — the one place that name belongs, since it answers "is this what I meant" and is deliberately *not* what gets sent as the caption — and a caption box seeded from the composer per the existing `captionForAttachment` rule. Nothing is encrypted or uploaded until Send; every way out (Cancel, ✕, scrim, Escape, back gesture) is indistinguishable and leaves the composer's draft exactly as it was. `widgets/attachment_preview.dart`, `ui/AttachmentPreview.kt`, `openAttachmentPreview` in `desktop/ui/main.js`. **(3) What the preview shows is a shared decision, not three guesses** (`attachmentPreviewKind`): a photo as itself, everything else as a card — and a video in a player *only where that player needs no file*. The desktop's blob URL qualifies and really does play; Android's `VideoView` and Flutter's absent plugin would both mean staging an unsent attachment's plaintext to disk, which is exactly what AUDIT S-4 forbids, so they card it. No still frames either: extracting one costs a decoder none of the three carries. **Voice notes are exempt** — the recording strip already holds the clip with a discard button and a send button, so a second confirmation would be one too many; the composer says which it wants via `onAttachment(…, preview:)`. Two things fell out of the restructure. Android now deletes a capture's plaintext at *read* time rather than after the send resolves, so backing out of the preview drops it too — the send-immediately path never had to think about that. And Flutter's paper clip stopped spinning through the sheet: `_guarded` dropped `_busy` before the upload, since a spinner claiming an upload is in flight while the sender is still writing a caption is a lie the old flow could not tell. Even the picture's height bound is shared rather than guessed three times — `attachmentPreviewMediaHeight` is `clamp(120, 52% of the viewport, 420)`, the fraction so the caption box and Send never need a scrollbar on a short window, the cap so a sheet on a large screen still reads as a sheet, the floor so a very short one still shows a recognisable picture. Seven new rules and their tests are mirrored three ways alongside D18b's (`util/attachment_caption.dart`, `ui/AttachmentCaption.kt`, `attachment_caption.mjs`), plus a widget test for the sheet itself; divergence **D18c** in `app/lib/SCREEN_INVENTORY.md`. The desktop path was additionally driven end-to-end in Chromium against a stubbed Tauri backend — seeded caption, Escape leaving the draft intact, an edited caption reaching `send_media_bytes`, an 11 MB file refused before the sheet, and Send still on screen at 900×420.
- **2026-08-04 (owner, from the handset — the minute that never ended):** *"It doesn't seem to be stopping after 1 min which is the default."* **A real bug, and one this ledger argued for.** The entry of 2026-08-03 ("Duration is now the reader's") recorded the clock as deliberately **keeping count past the chosen length** — *"being done is a thing the button says, not a thing that freezes the circle mid-breath"* — and that reasoning is now struck. It was written from the practice framing, where a sit running long is someone continuing; read against the purpose the owner has since stated (anxiety, panic, stress), it is a screen that offered a minute and then quietly declined to honour it, leaving the person who is least able to make decisions right now to decide when to get out. **A duration the screen offers is a promise to stop.** So `BreathingScreen.kt:170` keys the ticker on `complete` rather than `Unit`: reaching the total cancels the effect, and picking a longer chip afterwards restarts it from wherever `elapsed` reached, which is what makes "two more minutes" work on a finished sit without restarting the breath. **Three things had to learn a state the screen could not previously reach.** A sit ends on a cycle boundary, and `breathingPhase` of a boundary is `IN` — so left alone the circle would have gone still under the word *"Breathe in"*, telling someone to draw a breath it had no intention of finishing; hence `breathe_rest` and a `breathe_complete_line` that asks for nothing (*"That is the time you asked for. Stay as long as you like."* — not "come back tomorrow", because the person is still sitting there). The haptic is **cancelled** rather than left to fall silent: its last ramp was handed a four-second span, so expiry would keep a phone going in someone's hand for up to four seconds after the screen said the pause was over. And the circle is snapped to `MIN_SCALE` — already true at a boundary, held explicitly so that moving where a sit may end cannot leave it stopped half-inflated. **A minute is not a whole number of breaths**, which is the arithmetic the old never-stopping design got to ignore. `breathingRunSeconds` rounds to the nearest whole cycle — 1 min → 56s, 2 → 126s, 3 → 182s, 5 → 294s — because stopping on the raw count would freeze the circle part-grown four seconds into a fifth inhale with the buzz mid-ramp, where a boundary ends on an out-breath and a settle. **Nearest, not up:** rounding up would make every chip wrong in the same direction, and for someone who chose one minute *because* they had one minute, overshooting is the worse half. Tests pin that every offered length lands on a boundary, that the last second of a run is `HOLD_EMPTY` (the property the `MIN_SCALE` snap depends on), that no chip drifts more than half a cycle from its own label, and that a zero or negative minute count still gets one whole breath rather than opening on the completed state. `breathingProgress`'s KDoc and its test both lost the "the clock outlives the sit" justification — the clamp survives on the other reason, that shortening the duration moves the total under `elapsed` in one tap. **Verification:** `./gradlew test` **347 JVM tests, 0 failures** (`BreathingScreenTest` 16 → 20 cases, plus the 2-case `BreathingLinePairingTest`), run locally on the tree merged with `main` at b1b4330 — the Android SDK is absent from this container, so this is the Kotlin actually compiling rather than a claim left to CI. Worth recording for whoever hits it next: the **first** post-merge run failed with `Unresolved reference: shortNpub` and an overload ambiguity in `Notifier.kt`/`ChatsScreen.kt`, two files the change never touches — each of those helpers is defined exactly once, in `ui/DisplayName.kt`, so the errors were spurious Kotlin incremental-cache corruption (this container had exhausted its disk earlier, and a half-written cache survives the disk being freed). Deleting `android/app/build` and `android/.gradle` fixed it. The trap is that the symptom points at unrelated source and invites you to "fix" code that is already correct. Rust, desktop and Flutter untouched. **Not verified:** nobody has sat through a stop. Whether ending at 56s reads as "that was my minute" or as being cut short is a body's answer — **The second symptom in the same message — "the same message is still there for both breath in and breath out" — is a stale install, and the release timeline proves it rather than argues it.** Auto-release cuts a patch per merge, so: **v0.0.71** (03:10Z) carried #80, **v0.0.72** (06:37Z) carried #81, and **v0.0.73** (07:09Z) is the *first* release containing #82's paired lines. The report arrived before 07:09Z. In v0.0.71/72 the line came from a single `breathe_lines` array indexed by `LINE_SECONDS = CYCLE_SECONDS * CYCLES_PER_LINE` — **28 seconds, two entire breaths per line** — which is exactly the reported symptom, and those builds also never stopped at a minute, which is the other one. One build explains both. Nothing was changed on that path: `showsInhaleLine` is already tested to flip on `OUT`, and rather than fix code that is correct, the gap the arithmetic tests could never cover is now closed from the copy side — **`BreathingLinePairingTest`** parses `strings.xml` with the JDK's own parser (no Robolectric on this classpath, and the mockable `android.jar` throws from every resources method) and asserts that no pair says the same thing on both halves, that the two arrays stay the same length, and that neither repeats a line within one pass. A selector that picks correctly between two identical strings still shows one sentence for a whole breath; that is now a test failure rather than a handset report.
- **2026-08-04 (owner, from the handset — the motion, not the timing):** *"The animation is not quite smooth while switching messages and transition from breath out and settle is not natural also improve the text animation i.e breathe in -> hold -> breath out -> settle."* Three reports, and **two of them had a cause that was not the thing being looked at.** (1) **The out-breath into the settle was `LinearEasing`, and the comment defending it had the right premise and the wrong conclusion.** It read: "linear, not the default ease: the haptic swells at a constant rate, and a circle that raced ahead and coasted would be pacing a different breath from the one in your hand." Circle and buzz must indeed not drift — but the way to hold them together is to give them **one curve**, not to make both of them straight. A constant rate means the circle reaches empty still travelling at full speed and then stops dead for two seconds, and a breath has no sharp edge where it turns around. Both now read `BreathHaptics.curve` — sinusoidal ease-in-out, `(1 - cos(πf)) / 2`, zero velocity at both ends and fastest in the middle, which is very nearly the airflow profile of real respiration and is why a breath turns around smoothly. Structurally they can no longer diverge: the animation's `Easing` *is* that function, and `amplitudeAt` interpolates through it. A side benefit worth stating because the code now depends on it: the circle is barely moving as it arrives at full or empty, so the `snapTo` both holds perform has almost nothing left to absorb — under linear motion that snap was swallowing whatever fraction of a phase the one-second ticker had drifted, at full velocity. (2) **The message switch was not the fade at all — it was layout.** The lines are not the same length, so some wrap to two rows and some to one, and in a `verticalArrangement = Center` column a line arriving one row shorter pulled the 200dp circle, the progress bar and the duration chips up by a row and dropped them back on the next change. The text was crossfading correctly the whole time; the thing beside it was hopping. `minLines = 2` reserves the taller case, which costs a row of blank space under the short lines and makes the column hold still — **the cheapest fix was in the one place nobody was looking.** The fades were also slowed to 900ms, which the phase lengths have room for. (3) **The phase word was a hard cut**, four times a cycle, dead centre of the only thing being watched, landing at precisely the moment the circle is meant to be gliding to a stop — the most abrupt edge on a screen whose entire premise is that nothing on it arrives abruptly. Now crossfaded at 450ms: slower than a UI transition normally warrants, faster than the sentence above it, because two words must be legible well before the phase they name is under way. **A test had to be added to protect a margin the change quietly ate.** `theInBreathSwellsAndTheOutBreathFades` asserts the amplitude ramp is **strictly** increasing, and an eased curve compresses both ends — at `STEPS = 20` over a 24..120 span the smallest increment is now exactly **1**. It still passes, but with no room left, so `theRampStaysStrictlyMonotonicNowThatItIsEased` fails first and says why: fewer steps or a narrower amplitude span would round two neighbours together, and a swell that repeats a value reads as a stall rather than a rise. The curve itself is pinned for the properties the *visible* motion depends on as much as the felt one — both endpoints exact (an easing that fell short of 0 or 1 would leave the circle short of full at every boundary for the hold to snap away), symmetry about the midpoint (which is what lets one function serve both the rise and the fall), monotonicity and no overshoot across 0..1 (Animatable would clamp an overshoot into a visible stall at the top of the in-breath), and clamping outside 0..1, because Compose does not enforce the easing contract and a rounding error at a phase boundary is exactly where a 1.0000001 comes from. **Verification:** `./gradlew test` **351 JVM tests, 0 failures** (`BreathHapticsTest` 10 → 14 cases), on `main` at ef6cbf1. Rust, desktop and Flutter untouched. **Not verified, and this is the whole point of the change:** nobody has watched it. Whether a sine ease reads as a breath rather than as a drift, and whether 900ms/450ms are calm or sluggish, are a pair of eyes' answers — there is no Android SDK in this container and nothing in CI navigates to this screen, so the compile and the arithmetic are the entire local guarantee.
- **2026-08-04 (the desktop half of a large attachment, and the ceiling it has to admit to):** `comrade_core::handoff` landed the protocol and both frontends routed `BridgeEvent::AttachmentHandoff` into nothing, deliberately, so that no UI offered a large send that could not complete. The desktop now completes it. **The Android half landed the same day, from a parallel session** (the entry above), so the two are worth reading together: one protocol, one refusal vocabulary, and exactly one divergence — where the incoming bytes go, which is `SCREEN_INVENTORY.md` D34a/D34b. **The engine is the one that already shipped**: `share_transfer.mjs`'s pump, framing, tracker and watermarks are untouched, and what was `together`-specific came out of the *driving* code in `main.js` instead — the driver emits abstract steps and a codec in the new `handoff_transfer.mjs` encodes them into either `{"share":…}` (scoped to a session) or `{"handoff":…}` (scoped to a transfer id), receiver requests anchor at a playhead only where there is one, and the finished bytes go to the player or to a Save button. The two codecs sit in one file on purpose: a change to the driver cannot silently serve one encoding and break the other, which is the only way a shipping feature can share a driver with a new one and be *shown* not to have regressed rather than argued not to have. **The sink question has no good answer at 400 MB in a webview, so it is answered honestly and capped.** A browser context has no filesystem; the Tauri side has one, and spooling a `.part` file of decrypted plaintext into the app data directory is exactly what **S-4** forbids — the unified app's whole claim is that it has no disk-plaintext path. So an incoming file is held in memory and the window states a limit instead of pretending it has none: `MAX_HANDOFF_BYTES` = **256 MiB, in both directions**, because `SubtleCrypto` has no streaming digest — the sender must fit the whole file in memory to fingerprint it and the receiver must fit it again to check it, and a 4 GB offer would take the tab down mid-transfer where a refusal arrives in one second. Android is not capped and should not be; it stages to disk under `handoff::staged_file_name`. The exit condition is a Rust-side streaming digest writing into a destination the person picked — a deliberate save rather than a cache, which S-4 permits — and it would lift the sender's half too. Nothing accumulates in the meantime: the chunk views are dropped the instant the blob exists, the object URL is revoked on Dismiss and on unlock, and `endShare` clears `parts` and `file` rather than waiting for the next transfer to overwrite them. **The two costs of this road are stated in the UI, not discovered in it.** The preview sheet asks the core which road a file takes (`attachment_route_for_bytes`, a new Tauri command — the threshold *is* the hosted ceiling, so a frontend with its own 10 MB constant is one that disagrees the day it moves) and says what that road promises: *"encrypted and uploaded, so it reaches them even if they're away"* or *"straight to their device. They have to be online now, and there has to be a direct route between you."* A comrade known to be away blocks the send before the sheet, since there is no store-and-forward here; a peer whose presence this device cannot know gets a warning and not a block, because presence exists only for comrades and refusing on a fact we do not have is worse. When the path verdict refuses (§8.1: CGNAT leaves 30-40% of remote pairs with no direct path) the message now names the way out — anything under 10 MB takes the hosted road, which needs neither of you online at once. **The mirrored trio kept its single home, and the reconciliation is worth recording** because two sessions reached for the same rule at once. `attachmentRejection`'s 10 MB refusal is wrong for the peer-to-peer road but right for the hosted one, and it lives in three files with three test files; this branch therefore did not edit it, and `attachmentSendPlan` decided the road *first*, consulting the shared rule only when the road was hosted — with its own sentence for the empty file. The Android pass then added `peerToPeerAttachmentRejection` to all three mirrors, which is the better answer: the direct road's rules belong beside the hosted road's, in the file whose whole job is that the three frontends refuse in the same words. So on rebase the desktop's own spelling was **deleted** rather than shipped alongside it — `attachmentSendPlan` now asks the mirrored rule for whichever road applies (and its tests assert the refusal *is* that function's output, so moving the message cannot leave the desktop saying the old thing). What stays local is the one rule that must not be mirrored: the 256 MiB ceiling, which is this window's lack of a disk and would wrongly cap the phone. **Every field of an incoming offer is peer-chosen and each is now checked with tests**: the filename is reduced to a last path segment and stripped of control characters and bidi overrides (`holiday<U+202E>gnp.exe` must not read as `holiday.png`), the caption is bounded and stripped because it is drawn verbatim, a `sha256` that is not 64 hex characters refuses the transfer outright (nothing could verify it), and the chunk size is bounded at **both** ends — a 256 MB offer in one-byte chunks asks the tracker for 256 million slots, which is an out-of-memory before a single byte arrives. An offer that fails any of these renders **with no Accept button at all**, because a button that cannot work is worse than no button. Declining sends `Decline` and never `Refuse`. **New gap recorded rather than papered over: A8** — the protocol has no way to say "this device cannot take that file", so a window that is full or already busy sends *nothing* and says so on screen instead of attributing a device limit to the person. **Verification, re-run after rebasing onto `main` at 6f432d7 — which is the Android half, so the two passes are proven to compose rather than assumed to:** `node --test desktop/ui/*.test.mjs` **209** (36 new here, 2 from the Android pass's additions to the shared caption tests), `cargo fmt --check` clean, `clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings` clean, `cargo test --workspace --locked` green (17 test targets, 0 failures). The SPA was then **driven in Chromium** against a stubbed Tauri backend, and twice over a *real* loopback data channel with a second `RTCPeerConnection` in the same page playing the peer: 12 MB out (768/768 chunks, progress moving, "Sent — all of it went across.") and 4 MB in, where a deliberately malformed chunk was refused and the whole-file SHA-256 still matched — which is `chunkFrameFits` doing its job, since a banked short chunk would have failed the hash. That run found two defects reasoning had missed: sender progress stalled one window short of the end (it read 92% on a completed transfer), and the sender had no completion signal at all. **Not verified:** a transfer between two devices. There is no second peer here and a loopback shares one network stack, so it says nothing about ICE across two NATs — the failure §8.1 measures. `desktop/src-tauri` still cannot be compiled in this container (`pkg-config` reports `webkit2gtk-4.1`, `javascriptcoregtk-4.1` and `gdk-3.0` missing), so the two new commands are read, not built, until CI's desktop lane runs them — the one claim on this branch whose first real check is CI.
- **2026-09-01 (the Android profile page, drawn at last — and the two things the page could not honestly claim):** `8033c7f` landed the rules a profile page needs and `0492ad6` drew one on the desktop; Android, the priority frontend, still had only `ui/ProfileView.kt` and no screen to consume it, which is why the 2026-08-04 entry above ends "no profile page has been drawn on any frontend yet". `ui/ProfileScreen.kt` is that screen: a collapsing header whose avatar follows `collapsedAvatarSize`, the action row `actionRow` chose, the info block `infoRows` ordered with the key row last and unconditional (D35), and Media/Files/Links/Voice tabs opening on `initialMediaTab`'s answer. It is reached the way Telegram reaches one — tap the name in a conversation header, or the name and key at the top of the drawer, which opened Settings until there was somewhere better for it to go. **Four rules moved from the desktop module into Kotlin**, because the page needed them and a second copy written at the call site is how a security-relevant rule drifts: `bucketMedia`/`mediaTabCounts` (newest-first within a tab, nothing ever dropped) and `extractLinks`/`hostOf`/`collectLinks` (whitespace split plus a scheme test, deliberately not a regex, with the host returned *separately* so `https://evil.example/login?next=paypal.com` cannot be drawn as a PayPal link — D38). Userinfo is dropped, a port is not the host, an IPv6 literal keeps its brackets, and the text is sanitised before the split so a U+202E inside a URL cannot reorder a host on screen. `ComradeCore` grew the five bindings the page reads — `peerProfileTyped`, `peerAvatarTyped`, `setAboutTyped`, and the remote-avatar switch — and `Profile` widened to carry `about`/`picture`/`avatarCached`, which the Rust DTO has carried since the plumbing landed and nothing on Android read. **D40's off switch is now reachable**: Settings gained "Load profile pictures", which is the control that entry promised and which only started mattering the moment a frontend drew a fetched picture. **Two things the page refuses to pretend.** A blocked peer gets no action row at all and a sentence saying why (D36) — there is no unblock command in the core, and a button that cannot work is the fake switch `SettingsScreen`'s own rule forbids. And nothing on the Links tab opens: it copies, because fetching a sender-chosen URL leaks this device's IP and an implicit read receipt to whoever sent it. **Writing the port found a bug in the original.** `hostOf` sliced at `indexOf("://") + 3` unconditionally, so a string with no scheme lost two characters and the remainder was returned as its host: `example.com/x` answered `ample.com`. Unreachable through `extractLinks`, which tests the scheme first — but the function is exported, and a wrong host is the one output it must never produce. Both ports now refuse a schemeless string, with the case pinned on each side. **Verified here, which is more than this frontend usually gets:** `ProfileViewTest` compiles and *runs* under a plain `kotlinc` + JUnit (51 cases, up from 36 — the file imports only the Kotlin stdlib, which is the whole argument for keeping decision logic on that side), `node --test desktop/ui/*.test.mjs` is 473 (up from 472), and `.claude/scripts/android-typecheck-compose.sh` resolves all 162 sources — main and androidTest — against real Compose 1.6.1, Material3 1.2.0 and the real generated uniffi bindings. **Not verified, stated rather than implied:** nobody has looked at it. There is no Android SDK here, so `./gradlew test` and the emulator leg added to `MainActivityUiTest` (which opens the page from the drawer and waits past a state change, per the early-return bug class Tasks and Ride both shipped) run first in CI; whether the collapsing header reads well, and whether a four-tab strip is right on a phone, are a pair of eyes' answers. Flutter still has the rules and no page, and now also lacks the four functions this entry added to Kotlin — named in `app/lib/SCREEN_INVENTORY.md` as D43 rather than quietly diverged.

---

## 2. Repo Map

**Purpose.** "Comrade" — a privacy-first, cross-platform social client over Nostr, with an off-grid libp2p mesh mode and an encrypted couple's shared ledger. Hindi-derived nomenclature: public post = *Chitthi*, engines = *Sabha* (public feed), *Vault* (E2E DMs), *Saathi* (mesh), *Sakha/Sakhi* (couple CRDT ledger).

**Stack.** Rust 2021 workspace (tokio, nostr-sdk 0.44, libp2p 0.56, yrs 0.21, redb 4, aes-gcm/argon2/secp256k1) · Kotlin + Jetpack Compose Android app with an offline Vosk voice assistant · Tauri 2 desktop shell with a no-build vanilla-JS SPA · interactive CLI harness. ~11k lines total. Built through a series of AI-assisted PRs (milestone-labeled commits), no releases yet.

**Architecture sketch.**

```
                    ┌────────────── frontends ──────────────┐
   CLI (src/main.rs)   Android (Kotlin ⇄ comrade_jni)   Desktop (JS ⇄ Tauri commands)
                    └───────────────┬────────────────────────┘
                          comrade_ui (UiService + ComradeRuntime + event bus)
                    ┌───────────────┴────────────────────────┐
        comrade_core (crypto, sabha, vault, sakha, saathi*, relay*, media*)
        comrade_state (pure workspace state machine)
        comrade_storage (redb + Argon2id + AES-256-GCM)
                                          * = built & tested but never wired to a frontend
```

**Key directories.**

| Path | What it is |
|---|---|
| `crates/comrade_core/` | Protocol engines; the real product logic (crypto, feed, DMs, CRDT ledger, mesh, relay routing, media) |
| `crates/comrade_state/` | I/O-free progressive-disclosure state machine (Base / OffGridTravel / CoupleSandbox) |
| `crates/comrade_storage/` | Encrypted-at-rest KV store + typed repositories; the best-tested crate |
| `crates/comrade_ui/` | Framework-agnostic view-model (`UiService`) + async bridge orchestrator (`ComradeRuntime`) |
| `crates/comrade_jni/` | Android FFI boundary — uniffi-generated Kotlin bindings (typed, panic-guarded by uniffi's own scaffolding; no hand-rolled JSON, as of 2026-07-12) |
| `src/main.rs` | 720-line interactive CLI harness; mostly demo commands |
| `desktop/` | Tauri 2 shell (excluded from workspace) + vanilla JS SPA (`desktop/ui/`) |
| `android/` | Compose app: workspace list, keygen, "Hey Comrade" voice assistant |
| `.github/workflows/` | CI (Rust-only) + manual APK release |

**Surprising things.** The desktop JS contains a full mock backend for browser preview (`desktop/ui/main.js:621`); the Android app displays the raw `nsec` on screen; the "Couple Sandbox" pairing dialog on desktop validates a token client-side but never sends it to the backend; the Tauri bundle references icons that don't exist; there is no LICENSE file anywhere.

---

## 3. Audit Report

Severity: **C**ritical / **H**igh / **M**edium / **L**ow. Each finding is labeled **[fact]** (verifiable in code) or **[judgment]** (design opinion).

### 3.1 Security & Cryptography

| # | Sev | Finding |
|---|---|---|
| S1 | ~~H~~ | **[fact, resolved 2026-07-12]** DMs used deprecated NIP-04. `send_dm_reply` now builds NIP-17 gift-wrapped (NIP-59) NIP-44 messages via `EventBuilder::private_msg` — content, real sender, and exact timestamp are all hidden from relays; only the recipient `p` tag and a randomized (±2 day) timestamp are public. Legacy NIP-04 decrypt is kept, read-only, so a peer's older DMs still open. See M1-1 and the decision log. |
| S2 | **H** | **[fact] `change_pin` is not crash-safe.** `crates/comrade_storage/src/lib.rs:207-238` re-encrypts every value in place under the new key (lines 214-227) and only afterwards rewrites the salt + verification token (230-233). A crash mid-loop leaves a store whose meta says "old key" but whose values are a mix of old- and new-key ciphertexts — the mixed values are permanently undecryptable under either PIN. No test simulates this. (Latent today — `change_pin` has no non-test callers — but it is the published re-key API.) |
| S3 | **M** | **[fact] Weak-PIN reality vs. "encrypted at rest" promise.** The unlock secret is called a "PIN" throughout (`EncryptedStore::open(path, pin)`, CLI `unlock <PIN>`); nothing enforces minimum entropy (desktop `main.js:207` only checks non-empty). Argon2 uses library defaults (`lib.rs:255`, argon2 0.5 ⇒ m=19 MiB, t=2, p=1 — OWASP minimum). A 4-6 digit PIN over a 10⁴–10⁶ keyspace is offline-brute-forceable in seconds regardless of Argon2. The at-rest encryption is only as strong as the passphrase policy that doesn't exist. |
| S4 | **M** | **[fact] Social graph stored in plaintext keys.** `comrade_storage` encrypts values only; tree keys are plaintext by design (`lib.rs:23-25`). Contacts are keyed by npub (`repository.rs:143`), so a device-level attacker reads the full contact list without the PIN. The header note says "callers that need key confidentiality should hash keys" — no caller does. |
| S5 | **M** | **[fact] Secret-key exposure surfaces.** Android `KeygenSection` renders the full `nsec` on screen with no masking and no `FLAG_SECURE` (`MainActivity.kt:284-290`); the CLI prints the first 20 chars of the nsec (`src/main.rs:592-594`) and 16 bytes of a DH shared secret (`src/main.rs:617-621`); the nsec crosses the JNI boundary inside a hand-rolled JSON Java `String` (`comrade_jni/src/lib.rs:113-117`), which is immutable and unzeroizable. `KeyProfile.nsec` / `StoredIdentity.nsec` are plain `String`s — the README's zeroization claim (`README.md:171`) is true only for the storage key. |
| S6 | **M** | **[fact] Tauri CSP disabled.** `desktop/src-tauri/tauri.conf.json:20` sets `"csp": null` in a webview that renders content from public relays. Mitigation: `desktop/ui/main.js` is disciplined — all dynamic text goes through `textContent` (`el()` helper, `main.js:35-51`), and `innerHTML` is only used for clearing. CSP is the missing second layer, cheap to add. |
| S7 | **M** | **[fact] `allowBackup="true"`** (`AndroidManifest.xml:17`). ADB/cloud backup can exfiltrate the app's data dir. Values are encrypted, but combined with S3 (weak PIN) a backup is an offline brute-force target; privacy-focused apps conventionally set `allowBackup=false`. |
| S8 | **M** | **[judgment] Sakha has no forward secrecy.** Pairing derives one static symmetric key from static-static ECDH (`sakha.rs:136-144`, `crypto.rs:78-104`) and publishes ciphertext to public relays as Kind-30078. Compromise of either partner's nsec at any future time decrypts the couple's entire ledger history retained by relays. Acceptable for a prototype only if documented as a known limitation; there is no threat-model doc (see N2). |
| S9 | L | **[fact] Sakha sync handler trusts the relay's author filter.** `sakha.rs:217-260` never checks `event.pubkey == partner_pk`; authenticity rests entirely on AES-GCM decryption succeeding. AEAD makes forgery infeasible, but replayed/reflected updates are accepted (mostly harmless — Yrs updates are idempotent). Cheap defense-in-depth check missing. |
| S10 | L/M | **[fact] Vosk model fetched without integrity pinning.** `scripts/fetch-vosk-model.sh:12,29` downloads a zip over HTTPS with no checksum; the bytes are baked into the APK. One `sha256sum -c` line fixes it. *Resolved 2026-07-16: sha256 pinned in the script (cross-verified against Alpine aports' sha512 pin of the same artifact); the new in-app on-demand download (`VoiceModelDownloader`) verifies the same pin before install.* |
| S11 | M | **[judgment] Saathi mesh is plaintext broadcast with spoofable sender.** Mesh messages are unencrypted application-layer JSON visible to every LAN peer, and `MeshMessage.sender` is a free-text self-declared label (`saathi.rs:41-59,289-291`) not bound to the libp2p identity. Fine for a demo; incompatible with the README's privacy framing if ever wired up (currently it is not — see A1). |
| J-1 | M | **[fact, new 2026-08-16] A journal recording — voice or video — is not sealed by the vault, while everything around it is.** The entry's title, words and mood are AES-GCM values in the encrypted store like any other journal entry (`comrade_storage/src/repository.rs:255-320`, proven by `journal_recording_title_never_plaintext_at_rest`). The recording is a plain `.mp4`/`.m4a` in app-private internal storage — `filesDir/journal-videos` and `filesDir/journal-audio`, `android/.../journal/JournalRecordingStore.kt` — protected by the Android sandbox and whatever full-disk encryption the device has, and by nothing this app does. That is the strongest available answer to "keep it out of the gallery" (`MediaStore` cannot index app-private internal storage at all, which is why no `.nomedia` is written), and it is a *weaker* answer than the rest of the journal gives: an unlocked, rooted or imaged device yields the recording without the passcode. **Deliberate, not overlooked** — `EncryptedStore` seals whole values through `serde_json` (`storage/lib.rs:155-167`), so sealing a 100 MB recording means holding it, and its ciphertext, in memory at once, and playback would then need it decrypted to a temp file anyway. **Exit condition:** a chunked sealed-file primitive in `comrade_storage` (framed AES-GCM, nonce and chunk index bound as AAD) that `MediaPlayer` can be fed through a streaming source, at which point the recordings migrate and this closes. Until then no copy anywhere may call a recording sealed or encrypted — `journal_video_where` in `strings.xml` states both halves, and the doc comments on `JournalRecording`/`JournalRecordingDto`/`JournalRecordingStore` cite this ID. |
| S12 | M | **[judgment] Untrusted DMs auto-compile to payment URIs.** Any inbound DM containing `/pay …` becomes a rendered UPI chip with a ready-made `upi://pay` URI (`vault.rs:34-50`, `desktop/ui/main.js:421-429`). Amount/VPA are shown, so the user does see what they'd approve, but money-adjacent parsing of attacker-controlled text deserves explicit confirm-flow design, and amounts are `f64` (see Q3). |

**Healthy:** AES-GCM usage is correct throughout (fresh random 96-bit nonce per seal, prepended; auth failure surfaces as typed errors) — `crypto.rs:123-149`, `storage/lib.rs:264-290`, `sakha.rs:39-65`. The ECDH x-only lift + SHA-256(x) + HKDF-labels construction (`crypto.rs:78-118`) composes standard primitives sensibly and is symmetric-verified by test. Storage's PIN verification fails closed (`StorageError::InvalidPin`), proven by tests including on-disk plaintext scans (`storage/lib.rs:346-370`, `repository.rs:377-408`).

### 3.2 Architecture & Design

| # | Sev | Finding |
|---|---|---|
| A1 | **H** | **[fact, adversarially confirmed] Off-Grid mode is a false privacy assurance — engines are never disconnected.** `toggle_workspace` (`runtime.rs:326-328`) only flips the pure state enum; a repo-wide grep shows `SabhaEngine::disconnect` / `VaultEngine::disconnect` / `SakhaEngine::disconnect` have **zero call sites**, and `SaathiEngine` is never constructed anywhere. Yet the desktop toasts "Off-Grid / Travel mode — public relays paused, Saathi mesh active" (`desktop/ui/main.js:470-474`) while the relay websockets stay live, `broadcast_chitthi` has no workspace guard, and the compose UI stays active. `GossipEngine` and `MediaEngine` are likewise unwired (call sites only in CLI demos/tests); the CLI even prints "Saathi mesh would spin up here (run 'saathi' binary in production)" (`src/main.rs:544`) — no such binary exists. The README's engine table (`README.md:8-17`) presents all of these as product features. |
| A2 | **H** | **[fact, adversarially confirmed] The Android app cannot reach any networked feature.** No Kotlin code ever calls `unlockVault`/`pollEvent` (`ComradeCore.unlockVaultTyped` has no caller; `MainActivity.kt` has no passphrase UI), so engines are never constructed on Android. Voice "post …" and "read my timeline" route to `broadcast_chitthi`/`fetch_sabha_timeline`, which always return `VaultLocked` (`runtime.rs:284,269`). The advertised assistant (`README.md:140-143`) can only ever answer "I couldn't post that. vault is locked…" — on the one platform the release workflow ships. |
| A3 | ~~H~~ | **[fact, resolved by 2026-08-03; ledger updated 2026-08-14]** Couple pairing was foreclosed by the ownership design: `pair_with` took `&mut self` behind an `Arc`, no bridge exposed it, and the desktop Partner Portal validated the token client-side and threw it away. All three legs are fixed on main. `SakhaEngine::pair_with` now takes `&self` behind `SyncRwLock` interior mutability (`crates/comrade_core/src/sakha.rs:163`, fields at `:124-125`), `ComradeRuntime::pair_sakha` exposes it (`crates/comrade_ui/src/runtime.rs:5170`, tests at `:10151`, `:10160`), and the desktop invokes the real ECDH+HKDF handshake (`desktop/src-tauri/src/commands.rs:123`, `desktop/ui/main.js:4108`). The DM composer stub is gone too — Send reaches `send_dm_reply` (`main.js:1742`). **Residual, tracked in the product table below ("Stay connected to a loved one" row):** pairing has no Android surface, and `comrade_jni` exports none of it (`git grep pair_sakha crates/comrade_jni/` is empty), so only desktop users can pair. |
| A4 | M | **[judgment] The layering itself is good — the gap is lifecycle ownership.** `comrade_state` (pure) → `comrade_core` (engines) → `comrade_ui` (view-model) → thin bridges is the right shape, and the three bridge layers are appropriately thin duplicates. What's missing is one owner that maps workspace transitions to engine start/stop (the thing A1/A3 need) and one owner for relay configuration — today Sabha hardcodes `DEFAULT_RELAYS`, Vault receives them as a parameter, Sakha gets an empty list, and the NIP-65 `RelayRouter` is an unintegrated island. Placing both in `ComradeRuntime` is the natural evolution. |
| A5 | M | **[fact] Voice "new identity" is a disconnected code path.** The JNI `generateKeypair` calls `KeyProfile::generate()` standalone (`comrade_jni/src/lib.rs:109-118`) and never touches the shared `ComradeRuntime`, so the voice assistant's "Created a new identity" (`CommandDispatcher.kt:60-63` via `ComradeCoreBackend.kt:28-29`) succeeds without changing the identity any engine signs with. Two identity paths, one of them cosmetic. |
| A6 | L | **[fact] JNI event delivery is poll-based and lossy-by-design.** A single process-global broadcast receiver is created lazily at first `pollEvent` (`comrade_jni/src/lib.rs:69-72`); events emitted before that are silently dropped, and lag drops surface as `{"lagged":n}`. Currently moot (no Kotlin caller — A2), but the contract should be documented or replaced with a callback when Android wiring lands. |
| A7 | L | **[fact] `comrade_core` declares an unused dependency on `comrade_state`** (`crates/comrade_core/Cargo.toml:7`; zero use sites in `comrade_core/src/`) — muddies the otherwise-clean layering story. |
| A8 | L | **[fact, found 2026-08-04 while building the desktop handoff] A handoff has no way to say "this device cannot take that file".** `RefusalReason` (`share/transport.rs:129-136`) is entirely about relays — `RelayForbidden`, `TooLargeForRelay { limit }`, `PathUnknown` — and `HandoffSignal::Decline` means, by its own doc, that *a person* said no. So a receiver that refuses for a reason of its own (the desktop's 256 MiB memory ceiling in `handoff_transfer.mjs`, or a transfer already running in that window) has nothing honest to send: `Decline` would attribute a device limit to the person, and every `Refuse` reason would name a relay that is not involved. The desktop's answer is to send **nothing** and put the reason on screen — the offer card renders with no Accept button and the person may decline in their own name — so the sender waits, which is true, rather than being told something false. Exit condition: either a `RefusalReason::NotAcceptable { limit }` (a wire change, plus the mirrored matches in Kotlin and Dart), or a written decision that leaving the sender waiting is the intended behaviour. |

**Healthy:** crate dependency direction is clean (storage has no core/nostr deps to avoid cycles — `repository.rs:6-7`; state is I/O-free by doc and by fact); DTO/event contracts are serde-typed and round-trip tested (`runtime.rs:489-510`); the JNI boundary is panic-guarded — as of the uniffi-rs migration (2026-07-12), this is uniffi's own generated `catch_unwind` scaffolding rather than the hand-rolled `guard_json` this line used to cite, so no unwinding crosses `extern "C"` either way; the Android voice layer is cleanly abstracted behind the `ComradeBackend` interface so dispatcher logic is JVM-unit-testable without the native library (`CommandDispatcher.kt:9-28`).

> **2026-07-12 note:** `comrade_jni/src/lib.rs` was rewritten wholesale for the
> uniffi-rs migration (hand-written `extern "C"` exports + `guard_json` + JSON
> marshaling → generated scaffolding + typed `Comrade` object). Several
> findings above (S5, A5, A6, Q8, O4, P2, M3-4, M3-8) cite specific old line
> numbers or mechanisms (`guard_json`, hand-rolled JSON, `format!`) in that
> file; their line citations are now stale and their substance has not been
> re-verified against the new code. Re-auditing them is a separate follow-up,
> not part of the FFI migration itself.

### 3.3 Performance & Concurrency

| # | Sev | Finding |
|---|---|---|
| P1 | **H** | **[fact] Sabha subscribes to the global Kind-1 firehose.** `sabha.rs:270-272` filters only by kind + since (no authors, no limit); `runtime.rs:243` requests the last **3600 seconds of every public text note on damus/nostr.band/nos.lol**. Every event is pushed onto the bus and prepended to the desktop DOM with no cap (`main.js:298-306`, `state.chitthis` grows forever). On real relays this floods the UI within seconds and grows memory unboundedly. This is simultaneously a perf, UX, and privacy problem (you render arbitrary global content). |
| P2 | M | **[fact] Blocking work on async threads and locks held across I/O.** *Substantially fixed 2026-07-15:* `unlock_vault` still runs Argon2id + the store open on Tokio's blocking pool via `spawn_blocking` (`runtime.rs`), so the KDF doesn't stall a reactor thread. Every command with a real relay round-trip — `send_dm`, `send_dm_reply`, `broadcast_chitthi`, `send_call_signal`, `hangup_call`, `upload_and_send_media`, `download_and_decrypt_media`, `search_profiles`, `sync_ledger`, `sakha_add_entry` — now takes a cheap `RuntimeHandles` snapshot under a briefly-held guard and awaits the network guard-free, in both `desktop/src-tauri/commands.rs` and `comrade_jni`; a regression test proves an unreachable-relay `send_dm` no longer stalls a concurrent `toggle_workspace` (see the 2026-07-15 idempotency-pass decision log entry). Android's tap-to-talk no longer dispatches from the recognizer's main-looper callback either. Still open: the `RwLock` write guard is held across `unlock_vault`'s own await (unlock is inherently exclusive, so this may be acceptable as-is — not re-examined here); `pair_sakha` intentionally keeps `&mut self` (`SakhaEngine::pair_with` has no internal network await to move out from under it, so its write guard is already bounded to local CPU work). |
| P3 | M | **[fact] Unbounded in-memory growth in engines.** Vault inbox `Vec<VaultMessage>` grows forever and `inbox_snapshot` clones it all (`vault.rs:82,215,225-227`); Saathi `received: Vec` unbounded (`saathi.rs:77,257`) while its outbox is capped at 256. |
| P4 | L | **[fact] Sakha sync payload grows without bound.** Every `publish_sync` encodes the full doc state from `StateVector::default()` (`sakha.rs:175-179`), so event size grows with ledger history for the life of the pairing. |
| P5 | L | **[judgment] Triple websocket pools.** Sabha, Vault, and Sakha each construct their own `nostr_sdk::Client` (`runtime.rs:201-215`), tripling connections to the same relays; nostr-sdk supports a shared client/pool. |
| P6 | L | **[fact] Timeline reads decrypt the whole cache.** `chitthi_cache()` iterates, decrypts, and sorts every cached row per fetch with no pagination or eviction (`repository.rs:174-178`); fine at prototype scale, a cliff once incoming posts are persisted (Q2). |
| P7 | L | **[fact, found 2026-08-03 while building §8.2] One desktop object URL per outgoing attachment is never revoked.** `desktop/ui/main.js:2702` mints `URL.createObjectURL(file)` for the optimistic local render of an attachment the user just sent, and stores it on the message. It is created *outside* the bounded LRU that `media_cache.mjs` exists to enforce (`main.js:303`), and `revokeAllMediaUrls` (`main.js:295`, called only from `handleUnlock`) does not know about it — so every attachment sent in a session pins its `File` in the webview heap for the life of the process. Smaller than the leak the media cache was written to fix (this is the user's *own* file, already on their disk, not decrypted plaintext), which is why it is Low and was left rather than fixed silently inside an unrelated change. Fix: put it through the same cache, or revoke it when the optimistic entry is replaced by the round-tripped one. |
| P8 | ~~M~~ | **[fact, found 2026-08-08 reviewing polius/FileSync; resolved 2026-08-08] The Android transfer receiver reopened the destination file once per 16 KiB chunk.** `onChunk` did `RandomAccessFile(path, "rw").use { setLength(totalBytes); seek(offset); write(payload) }` per chunk, so a 700 MB film was ~44,800 open/`ftruncate`/`lseek`/`close` cycles on the one path that is large by definition. (The original entry said each close forced a flush — **that was wrong**: `RandomAccessFile` has no user-space buffer and `close()` performs no flush and no `fsync`. The saved syscalls were reason enough.) Fixed: one handle opened and sized in `armReceive` (`android/app/src/main/java/mullu/comrade/transfer/FileTransfer.kt:326`), used at `:822`, closed in `finishReceive` once the hash verifies (`:888`) and in `tearDown` (`:528`). The write-then-`accept` ordering a live decoder depends on is unchanged. **The sender half was deliberately not taken**: holding its source meant a `content://` descriptor on another app's provider living until a card was dismissed, because the sender never learns the receiver got the last chunk, so `pump` still opens per invocation with a `finally` close (`:615`). P8 is a receiver finding and is implemented there. |

**Healthy:** the event bus is a bounded `broadcast::channel(256)` where slow consumers lag rather than block producers (`runtime.rs:41`); heavy relay/decrypt work runs in spawned Tokio tasks off the UI thread (`runtime.rs:233-263`); JNI owns one process-global multi-thread runtime rather than per-call runtimes (`comrade_jni/src/lib.rs:47-55`).

### 3.4 Code Quality & Correctness

| # | Sev | Finding |
|---|---|---|
| Q1 | **H** | **[fact] Saathi's offline cache drops messages on drain failure.** On mDNS discovery the outbox is drained by `pop_front` → publish; a publish error only logs — the message is not re-queued (`saathi.rs:216-231`). Worse, drain fires immediately on discovery, before the gossipsub mesh has formed (mesh formation needs a heartbeat exchange), so `InsufficientPeers` failures are the *expected* case — the advertised store-and-forward behavior (`README.md:170`) likely loses most cached messages in practice. Zero tests exist for this module. Currently unreachable from any frontend (A1), which contains the blast radius but also means it's never been exercised. |
| Q2 | M | **[fact] The offline-first storage layer is built but barely used.** Incoming chitthis and DMs are never persisted — the event-loop callbacks only forward to the broadcast bus (`runtime.rs:238-245, 253-257`); only *outgoing* chitthis are cached (`runtime.rs:301-315`). The contacts, vault-cache, and ledger-persistence repository APIs (`repository.rs:142-235`) have zero production callers. Compounding it, the Vault subscription filters `.since(Timestamp::now())` (`vault.rs:157`) with no history backfill, so DMs received while the app is closed are silently lost. The "offline-first" story the storage crate was built for effectively exists only for one's own posts. |
| Q3 | M | **[fact] Money as `f64`.** `UpiPaymentIntent.amount_inr` (`vault.rs:25`) and `LedgerEntry.amount_inr` (`sakha.rs:72`) are floats, formatted with `{:.2}` into payment URIs. Classic representation risk for a feature that constructs real payment intents; should be integer paise. |
| Q4 | M | **[fact] Live feed discards threading it already knows how to parse.** `ChitthiDto::from_event` hardcodes `reply_to: None` (`runtime.rs:57-69`) even though `sabha.rs:122-134` implements NIP-10 parent resolution — so live chitthis lose reply structure, and the `build_chitthi_thread` tree builder is reachable only from the CLI demo. |
| Q5 | M | **[fact] Background event loops fail silently.** If a feed/inbox subscription errors, the spawned task `warn!`s and exits (`runtime.rs:243-246, 258-261`); no status/error event reaches any frontend, so the app looks unlocked and healthy while live updates are permanently dead for the session. |
| Q6 | L | **[fact] Misleading error taxonomy in Vault.** Relay add, subscription, and send failures are all mapped to `VaultError::EncryptionFailed` (`vault.rs:92,119,130,162,222`) — a connection refusal reports as "encryption failed" in every frontend. `DecryptionFailed`/`InvalidRecipient` variants exist but are never constructed (`error.rs:79-83`). Similarly, `sakha.rs:64` labels a decrypt failure `EncryptionError`. |
| Q7 | L | **[fact] AES-256-GCM seal/open is implemented three times** — `crypto.rs:123-149`, `storage/lib.rs:264-290`, `sakha.rs:39-65` — identical `[nonce | ct+tag]` envelopes with per-module error types. One shared helper (crypto.rs's) would do. |
| Q8 | L | **[fact] Hand-rolled JSON at the JNI boundary.** `generateKeypair` formats JSON with `format!` (`comrade_jni/src/lib.rs:113-117`) — an error message containing `"` would produce invalid JSON. Fine for the current messages; trivially replaced with the `json!` macro already used elsewhere in the same file. |
| Q9 | L | **[fact] NIP-10 tree builder recurses to reply-chain depth** over relay-supplied events (`sabha.rs:169-186`; `ChitthiThread::len` likewise, `sabha.rs:60-65`) — a maliciously deep chain overflows the stack. Only demo-reachable today; matters when Q4 is fixed. Also: the CLI claims "Persisted N Chitthi(s)" then discards the flush result (`src/main.rs:158`, `let _ = store.flush()`). |
| Q10 | L | **[fact] README's "zero unwrap in network/parsing paths" is nearly true.** Counterexamples are all benign-but-notable: infallible-by-construction `.expect` in `derive_symmetric_key` (`crypto.rs:116`) and listen-addr parse (`saathi.rs:152`), plus `expect("keygen")` on the CLI bootstrap path (`src/main.rs:406,430,443,447`). Engine network/parse paths hold the claim. |

| Q11 | ~~C~~ | **[fact, resolved 2026-08-04] The Android event-drain loop died permanently on any one bad event.** `EventPump.drain` had no exception handler, and `ChatEventRouter.route` reaches WebRTC, SharedPreferences and `NotificationManager` — one throw completed the coroutine. `EventPump.acquire` then refused to restart it, because it returned early whenever the holder set had not changed, which is exactly a returning Activity's state. Delivery therefore stopped *silently and for the life of the process*: no notifications, no chat-list updates, no calls ringing. `EventBus` never drops critical events (COMMS-04), so DMs queued behind a consumer that was gone. Fixed with per-event isolation and a bounded backoff, plus `shouldStartLoop` separating "somebody needs a loop" from "a loop is alive". *Hardened 2026-08-14 (ported from the `bob-alice-message-notification-zeikr6` branch):* the pure logic — holder bookkeeping, restart rule, backoff, and a generic `drainLoop` — moved to `android/app/src/main/java/mullu/comrade/EventDrain.kt` so the outage scenario itself runs under JUnit; `EventPump.isRunning` now reports the loop's actual liveness instead of the holder set; `RelayConnectionService` re-checks liveness once a minute via `EventPump.ensureRunning` (the one stretch where no `acquire` will ever arrive); and every `Notifier` post goes through a `runCatching` wrapper, so a refused notification costs one notification rather than the rest of that event's routing. Regression tests in `EventPumpTest.kt` (13, including the poisoned-event, failing-poll, cancellation and backoff-reset scripts). |
| Q12 | ~~H~~ | **[fact, resolved 2026-08-04] A backgrounded app suppressed notifications for the chat it had left open.** `ChatEventRouter`'s open-conversation peer is set on navigation into a thread and cleared on navigation out; backgrounding is neither. Pressing Home with a chat open left that peer flagged "already on screen" indefinitely, so the person the user was mid-conversation with became the one person whose messages could not reach them. Visibility is now tracked separately (`RelayConnectionService.kt:225-244`, set from `MainActivity.onStart`/`onStop`) and both halves are required to suppress (`NotificationPolicy.kt:78-92`). Regression test: `NotificationPolicyTest.kt:37-64`. |
| Q13 | ~~H~~ | **[fact, resolved 2026-08-04] Quiet hours discarded messages rather than silencing them.** `NotificationPolicy` returned a `Boolean`, so the only expressible "quiet" was "no notification" — an overnight message never reached the shade at all and was discoverable only by opening the app, which is indistinguishable from a delivery failure. `NotifyDecision` adds the missing middle: messages, requests and update notices post `Silent` on a new `IMPORTANCE_LOW` channel (`Notifier.kt:31-45`); presence and nudges still `Suppress`, because both expire in minutes and a quiet one found at 08:00 would assert something about last night. `docs/ATTENTION.md` §phase-0 updated to match. |
| Q15 | ~~M~~ | **[fact, resolved 2026-08-05] The library resolver could never find anything, because the app never asked to read the library.** `LibraryResolver.query` reads `MediaStore.Audio.Media.EXTERNAL_CONTENT_URI` and the manifest declared no media-read permission, so the cursor came back empty on every supported release — `/play`'s automatic match and `TogetherManager.onInvited`'s auto-open of an incoming invitation had both been dead since they were written, and the silence was indistinguishable from "you don't have this track". **The owner's answer was to ask.** `AndroidManifest.xml` now declares `READ_MEDIA_AUDIO` and `READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` capped at API 32, and `together/MediaLibraryAccess.kt` owns *when* the question is put: at the moment somebody names a song, never at startup, and **at most once** — Android stops showing the dialog after a refusal, so a second request would be a button that silently does nothing. Two guards keep the prompt honest and are the part worth reading. `/play` asks core whether a local copy would change the route at all (`play_route(plan, false) != play_route(plan, true)`) and stays silent when it would not, so a Spotify link never raises a dialog to reach the same "open it there" sentence; the decision stays in Rust rather than being a second copy of the route table in Kotlin. And an invitation gets its own "Look in my library" button (`TogetherScreen.kt`, `TogetherManager.lookAgain`), because that is the moment the permission is obviously worth something. Refusal costs the automatic match and nothing else: the file picker needs no permission (SAF grants per file), and `ChatCommands.LIBRARY_UNSEEN` still says Comrade was not allowed to look rather than that the track is absent. `MediaLibraryAccessTest.kt` pins the two decisions, including that Tiramisu must not be asked for `READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` — a request that can never be granted — by asserting the literal permission strings rather than the constants, which would compare a compile-time constant to itself. |
| Q16 | ~~L~~ | **[fact, resolved 2026-08-05] A shared Tara line was a labelled ordinary message, and no frontend drew it as hers.** Her answer travelled as a DM prefixed `"Tara: "` and rendered as an outgoing message from you that happened to begin with her name — legible, but not the third-participant bubble the feature was asked for. The exit condition was a container with `flutter_rust_bridge_codegen`, and this session had one: `comrade_ui::MessageDto` now carries `author: MessageAuthor` (`Human` | `Tara`), `frb_generated.rs`/`.dart` were **regenerated** rather than hand-edited, and all three frontends draw her bubble from the field. Two decisions differ from the exit condition as it was written, both deliberately. **The prefix stays on the wire and on disk** instead of going away: a NIP-17 DM opened in some other Nostr client has no author field to read, and "Tara: …" is a truer fallback there than her words in the sender's mouth — `split_author` is the single place that turns the wire form into `(author, content)`, called from both `MessageDto` construction sites so a message cannot read one way when it is sent and another after a reload (`her_line_is_still_marked_as_hers_after_a_reload`). And **her bubble is left-aligned on both devices, with no delivery ticks**: her answer is carried by whichever phone asked, so aligning by `outgoing` would put one line on opposite sides of the two screens, and a tick on a third party's line reads as a claim about her. What the field does **not** buy is authentication — see Q17. |
| Q14 | ~~M~~ | **[fact, resolved 2026-08-04] A second message from one peer erased the first.** One notification id per peer is right, but with only `setContentText` behind it each message overwrote the last, so three messages left a shade entry claiming one. Now `MessagingStyle` over a bounded per-peer line buffer (`MessageShade.kt`), pinned by `MessageShadeTest.kt`. The group summary also posted with default alerting while being re-posted on every message, so one arriving message sounded twice; it is silent now. |
| Q17 | L | **[fact, accepted 2026-08-05 with Tara's own bubble] A Tara bubble is attribution, not attestation.** Nothing signs `comrade_core::tara::TARA_CHAT_PREFIX`. Anybody can type "Tara: " in front of a sentence, so `MessageAuthor::Tara` means *the sending Comrade says this came from her* — the same standing a quoted reply has, and the same standing `@Meta AI` has in WhatsApp. Q16 was the smaller version of this and it got sharper, not softer, when the field landed: before, a forged line was plain text; now it is a named, differently coloured bubble, so a peer who wants to put words in the companion's mouth has a better-looking way to do it. Deliberately accepted rather than papered over — `the_marker_is_a_label_and_the_test_says_so` asserts that a hand-typed line parses, the desktop mirror's test asserts the same, and no code may gate anything on the field. **Exit condition:** a signed attribution, which needs a wire-level author claim the sender's key covers — realistically a NIP-17 rumor tag plus verification on receipt — and is not worth building until something depends on the answer. Until then, style with it and trust nothing. |
| Q18 | ~~H~~ | **[fact, found 2026-08-08 reviewing polius/FileSync; resolved 2026-08-08] A re-delivered `ShareSignal` rebuilt the transfer session on Android, ending a live transfer and leaking its `PeerConnection`.** Comrade replays gift-wraps by design — `inbox_since` widens the subscription floor back to the persisted watermark on every reconnect (`crates/comrade_core/src/vault.rs`) — and `TogetherSignal::Share` was the one arm of `handle_together_envelope` with no replay guard, while `Start`, `Join` and `State` all had one. Android's `armSend`/`armReceive` then overwrote `session` with no teardown. Fixed at both layers, which have different reach: core keys on the wrapper event id (`crates/comrade_ui/src/runtime.rs:8118`), covering every frontend and the negotiation signals, and bounded by the age gate above it so it can only suppress a retry inside the same minute one was possible; Android decides from the offer's identity (`ShareDecisions.decideArm`, `android/app/src/main/java/mullu/comrade/transfer/ShareDecisions.kt:165`) and on a redelivery returns **before** touching the filesystem, since the staged file is the live transfer's partial download. Regression tests: `runtime.rs:14123` (confirmed failing with the guard neutralised) and `ShareDecisionsTest.kt`. **Accepted trade-off:** the core guard is coarser than Android's — it drops by event id even when the frontend did nothing with the first copy, so a redelivered `Offer` no longer retries a staging failure (disk full). That path is not silent, so it fails the Q19 bar rather than this one. The scenario originally feared — an `Ask` lost because the player had not opened — was checked and is **not reachable**: `openedPath` is assigned as the first statement of `TogetherManager.openPlayer`, and the call creating a session runs in that method's `onReady`, strictly later. |
| Q19 | ~~M~~ | **[fact, found 2026-08-08 reviewing polius/FileSync; resolved 2026-08-08] A transfer that stalled on a live connection froze both progress bars with no error on either device.** The only thing that ended a transfer was `PeerConnectionState.FAILED`; the sender's pump had five bare `return`s on a healthy connection, and the receiver only re-asked from inside `onChunk`. A revoked `content://` grant or removable storage vanishing produced two frozen bars and no message. Fixed: the abandon paths route through `abandonSend` (`android/app/src/main/java/mullu/comrade/transfer/FileTransfer.kt:696`), and a bounded receiver watchdog (`:742`) notices the silence, re-asks once and gives up. The rule is pure and tested — `ShareDecisions.stallAction` (`ShareDecisions.kt:294`), with the four tests confirmed failing against the rule that preceded it. Two things the first attempt got wrong and a review caught, both now covered by tests: the watchdog armed on data-channel open with a single budget, so it ended healthy transfers waiting on relay consent and **dismissed the consent dialog mid-decision**; holding and waiting are now different answers, and setting up gets its own wider window (`SETUP_GRACE_MS`, `:68`) which also closes the never-connected case. **What it still cannot do:** nothing on the wire says a person at the far end is being asked, so `SETUP_GRACE_MS` doubles as a bound on waiting for a stranger to tap a button. See Q20 for the wire reason that is still missing. **Unverified:** nothing in this repo executes `armSend`, `armReceive`, `claim`, `tearDown`, `endIfCurrent`, `startStallWatch` or `pump` — those need Robolectric or a device; the decision layer is tested, the wiring is not. |
| Q20 | M | **[fact, found 2026-08-08 while fixing Q19] A transfer that fails for a reason other than relay policy cannot say so on the wire.** `FileTransfer.abandonSend` sets a local status and tears down, but sends nothing: `Wiring.sendRefuse` takes `RefusalReason`, whose every variant (`RelayForbidden`, `TooLargeForRelay`, `PathUnknown`) is a fact about relay policy, and none means "I could not read the file". So the far side learns only from the channel closing, and what turns that into a sentence over there is its own stall watchdog — after a wait, and with a message that names the wrong cause. The same gap runs the other way: nothing says *a person here is being asked to consent*, which is why Q19's `SETUP_GRACE_MS` doubles as a bound on human reaction time. Fix: variants on `comrade_core::share::transport::RefusalReason` for a source that cannot be read and for consent pending. **Cost to be honest about:** it is a `uniffi::Enum`, so it lands in the Kotlin and Dart exhaustive matches and the desktop `describeVerdict`/`describeHandoffVerdict` — a cross-layer change, which is why it was filed rather than smuggled into a transfer fix. |
| Q21 | L | **[fact, found 2026-08-08 while fixing Q18] `FileTransfer.onTransport` has no redelivery guard of its own.** `decideArm` is consulted from `armSend`/`armReceive` only, so a re-applied `TransferSignal.Offer` re-runs `setRemoteDescription` on a `PeerConnection` already in `stable`, creates a second answer, and that answer fails `setRemoteDescription` on the far side. `fail()` only logs and the data channel survives, so this is renegotiation churn and log noise rather than a killed transfer — which is why it is Low. Core's event-id guard (Q18) covers it on the relay path; the direct peer channel and the attachment-handoff envelope are not covered, and neither is a duplicate that core's 60-second age gate lets through. |
| Q22 | M | **[fact, found 2026-08-08 rebasing the transfer work onto the streaming work] After a *successful* file handover, a later stream offer is applied to the file transfer's `PeerConnection`.** `docs/TOGETHER.md` §15 routes a transport signal by whether the file engine is armed, and `finishReceive` deliberately never tears the session down (a player may still be reading the partial file), so `engine.active` stays true for the rest of the session. A stream `Transport.Offer` then reaches `FileTransfer.onTransport`, which finds `s.pc` non-null and calls `setRemoteDescription` with the stream's SDP on the file's connection. Neither half is new — "don't end on success" long predates the routing rule — and the two only met when both landed. Exposure is low in practice (after a successful handover both sides have the file, so there is little reason to stream) but it is a real hole. The mirror case *was* fixed: a file offer arriving after this side gave up is dropped rather than starting an unexpected stream (`ShareDecisions.transportPlan`). This one needs a view on how the two engines are meant to coexist, which is a design decision, not a patch. |
| Q23 | L | **[fact, found 2026-08-08 adding the Q18 guard] `together_starts_seen` is not cleared by `panic_wipe`.** The share seen-set added for Q18 is cleared there, beside `call_signal_dedup`, because it holds peer event ids and the method promises nothing survives. The invite set sits next to it and is not, which is pre-existing and smaller — it is keyed by session ids rather than event ids — but the inconsistency is now visible in one screen of code and should go one way or the other. |

| V1 | ~~M~~ | **[fact, found 2026-08-20 auditing the visual layer; resolved 2026-08-20] Four desktop tokens were used but never defined, and a `var()` fallback hid it.** `--accent-soft`, `--surface-2`, `--fg` and `--hover` appeared only as `var(--x, literal)` in `desktop/ui/styles.css`. A fallback renders, so nothing looked broken — the colours were merely *wrong*, and being literals they never responded to a skin swap, so the Travel/Sakha/Sakhi overlays silently failed to re-tint them. `--hover` resolved to `#232323`, a near-black grey, inside a navy app. All four are real definitions now (`desktop/ui/styles.css:101,134,135,136`) and the dead fallbacks are gone. The regression test is `desktop/ui/design_tokens.test.mjs:121` — confirmed failing against the previous file, naming all four. |
| V2 | ~~M~~ | **[fact, found 2026-08-20; resolved 2026-08-20] The desktop shell had no light mode at all.** `styles.css` contained no `prefers-color-scheme` block anywhere, so every value in it was tuned against near-black and a system asking for a light UI got the dark one regardless. There is a light ramp now (`desktop/ui/styles.css:3941`). It does **not** re-derive the light accents: `app/lib/src/theme/comrade_theme.dart` had already established which hues survive as text on a pale surface, and reusing its values is what keeps this from repeating the 2.1:1 contrast failure documented there. The token contract is `docs/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md`. |
